# circuitousness-front-end — Session Notes

Newest entries on top. See universal rule 9 in `../../CLAUDE.md` for what belongs here.

## 2026-09-04 — Release v1.79: Google Ads click ids captured for OFFLINE conversion import

- **Driver: a Performance Max campaign was being set up and there is NO Google tag anywhere on the site** — grepped for gtag / googletagmanager / google-analytics / conversion snippets across the game and the umbrella homepage, zero hits. PMax is conversion-optimized by design, so it would have spent the whole budget unable to tell an eleven-solve player from a 400ms bounce.
- **Chose offline conversion import over putting a tag on the page, and the reason is not code.** `official-intelligence-web/privacy.html` says in bold that we use "no third-party advertising cookies, ad networks, or cross-site tracking". A conversion tag is all three, so shipping one means rewriting that paragraph — on an account only just reinstated after "Unacceptable Business Practices", with a landing page a reviewer may well read. A tag would also drag EEA Consent Mode v2 (i.e. a consent banner we don't have) along with it. Offline import needs none of that: solves are exported keyed by click id and uploaded.
- **`tracking.js` captures `gclid` / `wbraid` / `gbraid`** in the existing utm_* block and sends a typed PAIR (`clickId` + `clickIdType`) on the visit POST **and** the `/api/load` impression. ⚠ The braid variants are NOT optional extras — Google withholds the gclid on much iOS traffic and sends those instead, so ignoring them discards the mobile half of a buy, which for this game is most of it. They also occupy separate columns in Google's import CSV, which is why the type has to travel with the value.
- ⚠ **The strip is the load-bearing part.** Click ids are now removed from the address bar alongside utm_*, for the same reason but with money attached: a landing URL that keeps `?gclid=` gets bookmarked and shared, every later visit re-captures that id, and each would upload ANOTHER conversion for one long-dead click — a campaign that appears to convert forever, with nothing visible from inside the game. Same lesson as TANTЯO's `?rdt_cid=` referrers.
- ⚠ **If a gtag is ever added, it MUST run before the strip** — it reads the click id from the URL at load. A comment says so at the site.
- Back end: `click_id VARCHAR(200)` + `click_id_type VARCHAR(10)` on `page_visits` AND `page_loads` (indexed on both — the export selects BY click id), migrations in `_COLUMN_MIGRATIONS` so they run on Render boot. **Deploy the back end FIRST**: a new client posting `clickId` to the old API is harmlessly ignored, the reverse is not.
- **Two deliberate design calls, both about not storing half a click.** `_click_id_fields` is kept OUT of `_campaign_fields` (200 chars, and the pair must be atomic), and an id whose type is missing or unrecognized is dropped ENTIRELY rather than stored typeless — an id that can't be filed into the right upload column silently never matches, which looks exactly like an ad that didn't work. On the dedup path the pair is gated on `click_id` alone, NOT field-by-field like the campaign columns, so a type can never be back-filled from a DIFFERENT click than the one that supplied the id.
- Assertions folded into the two EXISTING suites rather than a new file (`campaign-capture-test.js` already had the vm harness): 27 client + 29 server, all green, plus first-run-guard / potd-timing / mosaic-solve-telemetry. Both new behaviours mutation-checked — dropping `CLICK_ID_PARAMS` from the strip fails 5, swapping in the field-by-field stamp fails the back-fill assertion. Not browser-verified per rule 0.
- **Open, and this is the half that makes it useful: nothing exports the data yet.** Needs a query joining click ids to solves with timestamps in Google's import CSV shape, and a decision on what counts as a conversion (first solve per visit is the suggestion). ⚠ Google rejects an offline conversion whose click is older than **90 days**, so the export has to actually be run periodically once ads serve — a click id sitting in the DB unexported past that window is worthless.
- Campaign setup notes for whoever picks this up: the ad's Final URL suffix should be `utm_source=google&utm_medium=cpc&utm_campaign=<name>&utm_content=<asset group>` (Final URL suffix, NOT Tracking template — a redirect hop between ad and landing page is the wrong risk on this account). Auto-tagging appends the click id on its own.

## 2026-09-04 — Release v1.78: ASPCA reference removed (ad-policy false-affiliation risk)

- **Driver: naming a national nonprofit and pointing users at its donation page reads to an automated ad classifier as "affiliated with another brand/organization when you're not"** — a literal instance of the prohibited pattern, and unlike ad copy it lived *inside the game*. Ranked the second-likeliest contributor to the suspension after the Tetris references. There was never a logo, an affiliation claim, or any money routed through us; the links went to the ASPCA's own public pages.
- **Both references gone, link included — not just the name.** (1) the share-popup `share.donate` string, rewritten in **all 15 languages** as plain text with no anchor at all; (2) Circuitousness-only: the credits-roll line at the `data-credit-link="aspca"` anchor, now unlinked prose. Generic replacement wording throughout: "consider donating to an animal welfare charity" — names nobody.
- ⚠ **The donate-click tracking is now DEAD CODE, deliberately left in place.** `share.js`'s capture-phase handler still matches `.share-donate a` structurally and `Tracking.recordDonateClick` still exists, but no such anchor is left, so it can never fire. Kept because the `donate_clicked` column and the admin "ASPCA Donations" tile hold real historical counts — deleting the tracker would strand them. Rip it out only if the tile goes too.
- The `share.donate` string is now plain text, so nothing depends on `data-i18n` vs `data-i18n-html` for it any more — but the attribute was left as-is rather than churned.

## 2026-09-02 — Release v1.77: a paused Surge run could come back on a DIFFERENT puzzle

- **Player-reported (Ryan, mid-puzzle-16 Surge run): paused, resumed, and the board was back to scratch — "and I'm not sure it was even the same puzzle".** It was not. A resume from a **boundary** checkpoint GENERATES a fresh board and hands it the same puzzle number, so "reset" and "different puzzle" are the same failure wearing two faces. The other face is the puzzle-ready checkpoint: your own board with every rotation undone.
- **Root cause is structural, not a single bug.** The run save was written at five moments only — puzzle-ready, solve, resume, Pause/Quit, pagehide — with **nothing in between**. Each depends on its moment arriving intact, and a skipped write is SILENT: nothing errors, you just quietly resume from the previous checkpoint. ⚠ Any future code that can skip one of those moments re-creates this, which is why the fix is a per-move checkpoint rather than a patch to the five.
- **Fix: every committed board action checkpoints too** (`marathon.js` `scheduleRunSave`, hooked from `game.js` `appendMove` next to PotD's identical call — PotD has had this since 2026-08-02 for the same reason). Throttled to one write per 1.5s and ALWAYS via a timer, never synchronously: `undo()` and `resetPuzzle()` append their move with the board mid-restore, so a same-tick snapshot is stale. Worst case a kill loses ~1.5s.
- ⚠ **The `puzzleLive` guard in `scheduleRunSave` is load-bearing, not an optimization.** With it false we cannot tell a live board from a build window, and `saveRunState` would write a BOUNDARY save — actively replacing a good mid-puzzle checkpoint with one that regenerates the puzzle. Skipping is the safe reading. A queued checkpoint that fires while a tooltip beat holds the board **re-arms** rather than being dropped (beats loop until dismissed).
- **Skip path 1 — the two exit paths disagreed.** `pagehide` stopped a tooltip demo beat before saving (its comment says "ORDER MATTERS"); **quitToMenu did not**. `saveRunState`'s Tutorial guard then skipped the write ENTIRELY — note what that guard does: it doesn't save the demo, it saves **nothing**. Both now route through `checkpointForExit`, so a third exit path can't inherit the gap.
- **Skip path 2 — `Marathon.onPuzzleReady()` fired for builds that never landed.** `game.js` called it unconditionally after `await newPuzzle(...)`, including when newPuzzle had bailed on its `newPuzzleSeq` supersession token. `puzzleLive` is exactly what `saveRunState` reads to tell mid-puzzle from boundary, so flipping it against a board still being built is precisely how a resume turns into a different puzzle. `newPuzzle` now answers **false** when superseded, **true** when its build landed.
- ⚠ **`isBuilding` staying true on a supersession bail is CORRECT — do not "fix" it.** Supersession means a newer `newPuzzle` call exists; it set the flag itself and clears it at its own tail. (Noted because it reads like a leak.)
- New suite `../tests/run-save-checkpoint.test.js` — real config.js + marathon.js in a vm sandbox with **fake timers and a fake clock**, clicking the genuine Continue card and `#hudQuitBtn`. Draining the timer queue by hand is what makes the debounced write observable without sleeping. It pins the boundary-save build-a-new-puzzle behaviour alongside the mid-puzzle restore, because that contrast IS the symptom. Mutations restore both old behaviours and confirm the file fails on each. ⚠ Section 6 is **source-shape only** (game.js isn't loadable headlessly), and the mutations key on source spellings — see the file header before renaming `checkpointForExit` or the `scheduleRunSave` body.
- Suites green: run-save-checkpoint (new, 30/30), zen-finish-gate, surge-rank-agreement, board-reset-notice, tooltip, onboarding-ladder, potd-config. Not browser-verified per rule 0 — wants a real pause/resume playthrough, ideally one paused mid-puzzle with a few rotations banked.
- **Open, not done:** the mid-puzzle resume branch never calls `Analytics.puzzleStarted`, so a restored board opens no engagement row, and it doesn't clear `resumedFromSave` either — so the NEXT puzzle is mislabeled `resumed`. Harmless to play, but it means **"resumed" rows in the admin only ever describe boundary resumes**; read them with that in mind. It is also the discriminator that would have identified this bug from the data alone.

## 2026-09-02 — Release v1.76: the HINT button did nothing on ~1 in 5 small boards

- **A real gameplay bug, found by chasing a "flaky" test.** `potd-replay-rotation-test.js` solves boards by calling `Maze.hint()` in a loop and had been failing ~1 run in 6. It was not the test: `hint()` was genuinely returning **null on solvable boards**, and game.js guards its whole handler on `if (pick)` — so the button produced no message, no sound, and no rotation. (One mercy: it also never reached `Marathon.onHintUsed()`, so nobody was charged a hint or docked leaderboard rank for it.)
- **Cause:** `hint()`'s `tileEligible` vetoed a twin group when any partner returned true from `tileShouldSkip` — which is true not only for a partner at its EXACT solution but for one merely PORT-EQUIVALENT to it (a straight at offset 2, a cross at any rotation). So an **ELBOW twinned with an offset-2 STRAIGHT** was permanently unhintable, and once that group was the last thing standing, all three passes ran out of candidates.
- **The group was always winnable** — that is the point. Twin path members sit at ONE shared offset from solution (manufactured by the twin builder, preserved by every group rotation), so the delta that fixes the elbow lands the straight exactly ON its solution. The veto was pure loss. Guard now tests exact rotation, so it fires only if that shared-offset invariant is broken — the safety net it was written to be.
- ⚠ **Quad mode was never affected** and needs no matching change: `quadAnyTileCorrect` short-circuits on `quadScramble[qr][qc] !== 0`, so it already only reports "correct" for a quad at its exact solution.
- Verified 0 stuck across 204 boards / 10 configurations (4x6→12x12, 1–4 paths, quad both ways; 4x6 was 12/60 before), `potd-replay-rotation-test.js` 0 failures in 20 runs, and twin/gates/multipath/mosaic/verify-opt all passing.
- New suite `../tests/twin-hint-reachability.test.js` pins the contract "pressing HINT repeatedly always finishes the board", plus "every hint is forward progress" — the old guard's LEGITIMATE half was preventing a hint from knocking a twin partner off solution, so that property is asserted directly rather than argued away. Its mutation section restores the old guard and confirms boards still strand (11–15%), sized at 150 trials so the anti-flake test cannot itself flake.
- **Worth internalising:** a suite that builds real randomized boards and fails intermittently is not automatically flaky. This one was reporting a live defect for as long as it had been dismissed as noise.

## 2026-09-02 — Release v1.75: Zen could never report "finished" on a resumed run

- **Found by reading a real player's visit rows, not by reading code.** Zen never reaches `gameOver()` — the only caller is the Surge timer hitting zero — so its one path to the funnel's finished milestone was the first-solve hook in `onSolve`, gated on `solvedCount === 1`. But `solvedCount` is a RUN total and `resumeRun` restores it from the save, so the 0→1 transition happens **once in the life of a run and never again**. Any player who banked a solve and then kept returning to that saved run reported "started, never finished" on every later visit no matter how much they solved.
- Fixed with a module-scoped `zenFinishReported`, deliberately **never** reset by `startGame` / `resetRunState` / `resumeRun` — the server flag it drives is per VISIT, so the unit has to be the page load. ⚠ Anything derived from run state reintroduces the bug; the marathon.js comment says so at the site.
- **Left `gameOver()`'s `recordFinish` alone on purpose.** It is Surge-only and fires once per run, and `finished_count` increments per call — folding it into the same flag would quietly halve that number for a visit holding two Surge runs, which the daily summary reads.
- **PotD was never affected** (`potd.js` fires finish unconditionally per solve, no run-state gate), and neither was the admin's `puzzles_solved`, which counts `end_cause == 'solved'` engagement rows on a wholly separate write path.
- Context for whoever reads the admin next: the player who exposed this (JC, phone/iOS, 8/30–9/2) turned out to be a **PotD** grinder, not Zen — `1 started / 1 recorded / 0 solved`, 113–127 moves, 3–4 min per sitting, resumed every time, and `used hint` dark on every row. Their dark third dot is CORRECT. Useful read for the future: `abandoned` is actively written by the hide/quit path and therefore proves the beacon landed, whereas `in_progress` is the "closing beacon never arrived" state — do not read them as the same thing.

## 2026-09-01 — Release v1.74: the game-over rank preview disagreed with the board

- **Player-reported, and real: the Surge popup promised #2 and the board then showed #3.** Cause was tiebreaker drift. When `hints_used` became the primary tiebreaker after `solved`, `app.py` (`_top_n` + the "better" count in `/scores/submit`) and the local `board.sort()` in `saveScore` were all updated — `computeRank()` was not, and kept comparing **solved + time only**. Any run that was FASTER but HINTIER than an equal-solved rival got promoted by the client and demoted by the server. The live case: 9 solved / 10 hints / 8:47 against a 9 / 8 / 12:25 rival.
- Fixed by giving `computeRank` the hints argument and the full three-level ordering (solved DESC → hints ASC → totalMs ASC). ⚠ **Legacy cached rows have no `hintsUsed`** — they must read as **0**, which is what the server defaults legacy rows to; reading them as `undefined` turns every comparison into NaN.
- **Two side effects worth knowing, because neither is obvious from the rank text:** the same function gates whether the name-entry row appears at all (`rank <= LEADERBOARD_TOP_N`), so a hint-heavy run at the top-20 boundary could be offered a save it did not qualify for (or denied one it did); and the `rank === 1` `audience_cheer_long` fired on the wrong runs.
- **PotD was never affected** — it displays the rank the server returns (`potd.js` reads `result.rank`), and only Surge predicts client-side.
- New suite `../tests/surge-rank-agreement.test.js` pins the three-way agreement (client preview / offline sort / server SQL), with the live board as the regression fixture. It also asserts the OLD hints-blind formula still answers #2 on that fixture, so it can never quietly stop discriminating. It brace-matches `computeRank` and the real comparator out of `marathon.js` (both are inside the IIFE, unreachable via the `Marathon` export) and keys on source spellings — see its header before renaming any of them.

## 2026-08-27 — itch handoff: two paths, and a row-writing race

- **Worked out from live admin rows how `platform-redirect.js` actually behaves in the wild** — none of this is legible from the code alone. **Stage 1** = top-level on itch's CDN (itch's fullscreen launch): `location.replace` fires in `<head>` and the page just leaves, nothing is ever painted. **Stage 2** = itch's in-page iframe (top-navigation sandbox-blocked): the "play the full version" overlay covers the page and the player taps through. Field tell in the expanded row: ~11s on page = Stage 2, ~1s = Stage 1.
- ⚠ **Whether a Stage 1 load writes a visit row at all is a RACE.** `location.replace()` QUEUES a navigation, it does not halt the document — so `recordVisit()` (DOMContentLoaded+500ms) writes a row only when the timer beats the commit. Observed both ways on the same day (8/26 2:43 PM wrote one, 8/27 4:52 AM did not). **So the absence of a ↻itch row next to an arrival proves nothing.**
- **The ARRIVAL's referrer is the reliable stage signal:** `html-classic.itch.zone/` = Stage 1 (ordinary cross-origin nav, origin-only under the default `strict-origin-when-cross-origin`); `(direct)` = Stage 2, because Safari drops the referrer when a tab is opened from a cross-site iframe. **Not** `rel="noopener"` (which never strips referrers) and **not** sandbox opacity — that one is ruled out by the data, since the framed load's `localStorage` works and its rows carry real session ids.
- **Session ids can never match across a handoff.** `circuitousness_session_id` lives in localStorage, which is per-origin, so the itch load and the site load are structurally unlinkable — differing ids across a handoff are EXPECTED and are not evidence of two browsers. To identify an arrival, use the admin's ✕ dry-run (then **Cancel**): it counts a browser's lifetime rows, and `First-run rows: 1` means brand-new browser.
- Related referrer trap: **Safari caps cross-site referrers to origin regardless of policy.** Desktop itch rows show the full `digeratist.itch.io/circuitousness?…` path; iOS ones show bare `digeratist.itch.io/`. Do not read that difference as two different embeds.
- **Decided AGAINST** tagging `TARGET` with `?utm_source=itch&utm_campaign=handoff` (user call): a `(direct)` handoff arrival always has its ↻itch row immediately below it in Recent Visits, which is enough for spot-checking, and the marker would file organic itch traffic into the paid-campaign tables built for the Brave buy. Accepted cost is the aggregate gap noted in official-intelligence-web/NOTES.md.
- **Open, not started:** `recordVisit()` is not gated on page visibility while `analytics.js` `recordPageLoad` is, so unattended loads become full ○○○ visit rows and the two tables disagree by construction. The admin tagging is a read-side mitigation only; gating it at the source is still to do (and would need a release).


## 2026-08-27 — Release v1.73: "ganged" → **Echo**, the turn travels the group, per-tile click + lit hold
- **"Ganged" is dead — the mechanic is "Echo" (user call).** Reason recorded because it will read as churn otherwise: "ganged" is the right electronics term and lands perfectly for an EE, but the audience is not EEs and "gang" carries a negative connotation. 90 player strings (6 keys × 15 locales) reworded, each locale using its own word for an echo (eco / écho / Echo / эхо / yankı / صدى / प्रतिध्वनि / エコー / 에코 / 回声). ⚠ **Code and wire format still keep `twin`/`_twin`** (stored recordings), and the mosaic LAYOUT field `ganged` + its `g<pct>` save-code segment ALSO stay — stored data, not copy. Designer-tool labels did move (Echo coverage / Echo size / Echo %). See the front-end CLAUDE.md terminology section.
- **The name drove the motion: a group no longer twists in unison.** The touched unit spins first and each ring partner follows, starting as the one before it passes its HALFWAY point (`ECHO_OVERLAP = 0.5`, user call — strict one-after-the-next read as a queue of separate turns rather than one travelling wave). Ring order comes free from `twinPartnerCells`, which walks from the member the player touched. A queued step HOLDS its unit at the pre-turn angle (`tileRotationTransform`'s t<0 clamp) — Maze state moved the whole group on the click, so without the hold the waiting partners would sit at their new angle and then not appear to move.
- **Pacing knobs, and why there is a cap at all:** gangs run to 16 members, so per-step duration compresses to keep the span inside `ECHO_MAX_TOTAL_MS` (1200). The overlap bought back most of that — a 6-member group is 700ms and nothing compresses until 12 members. Steps never stop being distinct; only the tempo moves.
- **Click SFX is per TILE, not per input** (user call). `animateRotationAt` takes `opts.onStep`; game.js passes `ECHO_CLICK` from the three sites that already click on step 0 — live rotate, hint, live undo. ⚠ **Do NOT make this unconditional inside render**: replay rotations and the tutorial demo animate silently today and must stay that way (the tutorial never touches Sfx at all). `Sfx.click()` bypasses the ducking/throttle pipeline, so consecutive taps were already fine.
- **Three things a spinning tile must NOT take with it — all latent bugs that only 200ms of unison hid** (all field-reported this session, in this order): (1) LIT STATE — a held unit now keeps its pre-turn lighting (`applyLitFreeze`/`preTurnCellOf`), or the circuit visibly runs through a lane that has not swung into place; (2) bevel LIGHTING — `bevelColorForEdge` read every normal in the tile's FINAL orientation, so a turning tile wore its destination shading from frame one (`spinLightOffsetDeg`); (3) brushed-metal GRAIN — one repeating pattern in canvas space, so the spin snapped that tile's streaks 90° out of alignment with the whole board at click time (`activeSpin`, un-spinning the CTM for that one pattern fill). **The rule: a tile turns; the board's light and brushing do not.** Gates got (2) and (3) too, for consistency.
- **NEW suite `../tests/echo-lit-freeze-test.js`** pins the lit-hold algebra (which cell a unit is drawn on, which port each lane had) against the real maze.js, with ground truth from tile-OBJECT identity — quad/mosaic rotation moves objects between cells, so it owes nothing to the mapping under test. ⚠ It EXTRACTS `preTurnCellOf` from render.js by source text (render.js is not loadable headlessly); renaming it or moving it off `originX`/`originY`/`cellSize`/`Maze` fails the suite loudly. Verified load-bearing by mutation: flipping the hold-angle sign fails sections 3+4 128/128 and 84/85.
- Suites green: echo-lit-freeze (new), twin, mosaic, mosaic-library 180/180, tooltip, potd-replay-rotation. Not browser-verified per rule 0 — the whole change is animation, so it wants a real playthrough.
- **Open, not done:** the gold win-glow is driven by `Maze.pathsWon`, not the lit map, so on a winning move it can flare before the last echo tile has landed. Left alone deliberately (nobody has reported it); gate it on the echo finishing if it reads wrong.

## 2026-08-25 — Release v1.72: first structured data + full search/social meta
- **Same driver as TANTЯO v4.42** — Google Ads suspension for "Unacceptable Business Practices"; the work is making the advertiser's identity as an individual legible on every surface. See the tantro-front-end NOTES entry of the same date for the full picture.
- **Had no JSON-LD at all; now carries a `Person` + `VideoGame` graph.** Person — never Organization — is author + creator + publisher + copyrightHolder, sharing `@id` `https://official-intelligence.art/#ryan` with TANTЯO and the umbrella site so the three sites describe a single entity. `"Digeratist"` in `alternateName` bridges the itch storefront name to the person.
- **Had no `description`, `og:*`, `twitter:*`, or `canonical` either** — links to the game previewed as a bare URL. All added. `og:image` is the shared image host's `circuitousness/Singular 3 Paths.jpg` (verified live: 200, image/jpeg, 688 KB), matching the card the umbrella homepage already uses.
- **The meta tags are deliberately NOT i18n'd**, unlike the `<title>` directly above them — crawlers and unfurlers read raw HTML before any script runs, so `data-i18n` could never reach them. Same choice TANTЯO makes; a comment in the file says so, because the neighbouring `data-i18n` title invites the opposite assumption.
- `canonical` earns its place here more than on a normal site: the game is also served inside itch.io and CrazyGames iframes.

## 2026-08-21 — Release v1.71: first-timers get simplified intro (PLAY + INSTALL); overlay crop fixes; no more lone gates
- **Game-wide rule: never exactly ONE gate (user call) — 0 or ≥2 everywhere a count is determined.** All gates share one rotation delta, so a lone gate is trivial (one twist parks it clear and nothing else moves); the mechanic only bites when clearing one gate can swing another into the way. Four sites: practice board 3 `gates: 1→2` (config.js FIRST_RUN_PRACTICE; still one mechanic per board, teachGate unchanged), game.js live formula now `max(2, 1 + growth/4)` (zen gate-free openings stay 0; ramp above the floor unchanged — Surge L1 and Zen L4 go 1→2 gates), potd-gen2 `rollParams` maps a rolled 1→2 (defaults were already 2–6, this guards dev-panel ranges), `designGateTarget` floor 1→2 (small designed mosaics 4×4..5×6 go 1→2). Gates.assignGates itself deliberately untouched — placement still honors any explicit target, and gates-test.js's single-gate sections keep testing the requireBlocking machinery.
- New assertions: onboarding-ladder-test (no practice board carries a lone gate) + potd-config-test (no roll is exactly 1). All suites green (onboarding-ladder, gates, mosaic, mosaic-library, potd-config).
- ⚠ Teaching board now has TWO gates, one guaranteed blocking (requireBlocking anchors delta on the blocking one; the second is ordinary placement) — the gate demo/tip flow is unchanged, but worth an eyeball that the 5×5 practice board doesn't feel crowded.
- **First-visit full intro skip is GONE on the main site (user call, after first load on a cheap Android handheld): a newcomer auto-started into practice with no screen that could ever carry the Install button.** New policy: CG keeps the full skip (≤1-click rule, `intro-skip`); DESKTOP first-timers also keep the skip/auto-start (second user call, same day — desktop has no install button to show, so the intro stop bought nothing there; the phone/tablet test is a UA sniff inlined in index.html's pre-paint block mirroring `DeviceDetection.detect()`, deliberately UA-based so touch-screen laptops count as desktop); a first-time browser on a PHONE/TABLET gets a SIMPLIFIED intro — title + music/SFX/fullscreen toggles + ▶ PLAY + install hint/button. New pre-paint class `html.intro-first` (index.html decides, CSS hides `.introWarningBlock`/`.introPlaySub`, intro.js hides them inline as stale-CSS belt-and-braces). Toggles STAY for first-timers (user call, same day — first cut hid them): they're setup, not flavor; a newcomer should get to mute before PLAY starts the music. Phones still hide just the fullscreen toggle via the existing mobile logic. PLAY lands them in the same auto-start practice puzzle — dismiss() already routed through `Marathon.autoStartFirstPractice`, so only the skip condition moved.
- ⚠ **Funnel semantics shift**: phone/tablet first-timers' `recordAgreed` now fires on the PLAY tap, not at page load (desktop/CG first-timers still fire it at load via the skip path) — "agreed" is a real gate for newcomers again, so expect a nonzero intro bounce for them in the admin funnel (the WARNING gag that caused the old 29% bounce is hidden for them, though). Stale cached index.html still tags first-timers `intro-skip` → old auto-start path, still fully wired.
- First-timers also no longer draw from the warnings shuffle-bag (an invisible draw burned a warning before they'd ever seen one).
- **Centered-flex-overflow crop fixed in three places** (a centered flex column that overflows crops BOTH ends unreachably — same family as the `left: 50%` traps): the itch redirect overlay in platform-redirect.js (field report: landscape scanner cropped the message; content now in a `margin:auto` wrapper + `overflow-y:auto`), the intro's short-viewport media query (justify center → grow-only pseudo-spacers), and Tantro's `.start-overlay` (see its NOTES). ⚠ Pseudo-spacers must carry negative margins cancelling their two flex gaps (−0.35rem here) or they add phantom height that conjures a scrollbar on exactly-fitting viewports — bitten on Tantro, same day. #introOverlay also hides scrollbar chrome now (`scrollbar-width: none` + webkit twin; scrolling itself still works — NOT overflow:hidden, that would re-crop).
- The itch message appearing landscape on the sensor-less device is NOT fixable from the page (browser tab inherits panel-natural orientation; `screen.orientation.lock` needs fullscreen). Scrollability is the mitigation; CSS-rotate hack considered and rejected.
- **"Add to Home Screen" labels wrap as a unit**: the quoted menu-item label in `intro.fullscreenHint.ios/.android` uses `&nbsp;` between its words now, all 15 locales (scripted edit on the LAST `<strong>` per string; ja/zh have no spaces). Safe because the hint is set via innerHTML. Mirrored in TANTЯO the same day.
- ⚠ Testing the Install button: Chrome never fires `beforeinstallprompt` for an origin it considers ALREADY INSTALLED — the hint then shows the manual add-to-home-screen text, which looks like a bug but is the designed fallback (bit the user 2026-08-21; the installed desktop PWA was the cause). Uninstall via chrome://apps or use a clean profile. Also: installed-state keys on origin + start_url (`/`), so two games served from the SAME localhost port can suppress each other's prompt.
- Not browser-verified per rule 0. Worth testing: fresh-profile visit on own origin (simplified intro → PLAY → practice), returning player still gets the full gag, CG still skips. Version 1.70 → 1.71.

## 2026-08-19 — Release v1.70: Spotify + Apple Music credits clicks now tracked
- **Soundtrack store links in the credits were invisible to click tracking**: user clicked 4 links, only 3 recorded. The Spotify and Apple Music `<a>`s carried vestigial ids (`creditsSpotify`/`creditsApple`, referenced by nothing) but not the `data-credit-link` attribute share.js's capture listener keys on. Now tagged `spotify` / `apple-music`. Swept every other anchor: all credits links tracked, share-popup donate link is structural (`.share-donate a`), legal-footer links untracked by design.
- Same session, other folders (not zipped): per-visitor wipe ✕ added to the admin Recent Visits table (official-intelligence-web) + `dryRun` support and share/credits click counts on the back-end's `wipe-session-visits` (rate limit 5→30/min). ⚠ **Deploy back-end to Render BEFORE the admin page** — the old deployed endpoint ignores `dryRun`, so the ✕ button's preview request would real-delete.

## 2026-08-15 — Release v1.69: Mosaic gangs/gates/design-loop, "ganged" rename, difficulty calibration pipeline
- **Mosaic grew its designed-play loop**: editor Test Play routes through `PotdGen2.playDesign`; solving a designed board and clicking for a new puzzle regenerates the SAME layout (`PotdGen2.nextPuzzle`; panel Generate breaks the loop). Dev-mode boards now tumble in/out via the live spin machinery (`spinAwayCurrentBoard` + `loadIntoPlay`'s `spinInBoard`; failed builds must `cancelSpin` or the canvas stays hidden).
- **Mosaic got twins ("ganged") and gates.** Piece-level lockstep rings (`assignMosaicTwins`, quad's analogue): rotation/hint/lock/scramble/penalties/pricing all ring-aware. ⚠ **Every multi-cell piece is a MANDATORY ring member** (the gang color is what makes complex shapes legible — user call), each bringing ≥1 SINGLE partner; two pieces weld only when singles/palette run out (welded terminal-carrying pieces intersect their allowed-turn pools toward {0} and arrive near-solved — field-reported). Coverage 0 is the only off switch (tests rely on it). Gates: count derived from grid size (`designGateTarget`, marathon's curve) — never a designer knob.
- **"Twins" renamed "ganged" in ALL player copy** (90 i18n strings × 15 locales; each language uses its coupled/linked-motion term — see the front-end CLAUDE.md terminology section). ⚠ Code and wire format KEEP `twin`/`_twin` — stored recordings depend on it; never rename those, never let "twin" back into copy.
- **Mosaic difficulty reworked twice (user-called both times)**: now `0.45×size + 0.55×coverage×(1+0.5×complexity) − 0.12×ganged_effective`, where complexity = size-weighted mean piece size (big tangles amplify coverage) and ganged relief is small (~1 tier over its whole range) and computed on the MANDATE FLOOR `(pieces+partners)/(pieces+singles)`. Fully-covered boards cap at 88. Ganged % is part of the LAYOUT (saves/encodes as `g<pct>`; editor slider min = the floor). No mosaic back-compat existed — nothing deployed, no libraries stored.
- **Sealed-loop guard**: `solveGroupFillers` rejects fillings whose wired component never escapes the piece (a ring piece filled as a closed circle — field-reported, ~1 board in 15 without the guard, mutation-verified). Editor warns (stays open) when a test build's `minSolveMoves` < 4×paths — near-solved guardrail, same check a future submission gate would run.
- **Difficulty-calibration pipeline (NEW, needs back-end deploy)**: every SOLVED designed-mosaic test play POSTs to `/api/mosaic/solve` (fire-and-forget from `PotdGen2.onBoardSolved`, hooked in game.js's debug win branch): moves/time/hints/undos + full difficulty breakdown (JSON) + this build's `minMoves` floor + layout code + pathCount. Admin read: `GET /api/admin/mosaic/solves` (JWT; `?format=csv`, `?limit`; computes movesOverFloor — THE ratio to plot against score). ⚠ Row id 2 on prod is Claude's marked `verify-probe` canary — filter or delete. Analysis plan: where movesOverFloor-vs-score bends, the formula needs tweaking; pathCount recorded for the paths-easier-or-harder question (deliberately NOT in the formula yet).
- **Editor UX**: swastika-lookalike shape vetoed from the library by canonical key (26 shapes now; its CCW mirror and thick variants REMAIN — user only asked for the CW one); Rows/Cols/Ganged%/Test-paths are now sliders (dims commit on release — live commit would prune pieces mid-drag); board-wrap scrollbar fixed (refit now subtracts real padding; wrap overflow:hidden).
- ⚠ **Rollout direction (user, "nothing to act on yet")**: may fold Mosaic into Zen/Surge progression (coverage ramp), possibly DROP Quad (subset of Mosaic), simplify PotD 8→3 difficulty slots. Don't deepen Quad-only investments.
- Suites: mosaic-test (twin rings + mandate + no-weld + sealed-loop sections), mosaic-library-test 180/180 (ganged format/floor, complexity, veto), NEW mosaic-solve-telemetry.test.py (back-end). Not browser-verified per rule 0. Version 1.68 → 1.69. Back-end state: the telemetry POST is ALREADY live on Render (verified via probe — user's first solve stored as row 1), but the ADMIN endpoint (`/api/admin/mosaic/solves`) was written after that deploy → **needs one more Render push**.

## 2026-08-15 — Release v1.68: PotD portrait replays fixed; dims roll reworked for shape diversity
- **PotD replay orientation (sister-reported): a portrait player's moves are recorded on the 90°-CW-rotated board, but the submit shipped only the flat moves array, so every replay reconstructed against the server's canonical landscape snapshot and twisted the wrong tiles.** Three-part fix: submit now sends a `rotated` flag (derived from the recording's `initialState` dims — canonical is always landscape, so portrait-taller ⇒ rotated; survives quit/resume for free), the back end stores/returns it (nullable; **already deployed to Render this session**), and `startPotdReplay` rebuilds the rotated board via the same `Game.applyBoardRotation(false)` pipeline the player's client ran before pairing it with the moves.
- ⚠ **Legacy rows (rotated=null) go through two-stage inference, and the second stage exists because the first FAILED IN PRODUCTION the same day it shipped:** the coordinate proof (any move row ≥ canonical row count) has a blind spot on near-square boards — 8×9 leaves exactly ONE distinguishing index, and the sister's real 13-move s1 solve missed it. Fallback: `potdDryRunSolves` replays the moves data-only in both orientations (same Maze/Gates entry points as the real replay, minus Render) and keeps the orientation that ends SOLVED. Validated against all four of her production recordings: rotated solved 4/4, canonical 0/4, ~3ms.
- ⚠ **The mid-session "still broken after deploy" was ALSO a missing version bump** — new files were deployed under 1.67, so returning clients' service workers never refetched (the user's hard refresh happened to bypass it on desktop; her phone never would). Deploying front-end changes without `/release` is invisible to every returning PWA client.
- **PotD dims roll reworked (user call, after all four singular slots rolled 8×9 the same day): aspect ratio + area over an enumerated vocabulary, replacing roll-two-axes-then-clamp.** The old `fitToTileRange` first-fit shrink was a funnel — every roll whose smaller axis was ≥9 walked to exactly 8×9, making it **54% of all singular boards** (all-four-identical ≈ 1 day in 11). New roll: enumerate every stepped landscape pair inside size bounds + area window, roll target ratio uniformly across the vocabulary's ratio span (area breaks ratio ties), pick nearest. Singular now spreads 5–14% across all 12 shapes; identical-day odds ≈ 1 in 830. **Shape set is provably unchanged** (the enumeration IS the old allowlist), so no new 4-path proof was needed — but `--build` was re-run anyway since the rare-slow shapes (11×6 et al) now roll ~9× more often: all 16 shapes built, worst 18.3s. **Takes effect at next seed, i.e. tomorrow's puzzles.** Old walk kept only as a degenerate-panel-config fallback.
- New suite `../tests/potd-replay-rotation-test.js` (33 checks: heuristic both directions, cross-file flag round-trip greps, dry-run tiebreak against a real board). `potd-config-test.js` grew **diversity assertions** — every allowlisted shape must appear, peak share capped at 25% singular / 50% quad — because the allowlist alone was blind to the 8×9 collapse.
- Not browser-verified per rule 0. Version bumped 1.67 → 1.68; back end needs no further deploy.

## 2026-08-11 — Release v1.67: landing-URL campaign capture (Brave Ads groundwork)
- **Why:** the user is weighing a **Brave Ads self-serve** campaign (the "$500/month minimum" option from the 2026-07-08 Advertising session — re-verify that figure, it's a year old). A Brave notification click arrives with an EMPTY referrer, and `tracking.js` had no other source signal, so paid arrivals would have been indistinguishable from bookmarks and PWA launches inside the admin's "(direct)" bucket. Capture had to exist before any money was spent.
- `tracking.js` reads `utm_source/medium/campaign/content` at module init and sends them on the visit POST **and** the impression record (a click that bounces before touching a tile never becomes a `page_visits` row — for a paid buy that bounce is the number).
- ⚠ **The tags are STRIPPED from the address bar afterwards (`replaceState`), and that half matters as much as the capture.** A tag left in the URL outlives its campaign: TANTЯO still receives `?rdt_cid=` referrers a month after those ads stopped, because a bookmarked landing URL re-attributes every later visit. Only `utm_*` is removed — `?track`, `?dev`, `?nointro` are other modules' flags and must survive. The strip is deliberately NOT gated on suppression: an opted-out browser posts nothing but still shouldn't carry a live tag around.
- ⚠ **Ads must point at the bare origin, tagged — never at an itch/CrazyGames embed URL.** `platform-redirect.js` sends mobile embed players to a hardcoded `TARGET` with no query, so a tag wouldn't survive the hop.
- New suite `../tests/campaign-capture-test.js` (vm sandbox over the real `tracking.js`) covers both halves. Not browser-verified per rule 0.
- ⚠ **Deploy the BACK END first** — its `utm_*` columns must exist before a tagged client posts, or the first paid clicks land uncredited. Only `tracking.js` changed in this zip (+ the version pair).
- Version bumped 1.66 → 1.67.

## 2026-08-11 — Release v1.66: pitch gets Zen/PotD exits, demo-beat crash fixed
- **The practice→Surge pitch now offers Zen and Puzzle of the Day as secondary exits.** Surge stays THE suggestion (it's the only mode with a terminal state, which is what lets the first session end on a "try again" rather than trailing off); the two alternates are styled to lose to it deliberately — no gold fill, dimmer, smaller. Only ONE new i18n key (`marathon.pitchAlt`, ×15); the button labels reuse `mode.practice.name` / `mode.potd.name`, already translated.
- ⚠ **Zen starts a run; PotD goes to the MENU, and that asymmetry is deliberate** — the daily is EIGHT slots and the menu is where their badges, streak chips and countdown live, so picking one for the player would invent the one choice the mode is built around them making.
- ⚠ **Neither exit passes the auto-start flag to `startGame`.** That flag enrols a run in the first-run A/B ladder (`ensureFirstRunVariant`, `firstRunTiers`) and routes solves to `firstRunPuzzleSolved` — all of which describe the scripted Surge run specifically. Without it they correctly record as play OUTSIDE the initial run. Getting this wrong would have quietly polluted the variant test.
- ⚠ **KNOWN FUNNEL BLIND SPOT, open question for next session:** neither exit fires any tracking, so those players read as *reached `pitch`, no `surge`* — indistinguishable from a bounce. Deliberate (a funnel that claims a Surge start that didn't happen is worse than a gap), but it means the pitch's conversion number is now an undercount of "chose something". Splitting them needs a new first-run flag column server-side; not done.
- **Fixed a real crash reported this session: dismissing a demo tip mid-spin threw and froze the card.** Root cause — `rotationAnims`/`gateAnims` are MODULE-SHARED queues whose entries bake their pivot in the coordinate space active when pushed and are keyed `"r,c"` against the grid loaded then. `stopBeat` restored the board but left the demo's in-flight spins behind, so `endTutorial → resize → draw` took the partial-redraw path and walked demo cell keys into the player's smaller grid (`grid[r][c]` undefined → throw at maze.js getConnections). Fix: `Render.dropTileAnimations()` called at BOTH ends of `beginTutorial`/`endTutorial` — **nothing outlives the canvas swap** is the invariant. ⚠ This latently affected the How-to-Solve MODAL's close path too, which shares `endTutorial`.
- **Two secondary fixes from the same report:** `drawAnimatedFrame` now bounds-checks the anims' OWN keys (the neighbour loop always did; this path had none, so any anim outliving its grid walked off the end — a dimension change mid-spin is the same hazard from another direction), and `tooltip.js takeDownCard` wraps its `stopBeat` call so a foreign throw can't strand the card with its handlers attached. Regression test added (`throwOnStop` stub).
- ⚠ **Re-learned the `left: 50%` centering trap — SECOND time in this codebase.** A fixed box with `left: 50%; right: auto` computes its automatic width against only the space RIGHT of the 50% line, so the modal squeezed to ~180px on a 385px phone and the starved canvas shrank the demo board until its grooves hit render.js's `max(4, cellSize * GROOVE_FRAC)` DEVICE-pixel floor (looks "fat" because 4px on a 20px cell is 0.20, not the intended 0.17). `#solveTransition` documents the same bug from 2026-07-28. Now centered with symmetric insets + `margin-inline: auto`, which sidesteps it by construction. **Reach for insets+auto-margins, never `left: 50%`, on anything that must be wide.**
- ⚠ **Not browser-verified** (rule 0). Worth exercising: dismissing a gate tip DELIBERATELY mid-spin (the crash's trigger window is ~300ms per rotation), the pitch's two new buttons, and the demo modal in portrait AND landscape phone (the groove floor only misbehaves at dpr 1, so an emulator will look worse than a real device).

## 2026-08-11 — Demo-beat tips go modal; the non-blocking route we DIDN'T take
- **A beat tip now renders as a centered modal** (`.tooltip-demo`, added by `tooltip.js` from `Tutorial.isBeatPlaying()` read right after `playBeat`). Reason: a beat borrows the singleton `Maze` + renderer for as long as the card is up, so the real board is genuinely dead — and in the top-left corner that read as an optional aside that had mysteriously broken the puzzle. Text-only tips keep the corner treatment; they block nothing. Class keys off the beat ACTUALLY taking the board, so a refused `playBeat` degrades to a plain corner tip instead of a modal around an empty canvas.
- ⚠ **Rejected first attempt: bounding the loop** (`BEAT_LOOPS = 2`, hand the board back, leave a still frame). Reverted — the complaint was never that it replayed forever, it was that it stopped play at all. The 2026-08-05 loop-until-dismissed call stands.
- **BACK POCKET — how to make the demo non-blocking for real, if the interruption ever stops being acceptable.** `Render.renderSnippet` is the *transient* twin of `beginTutorial`: fully synchronous save → draw → restore in a `finally`. `title-renderer.js` already snapshots the live `Maze`, loads a synthetic grid, snippets, and restores it — at 60fps, against a real board. JS being single-threaded, the player's handlers can never observe the demo state. Switching the beat to that (interval-driven, discrete states, no tween) is a NET DELETION: `setBoardInert`/`.maze-demo-inert`, every `isBusy()`/`isBeatPlaying()` guard, the save-state bail-outs, the page-hide `stopBeat()` ordering, and this modal all go. ⚠ The one catch: `animateRotationAt` pushes into a module-shared `rotationAnims` with pivots baked in the ACTIVE coordinate space, so a demo spin would tilt the real board's tile at the same `r,c` around a wrong pivot — hence "discrete states, no tween" for v1 (arguably better for the twin lesson anyway: simultaneity is what's being taught). ~15 lines in `render.js` to add a beat-local rotation swap only if the snap reads too abrupt. **Considered and rejected: pre-baking an actual animated file** — needs a generator page, an encode step, no good format (GIF bands on the glow gradients, animated WebP needs a WASM encoder, WebM excludes older iOS Safari), ~200–600KB into a flat zip, AND the demo silently goes stale whenever the renderer's look changes.
- **Backdrop is a `box-shadow` spread, NOT a pseudo-element** — and this is a trap worth not re-falling into. The `::before { position: fixed; inset: 0; z-index: -1 }` version painted ABOVE the card's own background and border (CSS 2.1 painting order: element bg/border is step 1, negative-z descendants step 2), so it clipped the rounded gold corners square and flattened the card to near-black. A spread shadow paints strictly outside the border box and follows `border-radius` for free.
- **Fixed a latent bug the backdrop had been masking for an hour:** `handleHintClick` never carried the `Tutorial.isBusy()` guard that `undo`/`resetPuzzle`/board-penalty all have, so HINT during a beat locked a tile on the demo grid AND charged Surge's 25% time penalty. Pre-dates this session. Input protection now lives only in the guards (they cover keyboard; a backdrop never could) — the backdrop is purely visual and deliberately does not swallow clicks.
- ⚠ **Not browser-verified** (rule 0). Worth exercising: the twin + gate tips on a first run (centering, corner radius, backdrop weight — the board keeps its own `opacity: 0.3` dim UNDER a 0.7 backdrop, which may read muddy; dropping the inert dim is the fix if so), and HINT during a beat now doing nothing.

## 2026-08-10 — Release v1.65: Media Session, interrupted-song restart, practice "n of m"
- **Media Session API wired into `music.js`** (mirrors TANTЯO's audio.js) — Android's media notification / lock screen now shows song title + "Circuitousness" artist line and prev/next skip buttons instead of a bare pause-only player; earbud/keyboard media keys work too. Metadata + playbackState refresh through `notifySongChange`, so every existing state path updates the OS card with no new call sites. Found because the user's sister backgrounded both games and compared the notifications.
- **Interrupted-song restart:** a gameplay song cut off by a puzzle ending (PotD solve, game over, quit-to-menu — i.e. `Music.stop()`) now replays from the top on the next gameplay pick, UNLESS it was ≥75% played (then it advances as before). One-shot override consumed in `pickNext`; persisted as `<slug>_interruptedSong_v1`. ⚠ Deliberate exclusions from capture: menu-pool songs, end-credits track (`stopCreditsSequence` now stops BEFORE clearing forceCredits so the guard can see it), replay playback, muted. Manual ⏭ skips do NOT trigger a restart — only system-initiated stops.
- **Practice solve popup now says "Practice puzzle {n} of {of} solved!"** — `marathon.js` passes `practiceCount()`, all 15 i18n locales updated reusing each language's `hudPractice` idiom. The FINAL practice solve never shows this popup (goes to the mode pitch), so only "1 of 3"/"2 of 3" appear.
- ⚠ **Not browser-verified** (rule 0 — user tests). Worth exercising: background the game on Android mid-song (check title + skip buttons), solve a puzzle mid-song then start the next (song should restart), solve one at >75% (should advance), dismiss credits then start a puzzle (credits song must NOT restart).

## 2026-08-08 — Mosaic layout EDITOR (step 1 of the hand-authored puzzle library)
- **The premise, and it's a direction change worth stating: mosaic puzzles are meant to be DESIGNED, not rolled.** `Mosaic.pack()`'s random darts stay exactly as they were (and got a second job — the editor's Auto-fill seeds a board from them), but shipped mosaic puzzles are to come from a hand-built library ranked by difficulty, which Surge/Zen then draw from in increasing order. Player-contributed layouts are the eventual goal, so the format and the editor were built with that in mind from the start.
- ⚠ **A LAYOUT IS THE PIECE PACKING ONLY — it is not a maze.** The wiring (paths, ports, twins, gates, scramble) is still generated fresh by maze.js every time a layout is played, so one design is a *family* of puzzles sharing a shape. That split is not incidental: it's what makes the difficulty ranking well-defined, since both of its inputs are properties of the layout and neither depends on the maze rolled on top. Don't "improve" this into a whole-puzzle editor without re-deciding the ranking.
- **Difficulty = size + mosaic coverage %, per the design call, and NOTHING else.** `100 × (0.5 × sizeScore + 0.5 × coverage)`, ten tiers. sizeScore normalises area over 16 (4×4) → 256 (16×16) and clamps. Coverage is linear — the literal reading, and there's no solve-time data yet that would justify a curve. ⚠ **Piece-size distribution is deliberately excluded** (a 5×5 ring is surely harder than four 2×2s at equal coverage); the editor SHOWS mean piece size beside the score so the omission is visible rather than forgotten. Constants live at the top of `mosaic-library.js` and the readout shows the arithmetic, so retuning is a one-line change with visible consequences.
- **Coverage is counted through `packFromLayout`, not off the raw piece list** — so a piece that's out of bounds or overlapping contributes zero. A score taken off the list would over-report exactly the layouts that are broken.
- **New: `mosaic-library.js`** (format + ranking + store, pure logic, no DOM) and **`mosaic-editor.js`** (all UI). `mosaic.js` gained the geometry half — `packFromLayout` / `layoutFromPacking` / `findShape` and the `(n, mask)` encoding. That split is on purpose: maze.js and the worker need only `mosaic.js`, so **no new dependency entered the build path**.
- ⚠ **The `(n, mask)` encoding is what makes a stored layout safe.** `findShape` is keyed off the SAME enumeration that guarantees rotation invariance, so a layout can never smuggle in a shape the packer would have refused — and a non-invariant piece rotates cells into its neighbours and corrupts the board. Mask rather than the enumeration index or `key` string, so a change to MAX_FRAME or the sort order can't reinterpret stored puzzles.
- **`Maze.setMosaicLayout(layout)`** — null = the random packer, unchanged default. ⚠ **Set it unconditionally per job**, same rule as `setTwinCoverageTarget`, with more at stake: the Maze instance outlives a build, so an inherited layout would ship somebody's DESIGNED board to a job that never asked for one. maze-worker.js already does. **A dims mismatch falls back to the random packer rather than replaying a partial design** — packFromLayout would happily drop what no longer fits, and a designed board quietly delivered as a partial one with nothing on screen saying so is the bad outcome.
- **Editor reached from Developer mode** — "Open Mosaic Editor" in the debug panel, or Ctrl+M while it's open. Drag from the palette (built FROM `Mosaic.SHAPES`, never a hand-listed copy — all 27), drag off the board to delete, right-click removes, arrows nudge, tap a palette shape to drop it in the first free spot. Pointer events throughout, so touch works with no second code path. **No rotate or mirror control, and both omissions are correct**: every shape is rotation-invariant (rotating does nothing) and chiral shapes already appear in the palette alongside their mirrors.
- **Pieces NEST** — a ring's hole takes a smaller piece or singular fill, which is why the overlap check is per-CELL. That concentric look is the whole reason holes are kept in the shape library.
- **No i18n, deliberately** — dev tooling behind the same gate as the PotD tuning panel, whose chrome is likewise plain English. Universal rule 5 governs player-facing text; the eventual player-facing contribution UI is a different surface and gets translated.
- **Storage is localStorage (`<slug>_mosaicLib_v1`) as an explicit stand-in for the DB.** The entry shape is already the row shape (id/name/author/layout/difficulty/tier/createdAt) and `encode()` produces the wire form — `M1:10x8:3,0,0,186:…`, ~12 chars a piece — so promoting it is a transport change, not a format change. ⚠ **Export is the ONLY backup this library has** until the back end lands. Import merges by id rather than replacing wholesale.
- `tests/mosaic-library-test.js` — **162/162 passing**, and §10 boots the real editor behind a fake DOM to drive one drag through its shipped pointer handlers. That section exists specifically because rule 0 means the editor is never browser-verified here.
- ⚠ **NOT browser-verified** (rule 0 — user tests). Worth exercising first: a drag from the palette, a drag off-board to delete, Auto-fill → edit → Save → Load, and Test play at 2+ paths.
- **Still to come (deliberately not in this step):** the back-end `mosaic_layouts` table + endpoints, and Surge/Zen selecting from the library by ascending tier. No admin endpoints were stubbed (universal rule 7).

## 2026-08-07 — Store submissions: IndieDB submitted, mod.io rejected
- **mod.io evaluated and SET ASIDE — don't re-derive it.** The goal was audience; mod.io is UGC *infrastructure*, not discovery (its browsers already play the game), so an empty profile wins nothing. IndieDB is the discovery site — same parent, DBolical, which is why IndieDB funnels devs toward mod.io. Full reasoning in `../../PLAY_STORE.md` step 5a, which also spells out what Play submission would grow into IF UGC ever ships (in-app reporting, moderation, content-rating and Data-safety changes).
- **Even after the planned Mosaic builder, mod.io is still the wrong host:** a puzzle is a few-hundred-byte encoded string, not a versioned file payload. A share code or a puzzles table on the existing Render back-end fits; mod.io would carry a payload smaller than its own metadata.
- ⚠ **"Marathon" leaked into the first IndieDB submission.** Player-facing modes are PotD / ⚡ Surge / 🧘 Zen — `marathon` and `practice` are internal ids only (save keys, tracking rows, leaderboard routing). Listing copy also wrongly said "two modes" when there are three. Fixed on IndieDB and in `../../STORE_LISTINGS.md`, which now carries a warning against both mistakes.
- **Gamepad claim pulled from all listing copy** — user confirmed it's unimplemented and probably not worth building: Surge is grid-cursor navigation against a clock, where a d-pad loses to both mouse and touch. Universal rule b was softened the same day to make gamepad optional; this folder's `CLAUDE.md` now records the skip as deliberate.
- **Known dead weight, deliberately NOT removed yet:** `gamepad.js` is a no-op stub still listed in `index.html`'s script array (~line 1366). Deleting it is a code change wanting a version bump, so it was kept out of submission-day work. The `gamepadconnected` listener further down is unrelated — it's an audio-unlock trigger and should stay.
- Listing platform tags are **Web only** on purpose. All six of Windows/Mac/Linux/iOS/Android/Web are the same single web build; a store's platform tag implies a download exists. Android becomes honest once the Play listing is live.

## 2026-08-07 — Release v1.64 (the v1 PotD sizing fallback is DELETED)
- **User's call after v2 proved itself live.** `potd.js` no longer feature-detects `PotdGen2` — `rollSlotParams` calls it directly, and `generateDimsV1`, `GATE_TARGET_V1` and all 16 `SIZE_*` constants are gone. potd-gen2.js's `DEF_*` block is now the ONLY place a daily puzzle's shape is decided.
- **Why the "but what if gen2 doesn't load" worry is empty** (checked, don't re-litigate): both files are adjacent in index.html's `document.write` list and NEITHER is in sw.js's `CORE_ASSETS` — they're runtime-cached identically. Every failure mode loses both, and the fallback lived inside `potd.js`, so it could only have run in a world that can't occur. The bad outcome it "protected" against was worse anyway: a day's slot silently seeded to retired rules, permanent once seeded, with nothing on screen naming the generator.
- Fixed three comments left stale when v2 went live on 2026-08-01 — index.html's debug-panel header still called v2 experimental and "not yet swapped in", and maze.js's `twinCoverageOverride` comment listed live PotD among the *no-override* callers when every v2 build sets one.
- ⚠ **Not browser-verified** (rule 0). `tests/potd-config-test.js` passes and `node --check` is clean on potd.js/maze.js, but the suite only exercises gen2's rolled params — nobody has watched a real daily slot generate through the shortened path.

## 2026-08-07 — Where the analytics hooks live, and why there (companion to v1.63)
- **Puzzle rows open in `marathon.js onPuzzleReady`, NOT in `startNextPuzzle`.** The board isn't playable until then, and a quad build can take seconds — charging that to the player would inflate every duration. `startNextPuzzle` only stashes `pendingPuzzleMeta` (mode/type/dims), which is the last place that information exists. Its `state === STATE.PLAYING` guard is also what keeps replays out.
- **`game.js recordMove` is the single choke point** for the move counter AND the control-discovery tokens — every committed live action passes through it and replays never reach it. Don't add a second counting site; `Analytics.control()` short-circuits after a token's first occurrence, so the hot path costs one property lookup.
- **`resumed` is one-shot and means exactly one board:** the puzzle a restored run resumes INTO (marathon) or a checkpointed PotD attempt. Their clocks restart at zero while the player's real elapsed time doesn't, so the back end drops them from every statistic. Everything built after is a fresh puzzle whose clock starts at zero honestly.
- Mode labels: menu Practice and the first-run practice ladder are BOTH `'practice'` — `puzzle_index` and `first_run_stats` already separate them, and a third label would be a second source of truth. `'surge'` is specifically the run started from the pitch.
- ⚠ **Not browser-verified** (standing rule 0 — user tests). The hooks most worth exercising: practice→Surge→PotD start/finish, quit-to-menu mid-board, and a resumed run.

## 2026-08-07 — Release v1.63 (ENGAGEMENT ANALYTICS: playtime, per-puzzle outcome, abandonment)
- ⚠ **DEPLOY BACK-END FIRST** — two new tables (`puzzle_results`, `page_loads`), three ingest endpoints (`POST /api/load`, `/api/visit/<id>/session`, `/api/visit/<id>/puzzle`) and two admin reads (`GET /api/admin/engagement`, `/api/admin/retention`). The front-end starts POSTing to all three the moment the zip lands; ship the back-end ahead of it or the queue just accumulates 404s.
- **Why now: the CrazyGames trials were graded on numbers we did not have.** `page_visits` never measured time, and the funnel counted solves — so every puzzle collapsed into a tally and walking away mid-board was an absence of data rather than a fact. New `analytics.js` keeps two honest clocks (`visible`, tab-hidden excluded; `play`, only with a live unpaused board) and writes a row per puzzle attempt. See `../../CRAZYGAMES.md` before the next trial.
- Ported from TANTЯO's `analytics.js` / `engagement.py`, kept **structurally parallel on purpose** so the two admin panels read side by side. Divergence is deliberate where the games differ: Circuitousness has a `failed` outcome (Surge running out) that TANTЯO's top-out doesn't distinguish.
- **Two design rules that are not obvious from the code and must survive edits:** (1) every payload is queued in localStorage BEFORE it's sent and removed only on a 2xx — mobile glance-sessions are routinely shorter than a cold Render dyno's first response, so fire-and-hope loses exactly the sessions worth measuring; (2) the server merges WHOLE STATE, never deltas — `max()` on counters and a one-way rank ladder on `end_cause` — so a late duplicate beacon can't walk a solved puzzle back to `in_progress`. Retries and out-of-order delivery are expected, not exceptional.
- Suppression has ONE owner: `tracking.js` (localhost / `file://` / sticky `?track=false` / DevMode) calls `Analytics.disable()`, and nothing sends before it has a visit id. Don't add a second check here. Replays and the demo/AI board are deliberately untracked — the call sites sit inside `state === STATE.PLAYING` guards, which replays never satisfy.
- `engagement.py` is deliberately free of Flask/SQLAlchemy imports so `../tests/engagement-summary.test.py` can exercise it with namedtuples and no database — **35/35 passing** at time of release. TANTЯO shipped this logic inline and got a KeyError on its first real request; keep it importable.
- Version bumped 1.62 → 1.63.
- 🧹 **`first_run_stats` WIPED (728 rows) on 2026-08-07** via `POST /api/admin/wipe-first-run`, user's call. **The lifetime first-session funnel now starts at v1.62 — small numbers there mean a young dataset, not collapsed traffic.** Everything before was measuring a different game: pre-v1.58 rows are the retired Zen drop-in, and v1.58/v1.59 rows are players who hit the FROZEN PITCH SCREEN while v1.60's rows ran Surge with no visible timer. A date cutoff was considered and rejected for that reason — there was no salvageable window. **No effect on arm assignment**, despite what the wipe endpoint's docstring warns: that caveat predates `FIRST_RUN_VARIANT_FORCE: 'standard'` (locked 2026-08-05). The client now asserts `variant:'standard'` with `vLocked:1` on the row-creating sync, and `first_run_sync` honors a supplied variant BEFORE its `elif` reaches `_assign_first_run_variant()` — so the balancer never runs for a current client and had no row counts to lose. `PageVisit` deliberately NOT wiped, so referrers/devices/share history and the pre-Surge conversion baseline survive.

## 2026-08-06 — Release v1.62 (solve card says "Practice puzzle N solved!" during practice)
- The card read "Puzzle 2 solved!" through the practice sequence, which makes it look like a real run and undercuts the hand-off the pitch is about to make. New key `marathon.solveHeadlinePractice`, 15/15 languages.
- Gotcha for anyone editing that branch: **`practiceStep` has already been ADVANCED by the time the headline is painted.** The test still holds because it's only ever 0 outside practice — the final practice solve returns early to show the pitch, so it never reaches the headline with a stale value. `level` tracks the practice puzzles 1:1 and is the number the player expects.
- Version bumped 1.61 → 1.62.

## 2026-08-06 — Release v1.61 (FIX: banking did nothing — every puzzle started at exactly 2:30)
- 🐛 User caught it in play: the solve card said "+32s banked" and the next puzzle started at 2:30. So did the one after. And the one after that.
- ⚠ **CAUSE: v1.56 capped the TOTAL, and grants are the same size as the cap.** `min(banked + fresh, 150s)` with grants of 80-120s binds on essentially every puzzle from the third onward, so the clock read exactly the cap forever and playing well earned nothing. **A total cap makes the starting clock a constant — it is the wrong shape for a "banking" mechanic.**
- **Fix: `MAX_CARRY_SECONDS: 90` — cap what rolls IN, then add the grant on top.** `min(banked, cap) + fresh`. The starting clock now moves with performance (a competent player sees ~150-210s varying with the grant schedule instead of a flat 150), and hoarding becomes impossible BY CONSTRUCTION rather than merely bounded — you can never enter a puzzle with more than the cap.
- **The claw-back guard is gone**, and good riddance: a total cap needed `max(cap, fresh)` so quad's 380s grant wasn't instantly reduced to 150. Adding the grant AFTER the cap can never subtract what was just awarded, so the special case disappears.
- ⚠ **MY MISS, worth recording:** the model I wrote for v1.56 *printed* `clock=150s` on every line and I read past it — I was looking at puzzle counts and treated the flat column as the cap working rather than as banking dying. **When a modelled column is constant, that IS the finding.**
- **KNOWN LIMIT, documented at the constant:** competent-and-better players still sit AT the carry cap most of the run, because grants exceed what they consume — so skill barely separates them. The real lever is making grants taper (`TIME_DECREASE_PER_SOLVE` is currently cancelled out by the `START_TIME` bump at every tier-up), but that's a difficulty retune, not a banking fix, and should be measured before it's changed.
- Version bumped 1.60 → 1.61.

## 2026-08-06 — Release v1.60 (FIX: the first Surge run had NO VISIBLE TIMER)
- 🐛 The pitch handed off to Surge correctly, but the run started with no clock on screen.
- ⚠ **CAUSE: `body.mode-practice` is owned by ModePicker, not by the run.** `body.mode-practice #hudTimer { display: none }` is what hides the clock in Zen — and `ModePicker.DEFAULT_MODE` is `'practice'`, so a brand-new browser (no saved `_mode`) sits in practice chrome. The practice phase wants that; the Surge run that follows emphatically does not, and nothing was switching it. `startFirstSurge` now calls `ModePicker.setMode('marathon')`.
- **The same class drives more than the timer** — the undo cluster's layout and the How-to button's slot both key off it — so the rule is: **the body class must describe the run actually being played**, not the mode the player last picked.
- Harmless side effect worth knowing: `setMode` persists `<slug>_mode`, which is one of the three first-visit keys. By that point `AUTO_START_KEY` is already consumed, so `isFirstVisit()` was false anyway and nothing changes.
- **Also fixed:** the HUD quit button still said "📅 Daily & More" during Surge. That label is for the practice phase, where it advertises a menu the player hasn't seen; a chosen Surge run checkpoints on quit and wants the ordinary "Pause/Quit". Now keyed on `practiceStep`, like every other practice-only behaviour.
- Version bumped 1.59 → 1.60.

## 2026-08-06 — Release v1.59 (FIX: v1.58's pitch screen never appeared — frozen screen after practice 3)
- 🐛 **Symptom:** solve the third practice puzzle and everything vanishes — no pitch, no HUD, no buttons, apparently frozen.
- ⚠ **CAUSE 1 — `showOnly()` iterates a FIXED LIST of panels.** It hides every panel it knows about and reveals the one passed in. `firstRunPitchEl` wasn't in that list, so the call hid the HUD and revealed *nothing*. **Any new top-level panel MUST be added to that array**, not just styled; a panel missing from it is hidden-by-omission and can never be shown. Comment added at the list.
- ⚠ **CAUSE 2 — CSS specificity, and it would have bitten again on its own.** Panels are hidden by `html.mode-game #x { display: none }` (specificity **1,1,1**) and shown by `html.mode-game #x.visible` (1,2,1). The bare `#firstRunPitch.visible` I first wrote is only **1,1,0** — it LOSES to the hide rule. A panel that has its hide rule but not its *mode-gated* show rule stays invisible no matter what JS does. Both lists now carry the panel, with a warning comment.
- 🐛 **Also fixed, self-inflicted:** a sloppy scripted edit produced `html.mode-game html.mode-game #replayHud.visible`, a selector that can never match — that would have broken the REPLAY HUD too. Caught by a check that every panel in the hide list has a matching show rule; worth re-running that check after touching this block.
- `showFirstRunPitch` now also calls `clearTransition()` — onSolve returns early without painting a solve card but leaves `inTransition` set.
- Version bumped 1.58 → 1.59. No back-end change beyond v1.58's (still deploy that first if you haven't).

## 2026-08-06 — Release v1.58 (FIRST SESSION REBUILT: 3 practice puzzles → pitch → Surge)
- ⚠ **DEPLOY BACK-END FIRST** (three new `first_run_stats` columns), then this zip.
- **The premise, and it's the whole reason for the change: ZEN HAS NO TERMINAL STATE.** A Zen run only ends when the player quits, so the game never gets a moment to ASK for anything — every retention surface fires at someone already leaving. Our own funnel: **only 9.2% ever voluntarily started anything after their first run ended.** Surge ends FOR them, at tension, with a number, and "maybe I can do better" is a reason to press a button. User's call; the data backs it harder than expected.
- **New first session:** three labelled PRACTICE puzzles (`FIRST_RUN_PRACTICE`) → a pitch screen → a real Surge run.
- ⚠ **Each practice board introduces exactly ONE thing** — basics, then twins, then a gate. The funnel showed abandon roughly DOUBLING (20% → 32-39%) on any board that introduced a mechanic, so spreading them is the point. `twinScale`/`gates` are passed EXPLICITLY through `startPuzzle` opts rather than left to `TWIN_RAMP_START_LEVEL`/`ZEN_GATE_START_LEVEL`, which are tuned for a long ladder and would stack both onto one early board. `teachGate` asks for a gate that actually blocks (only ~20% of random placements do).
- **"Practice 1 of 3" in the HUD.** The retired warm-ups failed partly because they were an unbounded-feeling ramp; a visible finish line makes three boards a sequence with an end.
- 🐛 **Real bug caught by the suite: a tap during the 1s solve-reveal delay built a FOURTH practice puzzle.** `onSolve` leaves `inTransition` true and `advance()` was happy to run before the pitch appeared. Fixed with a `pitchPending` latch, checked first thing in `advance()`. The test's fake `setTimeout` never fires, which is exactly why it surfaced there and not in play.
- ⚠ **`isFirstRunAutoStart` now spans BOTH phases**, so every practice-only behaviour moved to `practiceStep`: the run-save skip (the Surge run IS a real chosen run and should be resumable), the HUD How-to button, the practice ladder. `isFirstSurgeRun` is cleared in `startGame` and re-set AFTER it returns, or a later menu-started Surge would inherit the Try Again ending.
- **Try Again** is an added primary button on the existing game-over card, not a new screen — that card already carries score, rank, save and the PotD hook. Shown only for the first Surge run when the score didn't rank (which includes the zero-solve case, the commonest first-run outcome); hidden the moment they DO place, so it never competes with the reward.
- **REMOVED the PotD nudge tooltip** (both the in-run and quit-to-menu copies). It interrupted play to advertise another mode and converted 17 of 404. The daily now lives on the game-over card where the player is already deciding what's next.
- **REMOVED the How-to-Solve action button** from the firstPlay tip (tutorial converts terribly: 78 opened, 8 finished; the animated demos do that teaching now). The HUD button stays for the practice phase only — once Surge starts the run is timed and that button sits in the timer's slot.
- **Four new counters** — practice solves (reuses `warmup_puzzles`, clamp 2→3), `reached_pitch`, `surge_started`, `retry_count`. ⚠ **`retry_count` is THE number**: the entire premise is that a run which ends makes a player want another go. If it stays near zero the premise is dead and we should know in a day rather than after another CG trial. A gap between reached_pitch and surge_started means the pitch copy is wrong.
- 5 new i18n keys × 15 languages. `onboarding-ladder-test.js` sections 2/3 rewritten for the practice sequence; the stale PotD-nudge staircase step retired with the tooltip.
- Version bumped 1.57 → 1.58.

## 2026-08-06 — Release v1.57 (one-time "why your score is gone" notice for the players who were ON the boards)
- A handful of real people had Surge entries. Having your run vanish with no explanation is a lousy way to treat the players who engaged most, so they get a one-time message when they next open the leaderboard.
- ⚠ **DETECTION HAD TO RUN BEFORE THE EVIDENCE ROTS**, which is the whole design constraint. Bumping `LEADERBOARD_ERA` doesn't DELETE the old keys, it just stops reading them — so they linger as proof this browser had scores. `initBoardResetNotice()` decides ONCE at init, persists `<slug>_lbResetNotice_v2` ('1' owed / '0' not), and never scans again. **Do not "tidy up" the pre-era keys** before returning players have had a chance to load this build.
- **Three shapes of evidence**, because one isn't enough: a pre-era `_own_rec_<type>` (only written after a SUCCESSFUL RANKED SERVER SAVE — exact); a non-empty pre-era `_lb_pending` (submitted offline, never confirmed); or the player's own `_lastPlayerName` appearing in a pre-era cached board (catches saves that predate recordings, or whose recording was dropped for quota).
- ⚠ **The load-bearing requirement is the NEGATIVE case.** Telling a brand-new player their nonexistent scores were deleted is worse than saying nothing. `tests/board-reset-notice-test.js` spends most of its assertions there — brand-new browser, played-but-never-ranked, cached-a-board-they-aren't-on, empty pending queue, **PotD-only player**, and new-era-only players all must NOT see it.
- **Best-effort by nature:** cleared storage or a different device can't be identified and simply won't show it. Right failure direction.
- **Rendered inline in the leaderboard panel**, not via the Tooltip system — tooltipCard is z-index 100 and the leaderboard overlay is 2000, so a tooltip would render BEHIND it. Shown on Surge boards only (PotD wasn't reset); persists across board tabs until explicitly dismissed.
- New i18n key `marathon.boardResetNotice`, 15/15 languages. Copy explains the change, owns the inconvenience, and invites them to take the top spot back.
- ⚠ **`tests/board-reset-notice-test.js` IS TEMPORARY** — retire it together with the notice markup and `hadPreEraScores` when the message is removed, rather than leaving a suite that asserts nothing.
- Version bumped 1.56 → 1.57.

## 2026-08-06 — Release v1.56 (Surge time REBALANCE + leaderboard era; step 1 of the practice→Surge onboarding)
- **Context:** two failed CG trials point at Zen being the wrong landing mode. ⚠ **Zen has no terminal state** — a run only ends when the player QUITS, so the game never gets a moment to make a pitch; every retention surface fires at someone already leaving (only 9.2% ever voluntarily started anything after the auto-start run). Surge ends *for* them, at tension, with a number. The plan is a 3-puzzle labelled Practice sequence → "Time to Surge" → Surge, with Try Again as the first-run ending. **This release is only the timing/leaderboard groundwork.**
- ⚠ **The old opening was granting 30 SECONDS for the first 4×4** (`sizeCap = 1.875 × 16`), against a measured ~100s that a first-timer actually takes. Modelling it (scratchpad `check-timing.js`, walks the real constants through the real arithmetic) showed **the old tuning handed a score of ZERO to every player slower than "competent"** — which matches the funnel's 70% who never reached puzzle 3. That's the bug.
- **`TIME_PER_TILE_SINGULAR` 1.875 → 5.0** (`[5.0, 5.5, 6.0, 6.5]`). First board now grants 80s. ⚠ **Above ~3.5 the value stops mattering to skilled players at all** — 3.5 and 5.0 both give 11/16/19 puzzles for competent/strong/expert, because once the cap stops binding the declining schedule governs and that's identical for everyone. So extra generosity is FREE past that point and buys exactly one thing: a first board a newcomer can finish. Failure modes are asymmetric — too tight = score 0 and leave; too loose = tension arrives on board 3.
- **Accepted cost:** top-end spread compresses (expert 27 → 19 puzzles), so the board separates strong from expert less sharply.
- ⚠ **I proposed 3.5 and the model said it wasn't enough** — it left the newcomer failing board 1. Went to 5.0. Worth remembering that the first model was ALSO wrong: it held solve time constant, which makes every run look endless. Solve time has to scale with tile count and path count or the numbers are meaningless.
- **New `MAX_BANK_SECONDS: 150`** + `bankTime()` in marathon.js — THE one place it's applied (the solve card projects the next clock too; if the two disagreed the popup would promise time the player doesn't get). Banking used to be explicitly unlimited, which is what the old size cap was really guarding against, indirectly, at the newcomer's expense. Now hoarding is capped directly.
- ⚠ **The ceiling is `max(cap, fresh)`, never a bare cap.** Quad skips the size cap and rides START_TIME_QUAD (180-380s), so `min(total, 150)` would grant 380s then immediately take back 230 — reads as a bug. The cap only ever bites carried-over SURPLUS.
