- **`LEADERBOARD_ERA: 'v2'`** now suffixes the local board / pending-submit / own-recording keys, retiring every cached client-side score. ⚠ **Bump it on any future balance change** — a score is only meaningful against runs played under the same clock. PotD keys deliberately DON'T carry it.
- ⚠ **`/api/admin/wipe-leaderboards` gained a `scope` param** (`marathon` | `potd` | `all`). Its default is still `all` for compatibility and **that default also wipes PotD's boards and sessions** — months of real player history that a Surge rebalance has nothing to do with. Always pass `{"scope":"marathon"}`. (PotD *streaks* live client-side in `<slug>_potd_streak_v1` and survive either way; the boards behind them do not.)
- Version bumped 1.55 → 1.56. **Back-end deploy needed** for the scope param before running the wipe.

## 2026-08-05 — Release v1.55 (version-only re-cut of v1.54)
- **No code change.** 1.54 had already been zipped when the version needed re-issuing, so this is 1.54's contents under a fresh number to bust the PWA cache. Everything real about this release is in the v1.54 entry below.
- Version bumped 1.54 → 1.55.

## 2026-08-05 — Release v1.54 (FIX: v1.53 still FLASHED the intro before skipping it)
- 🐛 **v1.53 skipped the disclaimer in intro.js — which runs at DOMContentLoaded, long after the overlay has painted.** A first-timer saw the gag flash by on the way to their first puzzle.
- ⚠ **ROOT CAUSE was load order, not logic: `config.js` was loaded LAST-ish**, first in the ordered `srcs` loader at the BOTTOM of the body, while `#introOverlay` markup sits at line ~110. So nothing that knows `PROJECT_SLUG` or `IS_CRAZYGAMES` existed before first paint, and the skip decision could not be made in time.
- **Fix: `config.js` is now loaded EARLY** via its own `document.write` tag (same dev cache-bust), immediately before a new inline block that adds `html.intro-skip`. CSS then keeps the overlay out of the first frame — `html.intro-skip #introOverlay { display: none !important }`, exactly the mechanism `html.mode-debug` already used. ⚠ **`config.js` must NOT be re-added to the `srcs` array** — a second load re-declares its consts and throws. It is still the first script after platform-redirect, so load order is otherwise unchanged.
- **`isFirstVisitBrowser()` + `FIRST_VISIT_AUTOSTART_KEY` moved to config.js**, because the pre-paint block needs them and config is the first file loaded. marathon.js's `isFirstVisit()` and its `AUTO_START_KEY` now defer to those (with inline fallbacks for a stale cached config.js). **One definition of "new player"**, which matters: marathon WRITES the key that config READS.
- **intro.js reads `html.intro-skip` rather than re-deriving the answer** — the DOM and the CSS then cannot disagree. A mismatch would mean a `display:none` overlay that intro.js still believes it must show, i.e. the game stuck behind an invisible screen.
- **Verified config.js is still worker-safe** (sw.js `importScripts` it): the new function only touches localStorage when CALLED, and it evaluates cleanly in a context with no window/document/localStorage. Keep it that way.
- **Lesson, same shape as v1.51's:** the logic was right and the *timing* was wrong. Anything that decides what the first frame looks like has to run before the first frame, which on this page means before the body's script loader.
- Version bumped 1.53 → 1.54. No back-end change.

## 2026-08-05 — Release v1.53 (first-time players skip the intro disclaimer everywhere, not just CG)
- **User call:** itch should give a newcomer the same landing CrazyGames does — arrive, and be playing. So the intro gag is now skipped for ANY first-time visitor on ANY host. Returning players still get it; it's a running joke, and by then it's their game rather than a stranger's toll gate.
- **No safety/legal cost:** the intro is a comedic liability disclaimer ("please do not solve while operating heavy machinery", modelled on Ridiculousness), not a photosensitivity or terms gate. Worth re-checking that premise if its copy ever changes.
- ⚠ **The CG skip deliberately drops the F11/PageUp/PageDown fullscreen shortcuts** (custom fullscreen controls are prohibited there; CG provides its own). A first-timer on itch must NOT lose them — so `registerFullscreenShortcut()` is hoisted out of the intro's own setup and called on the skip path for everyone EXCEPT CG. It carries a bound-once guard: exactly one of the two paths calls it today, but that's an easy invariant to break.
- ⚠ **`Marathon.isFirstVisit()` is now the single source of truth** for "brand new browser" (the three-key test: consumed auto-start flag / saved mode pick / saved leaderboard name), extracted from `autoStartFirstPractice` and exported for intro.js. **If these two ever disagreed** you'd get a player who skipped the intro but landed on the menu, or one who watched the gag and was then dropped mid-puzzle. Keep them on the one predicate.
- localStorage unavailable (private mode) → NOT a first visit, deliberately: nothing can be consumed there, so every load would look brand new and the player would be locked into the newcomer path forever.
- Version bumped 1.52 → 1.53. No back-end change.

## 2026-08-05 — Release v1.52 (demo beats LOOP, and slower — plus the board freeze that made that safe)
- **User call: the beat was over before the caption had been read.** Now ~2× slower and it loops until the tip is dismissed. New constants in tutorial.js: lead-in 700, step 900, slow step 1300, hold 1800, loop gap 900 — a twin cycle is ~9s, a gate cycle ~7s. Deliberately slower than the modal's, which is user-paced with Next and watched on purpose; this one plays in the corner of the eye.
- ⚠ **Looping changes the safety story, which is the real content of this release.** v1.50 played ONCE precisely because the demo has the singleton Maze on loan and game input is suppressed for the duration. Looping means that loan now lasts as long as the card does. A normal-looking board that silently ignores taps reads as BROKEN — which is nearly what the v1.51 bug looked like — so the freeze had to become visible.
- **`Render.setBoardInert(on)`** → `#maze.maze-demo-inert { opacity: .3; pointer-events: none }`. Resolves through `boardCanvas()` so it works from inside tutorial mode. ⚠ Declared BEFORE `.maze-spin-hold` in styles.css ON PURPOSE: equal specificity, so source order lets the spin's `opacity: 0` win if they ever overlap.
- **Widened the four existing `Tutorial.isOpen()` input guards to `isBusy()`** (game.js undo / resetPuzzle / board-penalty, marathon.js lock-tip timer). They all exist to stop something poking the swapped grid, and a beat swaps it exactly like the modal does. `pointer-events: none` covers the canvas; these cover keyboard and gamepad, which never touch it.
- **Loop mechanics:** `for(;;)` with a `beatAlive(token)` re-check after EVERY await — each one is a point where the player could have dismissed. Rewind is `installDemoPuzzle()` + `fastForward(idx)`, i.e. reload-and-replay rather than undoing actions one at a time, so it cannot drift over hundreds of cycles. There is no natural exit; every exit is `stopBeat()` flipping the token.
- Version bumped 1.51 → 1.52. No back-end change.

## 2026-08-05 — Release v1.51 (FIX: v1.50's demo beat could leave the board invisible)
- 🐛 **Symptom: dismiss the twin-tile tooltip and no puzzle appears.** Board gone, taps do nothing.
- ⚠ **ROOT CAUSE — a latent render.js hazard that the beats merely exposed.** `spinInBoard` schedules a timer that reads the MODULE-LEVEL `canvas` **when it fires**, not when it was scheduled. A demo beat starts at puzzle-ready, which is exactly when that timer is pending; `Render.beginTutorial` repoints `canvas` at the tooltip canvas; the timer fires and strips `.maze-spin-hold` from THAT element. `#maze.maze-spin-hold` is `opacity: 0; pointer-events: none` — so the real board stayed hidden and unclickable, permanently. Dismissing the tip couldn't help: the class is on the element, not in any state the tooltip owns.
- **Fix: `Render.boardCanvas()`** — returns `tutSaved.canvas` while `tutorialMode`, else `canvas`, i.e. always the REAL board. Every class toggle and ghost measurement in the spin + penalty code now routes through it (15 sites). **The rule this encodes: any code that HIDES the board and undoes it later must never read the bare `canvas`** — it can be a borrowed tutorial/demo canvas, and decorating the wrong element means the hide is never lifted. `spinOutBoard` also now takes its ghost's PIXELS and geometry from the same element (they could previously disagree).
- **The modal tutorial was always exposed to this too**, just never hit: opening it from the menu means no spin is pending, and the in-HUD button only exists on the auto-start run.
- **Second fix, defence + UX:** the mechanic tips are now HELD until the spin-in finishes (`spinDelayMs + 80`, with a level guard in case the player solved during the wait). Nothing to gain from racing the transition, and a demo competing with the board tumbling in reads as clutter.
- **Lesson for the next beat-shaped feature:** the hazard wasn't in the new code — it was in what the new code made *reachable*. Borrowing a singleton (the renderer's canvas, the Maze) surfaces every place that captured it implicitly. Grep for bare `canvas.` / `Maze.` in deferred callbacks before adding another borrower.
- Version bumped 1.50 → 1.51. No back-end change.

## 2026-08-05 — Release v1.50 (twin + gate tips SHOW the mechanic instead of describing it)
- **Why:** CrazyGames' quality guidance is "prioritize visuals and limit the use of text for onboarding… avoid explaining every single feature". The text tooltips added 08-02 did not dent the 32-39% abandon spike at the exact puzzles they explain. A card with two sentences asks the player to stop and read at the moment they hit something unfamiliar; it never SHOWS the mechanic.
- **How:** a "beat" = ONE step of the How-to-Solve walkthrough, auto-played on a small canvas inside the tooltip card. `Tutorial.playBeat(canvas, key)` — `BEATS = { twinTiles: 2, gates: 6 }` are indices into the existing `STEPS`. **Reuses the modal's hand-authored board wholesale** (it already contains the twin pair and the gates): replay earlier steps' actions instantly via `fastForward`, then animate the one step that matters. No second puzzle to author or keep in sync.
- ⚠ **THE INVARIANT: the demo board is installed in the SINGLETON Maze.** `snapshotLive` / `restoreLive` / `installDemoPuzzle` are now shared by the modal and the beats precisely so the two can't drift — a mismatch means a player's real board is silently replaced by the demo. Every exit path routes through `stopBeat()`, which is idempotent and restores Maze BEFORE `Render.endTutorial()`.
- ⚠ **Three consequences that each needed their own guard**, all because the tooltip card deliberately does NOT block the play area (it's designed so you can keep solving under it):
  - **Game input** — `handlePointer` and `startPress` no-op while `Tutorial.isBeatPlaying()`. Without this a tap during the ~3s demo lands on a board that's about to be thrown away and the twist silently vanishes.
  - **Persistence** — `marathon.saveRunState` and `potd.saveCurrentAttempt` bail on `Tutorial.isBusy()`. Snapshotting mid-demo would store the DEMO as the player's saved run. **This latent bug already existed for the modal** and was only masked by two accidents (the in-HUD How-to button only exists on the auto-start run, which doesn't save; menu-opened tutorials aren't in PLAYING state). Beats fire on every run, so it had to be closed properly.
  - **Page hide** — both modules' handlers now call `stopBeat()` FIRST, then save. The `isBusy` guard is the belt to that brace: without the stop it would skip the checkpoint entirely.
- **Plays ONCE (~3s), then hands the board straight back**; the canvas keeps the final frame as a still. Deliberately not looped — the live Maze is on loan for the duration, so the loan has to be short. `beatToken` strands any `await` that resumes after a cancel.
- **The sentence stays as a caption under the animation.** The guidance is to LIMIT text, not remove it, and it keeps the fifteen existing translations doing useful work.
- `tooltip-test.js` gained a beats section. The assertion that matters is **custody**: every start is matched by a stop on all three dismissal paths (Got It / solve / navigate-away), because a missed stop means the player's board never comes back.
- ⚠ **NOT verified in a browser** (universal rule 0). Syntax, wiring and custody are covered; the *animation* — canvas sizing, whether a 6×7 board reads at ~10rem tall, timing — needs eyes. `#tooltipDemoCanvas` height is `clamp(8rem, 26vh, 13rem)` and `layoutTutorial` fits-and-centres, so a wrong aspect letterboxes rather than distorting; tune the height in styles.css.
- Version bumped 1.49 → 1.50. No back-end change.

## 2026-08-05 — Release v1.49 (warm-up puzzles REMOVED; ladder back to a contiguous 1,2,3…)
- **The warm-ups lasted three days.** Added 08-02 on the theory that the opening was too hard; removed 08-05 because the data says it never was. 86.5% of new players solve at least one puzzle, and of those who never reached three real ones, **81% had already solved something before leaving**. Not a difficulty problem.
- **What they actually cost:** per-puzzle abandon was a flat 19.3% / 20.2% / 21.3% across the 3×3, the 4×3 and the 4×4 — three boards differing in nothing but size. They added two more near-identical boards to the front of a run that loses ~20% per near-identical board, spending a newcomer's most valuable twenty seconds on puzzles with nothing to discover. CrazyGames' quality guidance says this in as many words ("no overly repetitive or boring tasks", "balanced and well-paced").
- ⚠ **The ANALYTICS survives them, deliberately.** `first_run_stats.warmup_puzzles`, the sync field, and the admin histogram's `-2` / `-1` buckets are all KEPT: they hold the 223 players who solved a warm-up and left — the finding that reframed the problem from difficulty to reward — and old cached clients are still reporting into them. They just stop growing. Don't clean them up. tracking.js still SENDS `warmupPuzzles` (always 0 now) so a browser mid-run on v1.45-1.48 can't have its count regressed.
- **Reverted in marathon.js:** the negative level numbers, `levelAfter` (the stepper that skipped puzzle 0), `displayLevel`, `warmupCount`, `openingLevelSeed`, `isWarmupLevel`, and the warm-up branches in `levelConfig` / `dimsForLevel`. `level` is 1-based and contiguous again and the player sees `level` directly.
- **The staircase is unchanged and still spaced:** twins L3, gates L4 (`ZEN_GATE_START_LEVEL`), PotD nudge L5, lock tip L6. With warm-ups gone these are also the displayed numbers.
- **`tests/warmup-ladder-test.js` → `tests/onboarding-ladder-test.js`.** The warm-up sections went; the staircase-ordering and returning-browser sections stayed, and it gained a check that the ladder **holds flat across a tier boundary** (TIER_DROP cancelling the new level's growth — "same board, one more colour"). That behaviour was documented but untested, and walking to puzzle 5 now crosses it.
- Version bumped 1.48 → 1.49. No back-end change; the v1.48 back-end deploy still covers this.

## 2026-08-05 — Release v1.48 (STUCK-OR-BORED instrumentation; ladder locked to 'standard')
- ⚠ **DEPLOY BACK-END FIRST**, then this zip, then the admin page (separate repo). Adds `first_run_stats.abandon_secs`.
- **CG's second trial came back 7/15 and the decision is made**; the visibility-boosted traffic (200-425/day since 08-03) stops with it. This release is about capturing one measurement before the window closes — and, more durably, having the instrument in place for itch's organic traffic and the regulars.
- **The question it answers:** when a first session ends, had the player just SOLVED something and shrugged, or were they stuck on a board they never finished? `initial_run_puzzles` cannot tell those apart — someone who gave up four minutes into puzzle 3 and someone who solved puzzle 2 and closed the tab are both "2 puzzles". The whole difficulty-vs-reward question turns on which dominates.
- **How:** `abandon_secs` = seconds on the board the player walked away from, 0 if their last act was a solve. Set on pagehide/visibilitychange while a first-run puzzle is live and unsolved (marathon.js `reportStallOnHide`), cleared by every solve. Auto-start run only. Capped at 600s so an idle background tab can't report an hour.
- ⚠ **It is the ONE last-write-wins column on that row.** Every other field merges max()/OR. A max() merge would preserve a stall from a board the player went on to SOLVE, which inverts the meaning. The back-end suite asserts a smaller value must win, specifically so a future "make the merge consistent" cleanup fails loudly. Absent from an older client's payload → left alone entirely.
- **Deliberately NOT part of the no-content guard:** standing in an unsolved puzzle without touching a tile is exactly the visitor that guard exists to exclude. Asserted.
- **Syncs immediately on pagehide** via a new `_frFlushSync` (the 400ms debounce would never fire on a dying page); `_frSyncNow` already uses keepalive.
- **Admin histogram gains a "% stuck (Ns)" annotation per bucket.** Its denominator is rows created on/after 2026-08-05 — NOT the bar height — because older rows sit at 0, which would read as "left satisfied" without our knowing that. Don't "simplify" that denominator.
- ⚠ **Ladder experiment LOCKED to 'standard'** (`FIRST_RUN_VARIANT_FORCE`). Not because standard won — at 53 engaged per arm nothing could, and every behavioural column in that table came down to 1-7 players. Locking cleans up FUTURE measurement: on organic traffic a four-way split will never reach significance and dilutes everything else. All four ladders are identical through puzzle 3, where ~70% of players are lost, so the arm barely touches the population that matters.
- **What the funnel said, for the record** (725 new players, 82% of them post-08-02 build): 86.5% solve at least one puzzle; only 29.5% reach three real ones. Per-puzzle abandon is a flat ~20% on the 3×3 / 4×3 / 4×4 (three boards differing only in size), then jumps to 32-39% across real puzzles 3-6 — exactly the twins / gates / 2-paths introductions — then falls back. Reads as a ~20% baseline disinterest problem PLUS a ~15pp friction problem at each new mechanic. Not a "can they solve it" problem.
- Version bumped 1.47 → 1.48.

## 2026-08-02 — Release v1.47 (Marathon → "⚡ Surge"; tap/click wording follows the device)
- **Mode renamed to "⚡ Surge" (display only).** User call after asking for alternatives: "Marathon" described LENGTH when the mode is really about the draining clock — and it read as a near-synonym of Zen, the mode that actually runs forever. Zen ↔ Surge reads as a deliberate pair (calm vs charged) and is on-theme for a game about completing circuits.
- ⚠ **The identifier `marathon` did NOT change and must not.** It is load-bearing in `<slug>_run_save_marathon_v1` save keys, the `mode` value on tracking rows and score submissions, leaderboard routing, and the whole `marathon.*` i18n namespace — renaming it would orphan every saved run and split the stats history at the rename. ONLY `mode.marathon.name` changed. The name-split is documented at the top of `marathon.js` and beside the key in `i18n.js`, because "the file is marathon.js but the mode is Surge" is exactly what confuses a later session.
- **Surge is kept ENGLISH in all 15 languages, like Zen** — a change of policy as well as of name: it used to be translated (Maratón / マラソン / Марафон / …). Treated as a proper noun for the mode.
- **New `I18n.tDevice(key)` — pointer-aware strings.** Any key may carry an OPTIONAL `<key>.desktop` sibling; the base string is the TOUCH wording and the sibling wins on a fine-pointer device. `applyTranslations` routes through it, so anything marked `data-i18n` gets it automatically with no markup change. Five strings converted: `tooltip.gates` (the one the user caught), `tutorial.step1`, `tutorial.step7`, `game.youWon`, `marathon.clickToContinue`.
- ⚠ **Why a separate string and NOT a `{tap}` token:** in several languages the verb drags grammar with it. Spanish "Toca una pieza" → "Haz clic **en** una pieza" (the preposition moves); German "Tippe **auf** störende Tore" → "Klicke störende Tore **an**" (separable prefix, lands at the far end of the sentence). A token could only ever be right for the intransitive cases. Don't "simplify" this later.
- ⚠ **`tDevice` looks the sibling up in the CURRENT language's dict directly, bypassing `t()`'s fall-back-to-English.** zh / ru / ar use ONE verb for both gestures (点击 / нажмите / انقر) and deliberately have no sibling — going through `t()` would have served those players the English desktop sentence instead of their own perfectly good base string. Verified explicitly.
- **Deliberately left alone:** "press and hold" (correct on both input types), and the phone-only strings — `redirect.*` (only fires on phones), `intro.fullscreenHint.ios/.android`, `marathon.tapKeyboard` (iOS standalone keyboard).
- Version bumped 1.46 → 1.47. (1.46 was already zipped before this landed.)

## 2026-08-02 — Release v1.46 (warm-up solves are TRACKED — admin depth histogram splits its 0 bucket)
- ⚠ **DEPLOY BACK-END FIRST**, then this zip. Adds a `first_run_stats.warmup_puzzles` column; a client shipped ahead of the server just has its `warmupPuzzles` field ignored (harmless), and the admin chart stays flat until the column exists.
- **Why it exists:** v1.45's two warm-up puzzles are excluded from `initial_run_puzzles`, so a player who solved the 3×3 and quit was indistinguishable in the funnel from one who solved nothing. Both landed in the histogram's `0` bucket. Now the 0 bucket splits three ways — `0` / `-2` / `-1` — keyed on a SEPARATE column.
- ⚠ **`warmup_puzzles` must never merge into `initial_run_puzzles`.** That column drives the admin panel's "3+ puzzles" column, its avg-puzzles figure AND the server-side variant balancer; folding warm-ups in would shift the ladder experiment's numbers for a reason unrelated to the ladders. The back-end test asserts the non-leak explicitly.
- **Bucket keys `-2` / `-1` are LAST-PUZZLE-REACHED, not counts** — the only keys in that dict that aren't. The admin page therefore sorts by PROGRESSION (`0 → -2 → -1 → 1 → …`), since a numeric sort files the negatives ahead of "finished nothing", and labels them "warm-up −2 (3×3)" rather than the nonsensical "−2 puzzles".
- **`bounced` / `engaged` deliberately still ignore warm-ups**, so a warm-up-only player is still counted as bounced in the variants table. The bar exists to make the ladder comparison meaningful and the warm-ups are identical across all four arms. The distribution is where that player becomes visible as something other than a flat zero. Flagged to the user as a live option to change.
- Rows predating v1.45 sit at `warmup_puzzles = 0` and stay in the `0` bucket — accurate for them, they had no warm-ups to solve.
- 🐛 **Found while extending it: `tests/first-run-guard-test.py` had been exiting 0 UNCONDITIONALLY.** Its summary and `return 1 if failures` sat at the end of `prune_cases`, whose return value `main()` discarded → `main()` returned None → `sys.exit(None)` → 0. It printed FAIL lines while reporting success to anything scripting it. Summary moved into `main()`; verified a deliberately broken check now exits 1. **That suite's green was meaningless until now** (it does pass genuinely). Same trap applies to any future Python suite — keep the summary in `main()`.
- Version bumped 1.45 → 1.46. (1.45 was already zipped before this change landed.)

## 2026-08-02 — Release v1.45 (first-visit ONBOARDING: two warm-up puzzles + a one-thing-per-puzzle tip staircase)
- **Two extra opening puzzles for brand-new players only** (`MARATHON.FIRST_RUN_WARMUP_LEVELS`): a 3×3 then a 3-row×4-col, before the 4×4 every other run starts on. Auto-start run only — menu-started and resumed runs are untouched.
- ⚠ **They are numbered -2 and -1, and THERE IS NO PUZZLE 0.** The ladder runs `-2 → -1 → 1 → 2 …` via marathon.js `levelAfter`, which every level walk must go through — including pre-gen's lookahead, which used `level + i` and would otherwise pre-build a phantom puzzle 0. The negative numbering is the whole mechanism: it's what lets the twin ramp, the gate schedule and the funnel each tell a warm-up from a real puzzle without a separate flag.
- **The player never sees a negative number** — `displayLevel()` counts the warm-ups into an ordinary 1, 2, 3…, so a first-timer's real puzzle 1 reads as "Puzzle 3". First attempt shipped a "Practice ·" label instead; the user's call was that these aren't practice puzzles from the player's side, just puzzles. Two i18n keys were added and then removed again in the same session.
- **Warm-ups roll NO growth-sequence entries.** The growth accumulator is anchored to the 4×4 start, so a warm-up that consumed a step would shift the size of every later puzzle in the run. Test asserts puzzle 1 is still exactly 4×4.
- **Excluded from `initial_run_puzzles`** (tracking.js `firstRunPuzzleSolved` skipped below level 1) so the admin panel's "3+ puzzles" column and the ladder-variant balancer still count REAL puzzles. They DO count toward `solvedCount` (the "N solved · keep going!" line, the per-visit solve tally) — deliberate: they are solves.
- 🐛 **The tooltip bug the user actually reported: tips came back on every puzzle.** `onSolve` dismissed with `markAsSeen=false`, which is the NAVIGATE-AWAY semantics (deliberately re-queues). New `Tooltip.dismissSolved()`: solving under a tip counts as acknowledging it — seen-flag persists, `onAcknowledge` fires, gone. Tips merely QUEUED behind it are dropped **unseen**, so they get their own turn next puzzle rather than popping over the solve card. `onAcknowledge` firing on this path matters beyond bookkeeping: it's what reveals the in-HUD How-to-Solve button.
- **Two new tips**, `twinTiles` and `gates`, fired by `Tooltip.showBoardMechanicTips()` from every puzzle-ready path (Zen start + resume, PotD start + resume). Keyed to what the BOARD contains (new `Maze.hasTwins()`, `Gates.list.length`), not to a level — each mode introduces them on a different schedule. Suppressed after the tutorial like `lockTile`, since its steps 3 and 7 teach them.
- ⚠ **ONBOARDING STAIRCASE — one new thing per puzzle, and they must not collide.** Now a documented block in config.js: L3 twins → L4 gates (`ZEN_GATE_START_LEVEL`) → L5 PotD nudge (still a literal in marathon.js) → L6 lock tip (`LOCK_TIP_MIN_LEVEL`). **This took three rounds of playtesting to settle** because the lock tip is the only one on a 30s TIMER rather than a puzzle boundary, so it kept landing on top of whatever the boundary had just put up — first blamed on the gate/nudge collision, actually the lock tip both times. Gates moved 3 → 5 → back to 3 → 4 along the way. `warmup-ladder-test.js` now asserts the four levels stay strictly increasing with the lock tip last.
- **The first gate a player ever sees now genuinely blocks the route** (`assignGates`' new 7th arg `requireBlocking`, passed by game.js on the run's first gated board only). Both the vertex and the starting `delta` were random, and the new suite measures the damage: **only ~20% of ordinary boards opened with the route actually severed** — the other 80% let the player solve past a decorative gate seconds after the tip explaining gates had fired. Placement now prefers a vertex with a `blockDirs` entry and aims `delta` at it; can't come out 0, since `solutionDir` is never on the route. Best-effort — falls back to random when no vertex can reach the route.
- **Gate-free Zen opening re-keyed from solve count to LEVEL.** A solve-count test would have spent the allowance on the warm-ups and put gates on real puzzle 1. Needed a new `Marathon.getLevel()`.
- **Two new test suites** (`tooltip-test.js`, `gates-test.js` — gate behavior had never been covered) plus `warmup-ladder-test.js`, which loads config.js + marathon.js behind a Proxy DOM and drives a real run through `autoStartFirstPractice`/`onSolve`/`advance`. All three mutation-checked. **Harness gotcha worth remembering: the fake `setTimeout` must return a NON-ZERO id** — marathon.js tests its timer handles with `!== null`, so 0 reads as "pending forever" and the solve→advance flow stalls.
- Version bumped 1.44 → 1.45.

## 2026-08-02 — Release v1.44 (MOSAIC tiles — the general case of quad; DEV-PANEL ONLY)
- **New root file `mosaic.js`** (loads BEFORE maze.js — page script list, `sw.js` precache, and `maze-worker.js` importScripts all updated). maze.js's mosaic branch calls into it at build time, so a zip missing it breaks the whole game at load, not just the new mode.
- **Origin: the user's own 30-year-old DOS idea**, from two pencil renderings — pieces of many shapes (plus signs, rings, solid squares, pinwheels) that each occupy the same cells after a quarter turn. The debug panel's Quad **checkbox is now a Singular/Quad/Mosaic radio**. Named "Mosaic" by the user from a shortlist.
- ⚠ **The geometry constrains piece SIZES and this is not negotiable**: every cell except a dead-centre one sits in an orbit of 4 under a quarter turn, so a piece has 4k or 4k+1 cells. Sizes **2, 3, 6, 7, 10 cannot exist**. The user's set is {4,5,8,9,12,13,16}. Shapes are ENUMERATED from frame orbits, never hand-listed, so the library can't drift out of sync with the rule — 27 shapes, covering exactly the sketches' vocabulary.
- **Quad was deliberately LEFT ALONE** rather than reimplemented on top of the general machinery: it's load-bearing for Marathon, PotD and every stored recording, and its 2×2 fast paths are correct. Mosaic runs alongside. **Singular fill is modelled as one-cell groups**, which is what keeps rotation/locking/hint/scramble to a single code path.
- **Why this was cheap to add at all**: quad's flow already builds the maze at sub-tile resolution and groups AFTERWARDS. Mosaic slots into the same seam — **zero generation code changed**.
- **Mode flags are mutually exclusive, enforced in maze.js**: `setQuadMode` clears mosaic and `setMosaicMode(true)` clears quad. Chosen over patching the 14 existing `setQuadMode` call sites. **Ordering contract: set quad FIRST, mosaic SECOND** (potd-gen2's three sites and maze-worker all do).
- **Piece interiors are a CSP, not a repair pass.** Quad's "convert the offender to an outward-facing elbow" assumes every sub-tile has 2 exterior sides; the centre of a 3×3 solid has none, so repair doesn't generalise. **~5% of placements are genuinely unsatisfiable** — a filler pocket walled in by 3 path tiles that don't point at it, leaving <2 free sides, and no tile has fewer than 2 ports. Those pieces **dissolve** back into singular cells (the WHOLE piece — removing one cell destroys the rotation invariance of the rest). Debug readout reports the count; ~1/board with ~16 kept.
- 🐛 **Three bugs found in play, all from the same shape of mistake — a contract expressed as something mosaic could silently fall off:**
  - **Reflex-corner bevels.** The bevel-skip model is per-EDGE, which is complete only for convex groups — a 2×2 quad has no concave corners, so this could never have shown up before. Where a mosaic outline turns concavely the two edges belong to two DIFFERENT cells and each mitres against its own skipped edge, leaving an unbevelled square in the third. **24 of 27 shapes have them; 8–56 per board.** Fixed with `drawMosaicCornerPatches`; ownership rule is "the cell with both sides inner and the diagonal missing", which claims each corner exactly once.
  - **Dead hints.** A piece can sit at a non-zero offset yet already present its solved ports (a one-cell group holding a straight at offset 2, a cross at any offset, a symmetric piece). **~15% of hints were wasted.** Singular and quad both already had this guard — singular's comment even records a player reporting the identical thing about a CROSS. **The same flaw was in the move floor**, over-charging every symmetric piece; `mosaicGroupMinTurns` now walks all four offsets, so the floor is exact rather than approximate.
  - **Undo/reset dissolved the whole grid into singular tiles.** game.js's `cloneMazeSnap` / `deepCloneSnapshot` were hand-written field lists that dropped `mosaic`; `loadSnapshot` reads absent as "clear it". **The test suite missed this entirely because it clones with `JSON.stringify`, which preserves what a field list forgets** — that's the more useful lesson than the bug. Both cloners now share `cloneSnapshotCore`, based on `Object.assign({}, s)`, so a future forgotten field is shared-by-reference rather than lost. `deepCloneSnapshot` still drops `locked` on purpose (a reset starts un-hinted even on a resumed PotD board) — previously implicit, now explicit.
  - Also fixed alongside: mosaic `hint()` returned a bare `true` instead of `{r,c,turns}`, which cost hints their animation AND wrote undefined coords into the move recording. **Mosaic replays recorded before v1.44 are corrupt** — dev-panel only, nothing server-side.
- **Deliberate gaps, so nobody hunts for them**: twins are NOT assigned in mosaic (the twin sliders do nothing there); gates can land on an edge interior to a piece (safe — `assignGates` avoids solution edges — just visually odd); bevel stays 1× since no single multiplier suits a 2×2 and a 5×5 at once; mosaic is absent from Marathon, PotD, the mode picker and i18n, so **no translation work was needed**.
- New suite `tests/mosaic-test.js` (README documents all four sections). Every new check was verified to FAIL with its fix reverted — a check that passes either way is worthless, which is exactly how the cloner bug slipped through. **Maintenance coupling**: section 4 greps game.js for `cloneSnapshotCore`, so reshaping that function needs the matcher updated with it.
- Version bumped 1.43 → 1.44.

## 2026-08-02 — Release v1.43 (PotD solve times are now SERVER-measured; resume hardened + widened)
- ⚠ **DEPLOY ORDER: back-end FIRST**, then this zip. Adds `POST /api/potd/solve` + two `potd_sessions` columns, and raises the server-side PotD caps. A client shipped ahead of the server pings a 404 (falls back safely, no lost solve) but also offers 24h resumes the 6h server would reject — that rejection is a silent dropped 4xx.
- **Origin: sister couldn't resume a PotD she'd left mid-solve.** Investigating found the resume feature working as designed, and three *other* reasons she might have lost it — which is what the rest of this entry is.
- **Per-move checkpointing** (potd.js `scheduleAttemptSave`, hooked into game.js `appendMove`). The pagehide/visibilitychange saves are not guaranteed — a tab crash, OOM kill, or hard force-quit skips both and nothing was ever written. Now every committed action checkpoints, throttled to one write per 1.5s. Hooked at `appendMove`, NOT `recordMove`, so **undo** checkpoints too (it appends directly; a resumed board that ignored undos would desync from its own recording). Always deferred to a timer, never synchronous — `undo()`/`resetPuzzle()` append while the board is mid-restore, so a same-tick save could snapshot a stale grid. `clearResume` cancels any pending timer or a queued write resurrects a save that solve/watch just deleted.
- 🐛 **The 6h resume TTL was never the binding cap — `MAX_TOTAL_MS` (1h) was.** PotD's clock is wall-clock, so any resume >1h after the original start produced `timeMs > 1h` → `400 timeMs out of range` → dropped as non-transient. The player solved it and simply never appeared on the board, with nothing in any log naming the cause. Split `POTD_MAX_TIME_MS` / `POTD_MAX_SESSION_MS` (24h each) away from Marathon's constants, which are untouched (its countdown is a resource, not a score).
- **Solve times are now measured by the server** (user's idea, and the right one). `POST /api/potd/solve` fires the instant the board connects; the server stamps `solved_at`, computes `time_ms` from its own `started_at`, and `/potd/submit` ranks THAT — the client's claimed time is no longer read. Why a separate endpoint: the submit arrives *after* the player types a name, so timing the submit would put keyboard time on the score. Ping is idempotent (a retry reads back the first stamp; re-measuring would let a caller shop for a better time). Fired before the win-reveal delay and collected after the credits/music/share work, so the round trip hides inside animation the player is already watching.
- **Consequence worth remembering: `POTD_MAX_SESSION_MS` is no longer anti-cheat.** It existed to bound "study the board for hours with a patched client, then claim 30 seconds" — structurally impossible now that the clock runs from `/start` regardless. It's a GC bound. `POTD_MAX_TIME_MS` + `SESSION_TIMING_SLACK` + the timing-mismatch branch are **legacy-path only** (cached clients that never ping); delete them when old PWA caches age out.
- **Known cost, accepted**: server timing includes two one-way latencies the HUD never counted, so the solve modal can read a hair above the last HUD value, varying with connection. Sub-second. **Does NOT stop a real solver** — genuine elapsed time is genuine, so a bot that truly solves in 3s earns 3s; that needs move-recording plausibility checks, not timing.
- **Still-live limits on resume** (neither changed): the UTC-day prune (`pruneOldResumes`) is the real ceiling regardless of any TTL, and localStorage is per-browser, so phone→laptop never resumes.
- New suite `tests/potd-timing-test.py` — headline check pings a 2h-old session then submits **claiming 5 seconds**, asserts the board recorded 2h. Also guards the front-end/server coupling (`RESUME_TOKEN_TTL_MS` ≤ `POTD_MAX_SESSION_MS`) via regex on potd.js — reshape that declaration and the regex needs updating.
- `fmtTime`/`fmtTimePrecise` grew an hours field. Previously unreachable (1h server cap); a 3h entry would have rendered `185:07`.
- Version bumped 1.42 → 1.43.

## 2026-08-02 — Release v1.42 (CG first-click jump was the HUD, not the music chip; wipe buttons deleted)
- 🐛 **The CG "board jumps on first click" bug was `hudReserve`, NOT the Now Playing chip.** Settled by a temporary layout trace (since removed) rather than by reading code — I'd guessed wrong twice before instrumenting. The numbers either side of the click: `chip=expected npH=51 hudReserve=0 bottomReserve=32` → `chip=visible npH=51 hudReserve=17 bottomReserve=32`. **npH identical (51) on both sides** — the v1.36 predictive chip reserve had been working correctly the whole time. `marginTop = hudReserve - bottomReserve`, so 0→17 is exactly the 17px the board dropped.
- **Why the canvas was sized with a hidden HUD**: game.js's `newPuzzle` only calls `Render.refit()` when the DIMENSIONS change, and the first-visit auto-start builds its 4×4 at the dims `Render.init()` already set → no refit → the load-time sizing (taken before `showOnly(hudEl)`) stuck. Nothing corrected it until an unrelated resize came along; on CG that was the first click (music starts → chip appears → music.js calls `Render.resize()`). **The chip was a red herring — it just happened to trigger the resize.** Also explains the other half of the report: a board reserving 0 for the HUD sits 17px too high, which is why the top terminal hid under HOW TO SOLVE.
- **Fix — whoever flips an element the canvas measures owes it a re-layout** (the contract music.js already honoured for the chip): `marathon.js` `showOnly` and `potd.js` `showHud`/`hideHud`/`showMenu` now call `Render.resize()` when the HUD's visibility actually CHANGES (guarded on change, no redundant resizes). PotD needed it independently — `startPuzzle` refits BEFORE `showHud`, so every PotD board was carrying a HUD-less sizing too; same bug, but it never announced itself with a jump. Verified on CG: the correction now lands at LOAD (hudReserve 0 → 16 in the trace, before any click), leaving only a 1px 16→17 on first click (the RESET/UNDO buttons enabling nudges the HUD's measured height).
- ⚠ **The trap is still live for anything else**: `newPuzzle` refits on dimension change only, so any FUTURE element that alters the canvas's available space without changing puzzle dims will strand a stale sizing the same way. The HUD and the chip are the two known cases and both now notify.
- **Flicker fix (separate mechanism)**: assigning `canvas.width`/`height` RESETS the drawing surface — the spec clears the bitmap on ANY assignment, even to the value it already holds. Most `resize()` calls don't change pixel dims at all (a visibility flip only moves marginTop; the reserves either side often round to the same cellSize), so the board was being wiped and repainted for nothing — visible as a wink-out. `resize()` now only reassigns when the size genuinely changed; `drawCore`'s own clearRect still repaints, so skipping the reset leaves the previous correct pixels up instead of a hole.
- **All four wipe/reset buttons DELETED from the client** (user call — hiding them still shipped the REST addresses in the bundle for anyone to read). Gone: the `#dbgDevActions` markup + all four handlers, plus the now-dead helpers they were the only callers of — `Potd.devReset` + `resetLocalState` (this one held the live `/potd/dev-reset` fetch, the leak that wasn't in index.html), `Marathon.wipeLocalLeaderboards`, `Tracking.firstRunDevReset`, `DevMode.revealDevActions`. Grep for `admin/wipe` / `potd/dev-reset` across index.html + all JS now returns nothing.
- **The server endpoints all still exist** — call them by hand: `Invoke-RestMethod -Method Post "$api/admin/wipe-first-run"` (same for `wipe-leaderboards`, `wipe-visits`, `potd/dev-reset`). ⚠ The LOCAL halves are gone with the buttons: `wipe-leaderboards` no longer clears cached boards from your browser and `potd/dev-reset` no longer clears local PotD state, so hard-refresh / clear storage yourself afterwards.
- **Dev mode: `?dev=true` and Ctrl+D-at-the-intro are now equivalent.** They only differed to gate those buttons; with them deleted the distinction is gone (header comment updated so it doesn't describe a split that no longer exists).
- Version bumped 1.41 → 1.42.

## 2026-08-02 — Release v1.41 (first-run solve count was never being SENT; dev-mode gets an intro door)
- ⚠ **DEPLOY ORDER: back-end FIRST** (adds `POST /api/admin/wipe-first-run`), then this zip. The new Wipe First-Run Stats button 404s until the endpoint is live; nothing else in this release needs the server.
- 🐛 **First-run tracking was silently broken — `initial_run_puzzles` stuck at 0 for every row written during the window.** `marathon.js` calls `firstRunPuzzleSolved()` on each first-run solve and it incremented `st.p` in localStorage correctly, but tracking.js's `/first-run/sync` payload **omitted `puzzles`** (the key app.py reads). Server did `max(existing, 0)` → column never moved. Regression came in when the payload was restructured for `engaged` (v1.38). Found because a real 4-puzzle CG run left the Standard arm's 3+ column sitting at 1.
- **Blast radius is wider than the 3+ column** — the same field drives the engaged bar (`FIRST_RUN_ENGAGED_BAR = 3`), avg-puzzles, the 0-puzzle bucket, the completion histogram, AND v1.39's server-side variant balancer (which allocates by engaged count). So the balancer was running on frozen data, which is why Standard looks far worse than its siblings (1 of 42): v1.39 was routing new assignments there, so Standard absorbed nearly all the post-regression rows while Fast/Single/Extended kept their pre-regression counts. **Those numbers are a measurement artifact, not a result.**
- **Fixed**: `puzzles: st.p || 0` restored to the payload, plus `_frBackfillPuzzles()` — a ONE-TIME re-dirty (`pSync` marker) of any local state that already has solves, because browsers that had synced cleanly had `dirty` settled and would never have resent. Server `max()`es, so a late backfill only ever corrects upward.
- **Wipe First-Run Stats** (user call — a clean baseline beats a partly-repairable one): `POST /api/admin/wipe-first-run` empties `first_run_stats`, debug button also clears THIS browser's local state via new `Tracking.firstRunDevReset()` (both halves needed — otherwise the next sync re-creates this browser's row and re-seeds the table). NOT dry-run-able unlike prune-first-run: that one selects a subset so the preview is what makes it safe, this one unambiguously empties. ⚠ **Resets the ladder experiment** (arm assignments live in the same table; the balancer restarts from zero). Verified vs throwaway SQLite: 200 + row count, table emptied, **page_visits untouched**, idempotent, and a post-wipe sync with `puzzles: 4` stores 4 (the client fix's contract proven server-side).
- **Dev mode gets a second door**: Ctrl+D **while the intro overlay is on screen** now activates it — no URL edit. Window closes the moment the intro goes, so CG players (intro hidden during load + auto-start) and anyone already playing are locked out by construction; `IS_CRAZYGAMES` is refused outright too rather than resting on a race being unwinnable. Gate reads the overlay's COMPUTED DISPLAY on purpose: `intro-dismissed` isn't set on intro.js's debug bail, and `offsetParent` is null for position:fixed even when visible. Not persisted — a reload puts the intro back. Cost to normal players: one keydown listener (`init()` still only runs when active, so no rollover polling / seeding / tracking suppression).
- **The two doors are NOT equivalent** (user call): `?dev=true` is the ONLY one that reveals `#dbgDevActions` (wipe leaderboards/visits/first-run, reset PotD). Ctrl+D opens the PANEL with those still hidden — the panel is worth making easy to reach, a one-keystroke "delete every leaderboard" isn't. ⚠ **The panel is not a security boundary**: those endpoints are open/unauthenticated by design during dev and anyone knowing the URLs can POST them regardless. Gate server-side (à la `DAILY_SUMMARY_KEY`) before public release.
- **Now Playing first-click jump** (CG report: puzzle shrinks when the chip appears on first interaction, since audio can't autoplay). The v1.36 predictive reserve already targets this and by static reading SHOULD fire on that path (`musicMode` defaults to `game_playlist`, so `isMuted()` is false) — **root cause NOT confirmed**, and I couldn't reproduce it locally. Hardened the one real failure mode instead: `getBoundingClientRect()` returns 0 whenever the element isn't laid out at the instant we ask (plausible on CG, where the intro is skipped and `autoStartFirstPractice` runs synchronously during load), and a 0 silently drops the reserve entirely. render.js now caches the last good chip height (`npBandHeight`) and falls back to it, so predicted and actual always produce the SAME reserve. **If it still jumps**: test with music OFF in Settings — if it shrinks anyway the culprit is the `#hud` reserve (`hudReserve` measures 0 while the HUD is hidden), not the chip.
- Ruled out along the way: chip is `position:absolute` (can't reflow anything directly), and there are no web fonts (all system stacks), so late font settling isn't shifting the measurement.
- Version bumped 1.40 → 1.41.

## 2026-08-01 — Release v1.40 (PotD generator v2 is LIVE; start-join hole closed)
- **The PotD generator was replaced.** `potd-gen2.js` is now THE generator: `potd.js` rolls every daily slot via `PotdGen2.defaultParams()` + `rollParams()`. Four axes are rolled per puzzle instead of one — board size, twin coverage, twin GROUP SIZE, gate count — where v1 rolled only dims and pinned coverage at 30% / gates at 3. Old `generateDims` survives as `generateDimsV1` behind a load-failure fallback (a v1 puzzle beats no daily puzzle). Slot still fixes quadMode/pathCount; NO per-path-count ranges yet (user wants them eventually — `defaultParams` already takes pathCount and ignores it, so that's the one function to change).
- **Tuned live values** (user's call after panel testing): size **6-16 sub-tiles**, total **50-80 sub-tiles**, twins **11-42%**, group size **2-6**, gates **2-6**. Single definition in potd-gen2.js's `DEF_*` block — the debug panel's sliders OPEN on the same numbers, so it stays a faithful preview.
- ⚠ **The 6 size floor is LOAD-BEARING, not taste.** A floor of 4 let the area window produce 4:1 boards (16×4, 15×4, quad 8×2 tiles) that **cannot build at 3-4 paths**. maze.js's multi-path branch is `while (bestCount < target)` with NO attempt ceiling — it never settles for fewer paths, it spins, so such a slot's daily puzzle would simply never generate. Measured at 4p/90s: 16×5 aborted, 14×5 aborted, quad 16×4 aborted; 15×4 built once in 61.5s (a lottery). 3 paths handle those same shapes in 18-25s → the constraint is specifically a **4-path** one. **The missing attempt ceiling is still there** — the floor avoids it, the allowlist test guards it, neither fixes it.
- **New knobs in maze.js** (all null/off for Marathon/Zen, reset per worker job so they can't leak): `setTwinCoverageTarget` (ABSOLUTE coverage — the ramp scale is clamped to 1 and tops out at the 30% `TWIN_COVERAGE`, which is the FLOOR of the interesting range), and `setTwinGroupSizeRange` (MIXED group sizes — uniformity was only ever policy; rings are walked, never indexed by stride). No-override path is provably identical to before and makes no RNG calls, so build sequences are unchanged.
- **Start-join hole closed (affects Marathon/Zen too, not just PotD).** v1.38 taught `breakPreWonPaths` to detect joined paths but it only nudged the cells DIRECTLY carrying the shared lane, excluding endpoints and crosses — and those exclusions describe exactly where joins land. Field screenshot + repro both show it as two ADJACENT TERMINALS linked (6×6 4-path, cells 4,0 and 3,0, both endpoints) → every candidate excluded → "nothing safe to touch" shipped the joined board. Hit ~2-3% of 4-path builds. Two fallbacks added: widen to the joined paths' whole lit chains, then flip an elbow terminal to its OTHER notch-preserving rotation.
- **Portrait presentation**: boards generate landscape (rollParams orders the axes); portrait viewports rotate 90° CW at load via `Game.applyBoardRotation` (newly exposed — maze.js names it the single orchestrator for the Maze+Gates pairing). Server snapshot stays canonical = same puzzle for everyone. **Fresh starts only** — `resumeAttempt` loads an already-rotated board and would double-apply.
- **Debug panel is now the v2 tuning panel**: two-knob ranges (size/tiles/twins/groups/gates), Generate button + timing readout with rolling median, achieved-vs-requested twin coverage. Visual Tuning collapsed by default; dev-action buttons + preGenStatus hidden-not-removed. `Game.isPreGenBusy()` replaced scraping `#preGenStatus`'s TEXT for the timing-contention flag.
- **Generation cost, measured** (answers "why is 1-path slower than 4-path?"): two drivers pull opposite ways. Canonical rejection (singular 1-2 path ONLY) worsens with size — 1p needs ~210 buildPuzzle attempts at 15×15 vs 20 at 8×8. Path-fitting worsens as boards shrink and paths rise. Quad skips canonical at EVERY path count, so it's monotonic in path count. Corollary: fast 3/4-path boards have NO alternate-solution suppression. ⚠ Benchmarking maze.js in Node needs `setTimeout`→`setImmediate` — the 1ms clamp on `yieldToBrowser` dominates the multi-path branch and makes wall-clock meaningless.
- New test suites: `../tests/join-test.js` (start-state cleanliness on dense 4-path boards — volume-based, the failure is ~2-3%) and `../tests/potd-config-test.js` (live config invariants + a SHAPE ALLOWLIST; `--build` regenerates each shape at 4p to re-earn it). twin-test gained sections 9 + 10. All suites green.
- ⚠ **Watch after deploy**: quad variety is thin (4 shapes vs 12 singular — the even-step and the 50-80 window intersect narrowly). Today's already-seeded slots keep their v1 boards; only NEW days get v2 (a dev PotD reset re-seeds today). During cache turnover a day's 8 slots can be a MIX of v1- and v2-seeded boards — harmless, each slot is a whole puzzle.
- Version bumped 1.39 → 1.40.

## 2026-08-01 — Release v1.39 (ladder arm assigned by the SERVER, balanced on ENGAGED)
- Client no longer draws the A/B/C/D arm. `firstRunVariant` stays NULL until the server's assignment arrives in the `/first-run/sync` response (`Tracking.firstRunVariant()`), adopted by `ensureFirstRunVariant(level)` — called from `startNextPuzzle` ONLY. Do NOT call it from `levelConfig`: pre-gen asks that about FUTURE levels and would force the fallback draw early.
- Server balances on the **>=3 engaged count, not total players** (user call — standard was sitting at 1 of 8; players who quit before the ladders diverge carry no distinguishing signal). Seeded with the real table (totals 6/8/14/17, engaged 3/1/3/6) the next 5 assignments all go to `standard`. ⚠ This is response-adaptive allocation — see the back-end NOTES for the time-confound caveat and the skew guard that bounds it.
- Safe because all four ladders are identical for `MARATHON.FIRST_RUN_SHARED_LEVELS` (3) puzzles — an unknown arm falls through levelConfig to the standard ladder, which matches every arm over that stretch. Sync fires on the first move of puzzle 1, so there are 3 puzzles of slack.
- **Fallback**: if level > SHARED_LEVELS with no assignment (offline / cold dyno / blocked request), draw locally and `Tracking.firstRunLockVariant(v)` — locked arms are SENT and the server honors them, so the row records the ladder actually played rather than one assigned blind.
- `FIRST_RUN_VARIANT_FORCE` still works client-side and is locked immediately (asserted, not assigned) — lock-in needs no back-end deploy.
- Known cosmetic: pre-gen lookahead computed at level 1 may cache level 4 under the standard ladder; if the arm turns out to be 'fast' that's one cache miss ("Building puzzle…") at level 4. Self-correcting from level 2 on.
- ⚠ **DEPLOY ORDER: back-end FIRST** (assignment + response echo). Without it the response carries no arm and every new player falls through to the local locked fallback draw — i.e. the old random behavior. Not harmful, just no improvement.
- Version bumped 1.38 → 1.39.

## 2026-08-01 — Release v1.38 (twin coverage ramps by level; terminal-dock + start-join fixes; funnel gated on real play)
- **Twin coverage is now PROGRESSIVE per run level** (user call): none L1-2, ONE set at L3, linear to the full 30% at L12+ (`config.js` `TWIN_RAMP_*` + `twinScaleForLevel`). Scale flows marathon → game.js (cache keys include it) → worker (per-job, so PotD bg-gen stays full-coverage) → `Maze.setTwinCoverageScale`. First Marathon/Zen start after this update shows one "Building puzzle…" while starters rebuild under new cache keys — self-heals.
- **Regression fix (user screenshot)**: random twin membership could include MULTI-PATH terminals (only path 1's entry/exit were excluded) and the offset normalization spun them off their notches → boards started with undocked terminals. assignTwins now excludes ALL endpoint cells; singular terminals can never be twinned. Quad endpoint quads stay twinnable by design (allowedQuadTurns keeps stubs lit).
- **Regression-class fix (user screenshot)**: fresh multi-path boards could start with two DIFFERENT paths JOINED (dark-red state — chance alignment, never re-checked). breakPreWonPaths now also detects same-lane/two-path overlap (render's hasJoinedLanes criterion) and nudges it apart before shipping.
- **First-Time Players funnel gated on real play** (user call — their CrazyGames bounce ticked the 0-puzzle bucket): `firstRunBegin` still starts RECORDING at auto-start (so a How-to click before the first move isn't lost) but writes `eng: 0` and schedules NO sync; `_frSyncNow` holds everything until `firstRunEngaged()` fires from the first committed puzzle action (marathon.js `notifyPuzzleInteraction`, already called by game.js recordMove). Payload gains `engaged`. Fires on ANY committed action, not just the auto-start run. Legacy local state (no `eng` field) counts as engaged — those browsers already have a server row.
- ⚠ **Back-end was deployed FIRST** (2026-07-31 ~2:59 PM) and carries the matching half: the no-content guard (blocks bounce rows even from OLD cached clients — that's what protects the data during cache turnover) plus `POST /api/admin/prune-first-run`. User ran the prune: 8 never-played rows removed of 114. `initial_run_puzzles = 0` now means "tried but finished none".
- **Test harnesses promoted to `../tests/`** (twin-test / multipath-test / verify-opt / bench-median / first-run-guard-test + README) per NEW universal rule 11 — run `node tests/twin-test.js` etc. after touching maze.js. `/create` template + universal CLAUDE.md updated.
- Version bumped 1.37 → 1.38.

## 2026-07-31 — Release v1.37 (twin GROUPS — scaling size, random membership; generation 4-5× faster)
- **Twins → twin GROUPS** (user call): size scales with board — pairs <8 gridScale, triplets <11, quads <14, quints <17, then +1/+2 (Marathon singular hits those ~L11/18/24/30). Constant ~30% board coverage at every size (`TWIN_COVERAGE`, the one knob); group count stays ≤16 so palette colors never repeat. Stored as a RING (`_twin.partner` → next member; N=2 = old reciprocal pair, so ALL old recordings/snapshots stay valid). loadSnapshot's corruption guard now walks the ring (strips any tile whose walk doesn't come home — same graceful degradation).
- **Membership is RANDOM** (same-day policy change, user call, after their 4-path 8×8 showed only 6 twin tiles): each group = 1 path-tile ANCHOR + random members, path or filler. Dense boards starved the old one-path+fillers rule (4-path boards leave <10 fillers). **Solvability invariant**: path members are SNAPPED to the anchor's offset-from-solution at assignment; lockstep preserves equality forever, so they all reach solution together. An interim filler-scarcity size-shrink fallback was built and then deleted the same day — random membership subsumes it.
- **minSolveMoves prices groups as one shared turn count** (mirrors quadMinMoves) — path-path members solve together, so per-tile summing would overcount. Verified end-to-end: test solves every board (incl. 4-path with path-path-path triplets) via real lockstep rotate() calls in EXACTLY the floor.
- **Generation 4-5× faster** (user asked about 17×17; found 10×10 was already ~39s): minRotCost → 192-entry table; shortestPossiblePath BFS → typed arrays (was 13M key strings/build); breakEqualAlternates resumes at last offending edge; generateLanes DFS → flat lane store, Map materialized on success. Medians: 8×8 2.4→1.2s, 10×10 39→8.9s, 14×14 282→68s. NO behavior change — new BFS verified bit-identical to old (421 comparisons), same Math.random() call sequence in the DFS. Remaining cost is real search (12×12 runs ~130 build attempts before canonical passes) — further gains need behavior tuning (dfsCap/retry budgets), not attempted.
- **WATCH in playtesting**: path-path twins are a two-for-one solve (nominally easier) but more trap surface (any member can disturb settled work). If too easy: bias non-anchor membership toward fillers (few lines). Quad-mode twins unchanged (already unrestricted composition).
- Test harnesses (twin-test / multipath-test / verify-opt / bench-median) live in the session scratchpad — session-temporary; copy into the project if wanted for regression.
- Version bumped 1.36 → 1.37.

## 2026-07-30 — Release v1.36 (RESET button; run-start tumble-in; ⭐ tooltip rename; phone HUD reshuffle)
- **RESET button beside UNDO** (both HUD + debug): one recorded `reset` move through recordMove — undoable (pre-reset board banked; no confirm dialog needed), replayable (applyReplayMove branch reloads rec.initialState; silent-SFX jump like live), counts as ONE twist in the Zen stat and never refunds prior moves (user requirement: no timer/stat reset). Loads recording.initialState so it works past the 200-undo cap; drops player locks. Old cached clients desync watching reset-bearing recordings — accepted, same as the penalty move types. No keyboard shortcut (deliberate).

Covers startGame, boundary resumes, Watch puzzle 1 (replayAll i===0). Mid-puzzle resumes deliberately excluded (clock already running). Timer compensation free via existing onPuzzleReady path.

HINT keeps bottom-right. Watch: first-run pre-tip-ack shows DAILY & MORE row-2 LEFT, hops RIGHT when How-to appears — pin right if it reads badly.

## 2026-07-29 — Post-v1.35: broken-path fade halved AGAIN (750 → 375)

## 2026-07-29 — Release v1.35 (alternate-solution pressure: telemetry + equal-length suppression + targeted gates)
- **⚠ DEPLOY ORDER: back-end FIRST** (migration adds `page_visits.solves_alternate`; /solved PATCH + late-milestones count it; admin stats ship the sums), then this zip, then the umbrella admin page (Netlify).
- **Context**: user asked whether alternative solutions could be ELIMINATED. Assessment: exact uniqueness is impractical (the repair loop, not the check, explodes — alternate-freeness is rare under the 65% elbow filler bias, and 4-path budgets are already at 10k attempts). Shipped the practical trio instead:
- **(1) Alternate-solve telemetry**: `Maze.solvedViaAlternate()` (canonical-edge-set compare, quad-aware via the solutionEdges dance, error→false) rides recordSolve as a 3rd flag → per-visit `solves_alternate` counter → amber "🔀 Alternate route" bar in the admin Solve Audio panel (now "Solve Audio + Routes"; denominator = all solves). GROUND TRUTH for how often alternates actually happen — read this before investing further.
- **(2) Equal-length alternate suppression (1-2 path canonical only)**: the plain BFS can't see equal-length alternates (it rediscovers the designed route); `hasEqualAlternate` runs the BFS once per DESIGNED EDGE with that edge excluded — any ≤-designed-length route avoiding a designed edge is a distinct alternate. `breakEqualAlternates` rerolls fillers on offenders (shares smartReroll's breaker via new `breakFillerOnRoute`). Wired into 1-path isHardEnough + 2-path isCanon/rerollFor (now take pathIdx). Cost ~pathLen extra BFS per check — microseconds. 3/4-path still skips canonical BY DESIGN.
- **(3) Alternate-aware gate placement (all singular boards incl. 3/4-path)**: `Maze.alternateRouteEdges()` (one leftover alternate per path, computed once at gate time, null in quad — scrambled-position BFS would be noise) → `Gates.assignGates` 6th param: candidates/dirs whose SOLVED-state prong sits on an alternate edge win priority, so mandatory gates double as alternate-blockers. Falls back to old random-safe placement when nothing to target.
- **WATCH**: 1-2 path build times (equal-alternate loop is capped, can't hang, but filler-starved boards may retry more — if "Building puzzle…" appears more, suspect this first). Telemetry should skew alternates toward 3/4-path solves if suppression works.
- Version bumped 1.34 → 1.35.

## 2026-07-29 — Release v1.34 (first-run ladder A/B/C/D experiment; nudge fixed at 4 solves; global context-menu ban)
- **⚠ DEPLOY ORDER: back-end FIRST** (migration adds `first_run_stats.variant`; sync endpoint stores it; admin endpoint gains per-variant breakdown), then this zip, then the umbrella admin page (Netlify). Each layer degrades gracefully if the others lag.
- **First-run ladder experiment**: each NEW player's auto-start run randomly draws one of four ladders — `fast` (the [3,2,2,2] compression + quad hand-off), `standard` (4/tier), `extended` (6/tier, `FIRST_RUN_EXTENDED_TIER_LENGTH`), `single` (1 path forever, growth only). Draw happens in autoStartFirstPractice, persists to `_firstRunVariant_v1`, stamps the player's first_run_stats row via the first sync (set-once server-side). Growth accrues identically in all variants — only the path-count axis varies. Lock the winner later via `MARATHON.FIRST_RUN_VARIANT_FORCE` (one config value, no code change). This REPLACES the short-lived FIRST_RUN_FAST_TRACK boolean (added and superseded within this same unshipped window).
- **Admin First-Time Players panel** (deployed via umbrella site, not this zip): stats now ENGAGED-ONLY (≥1 initial-run solve; 0-puzzle bouncers tracked — players-card sub-line + red 0 bucket — but excluded from every avg/rate, numerators AND denominators; user call: bouncers are a lost cause). New "Ladder experiment" table: per-variant engaged-only stats following the window tabs, plus a `(pre-experiment)` row for NULL-variant rows. Admin endpoint restructured to one-fetch in-Python aggregation (the variant×window matrix would've been ~150 COUNT queries).
- **PotD nudge fixed at 4 solves** (fires as puzzle 5 appears), variant-INDEPENDENT — the old first-2-path-solve trigger would fire at different depths per variant (never, on `single`), making the nudge column incomparable. Trigger history in the onPuzzleReady comment.
- **Browser context menu suppressed globally** (game.js document-level handler): right-click is a game input, and the canvas-only preventDefault leaked during penalty holds (canvas pointer-events:none → click fell through → menu; user report). Carve-out: text-entry fields keep their native menu (leaderboard-name paste). Capture-phase audio/music gesture listeners unaffected (preventDefault ≠ stopPropagation).
- Test: fresh profiles until all 4 variants observed (localStorage `_firstRunVariant_v1` shows the draw); nudge at puzzle 5 in each; admin table rows fill per variant; right-click anywhere → no menu except in text inputs.

## 2026-07-29 — Release v1.33 (replays get the between-puzzle tumble transition)
- **Multi-puzzle replays (leaderboard Watch) now show the same 3D spin transition live play has between puzzles** (user: playback didn't match live). Seam sequence: solved board holds the existing 800ms breath (stands in for the solve popup's dwell) → `Render.spinOutBoard()` tumbles it away (fired in replayAll's loop, i>0 only) → playOneRecording draws the next board hidden behind the hold class → `Render.spinInBoard()` (fired inside playOneRecording after its setup draw) waits out the spin-out remainder and tumbles it in → move/music timeline HELD until the board lands (the wait rides replayTimer/replayResolve so Stop interrupts it like any inter-move wait; scripted music starts after, keeping songs beside their moves).
- Reuses the exact live machinery — durations/easing/mirrored-axes/reduced-motion all match automatically, and future spin tuning applies to both. spinInBoard returns 0 with no spin pending → the single-recording replay button and puzzle 1 of a Watch are untouched (matches live: a run's first puzzle doesn't spin).
- Test: Watch a multi-puzzle Marathon run — every boundary tumbles out/in; puzzle 1 appears plainly; Stop mid-tumble exits cleanly.
- Version bumped 1.32 → 1.33.

## 2026-07-29 — Release v1.32 (HOTFIX 2: replay freeze — shared _twin objects corrupted snapshots + recordings)
- **Field crash on v1.30/31** (user: Marathon replay froze, `TypeError ... reading '4'` in Maze.rotate's twin-partner lookup): every snapshot clone helper (`snapshotState`, `deepCloneSnapshot`, `cloneMazeSnap`) shallow-copied tiles via Object.assign — copying `rotation` BY VALUE but sharing the `_twin` OBJECT by reference. The penalty transforms mutated `_twin.partner` IN PLACE → retroactively rewrote partner keys inside every banked undo/replay snapshot AND inside recordings' stored initialStates. Undo/replay restoring a pre-penalty board then had post-penalty partner keys → OOB grid row on the next twinned rotate → frozen replay.
- **Recordings made on the buggy build are PERMANENTLY corrupted** (including server-stored leaderboard copies) — hard refreshes can't help; the bad keys are in the data.
- **Fixes (three)**: (1) rotateBoard/flipBoard now REPLACE `_twin` (`Object.assign({}, _twin, {partner})`) — replace-don't-mutate rule now explicit in comments; (2) all three clone helpers clone `_twin` per tile — shared-reference corruption now impossible from either side; (3) **loadSnapshot sanitizer** (the single choke point all replay/undo/resume paths enter): twin refs validated (partner in bounds AND reciprocal), broken pairs stripped BOTH sides → corrupted old recordings play through with those pairs degraded to plain uncoupled tiles (may visibly diverge / not reach solved — unrecoverable data, watchable not faithful) instead of freezing. ~25 unguarded partner-dereference sites made guarding-each a non-starter.
- New recordings on this build are fully isolated + faithful. Test: replay the corrupted run (plays through, no freeze), record + replay a fresh penalty run (faithful incl. twins), undo back through a penalty live then rotate twins.
- Version bumped 1.31 → 1.32.

## 2026-07-29 — Release v1.31 (HOTFIX: penalty crash/freeze on singular boards — stale quadScramble)
- **Field crash on v1.30** (user: fresh run, first 2-path board, joined paths → freeze + `RangeError: Invalid array length` at maze.js rotateBoard): `quadScramble` was NON-NULL on a singular board — building singular never nulls the leftover quad matrix, and the pre-gen worker alternates quad/singular starter builds, so singular snapshots have carried stale quad matrices FOR WEEKS, harmlessly, because every consumer gated on `quadMode` first. The new penalty transforms gated only on `quadScramble` → odd singular dims computed `new Array(ROWS/2 = x.5)` → throw mid-transform → ghost + canvas-hold stranded with input locked = frozen game.
- **Three-layer fix**: (1) `rotateBoard`/`flipBoard` gate on `quadMode && quadScramble` — LOAD-BEARING forever, already-seeded server-side PotD snapshots may carry the stale matrix indefinitely; (2) `snapshotState` only captures quadScramble in quad mode (source fix — worker singular snapshots now clean); (3) `runBoardPenalty` catches transform exceptions → `cancelSpin` teardown + input unlock + Logger.warn — any future transform bug degrades to "penalty aborted", never a frozen game. Comments at all three sites record the incident so the gates don't get simplified away.
- Test: fresh cache → auto-start run → join paths on the first 2-path (odd-dims) board → penalty plays through; quad boards still transform correctly.
- Version bumped 1.30 → 1.31.

## 2026-07-29 — Release v1.30 (penalty shake: in replays, after the tile lands, subtle + constant)
- **Shake unified into the ghost pipeline** (user caught it missing from playback): the vibration warning was a separate pre-apply stage on the REAL canvas, which replays couldn't reproduce. Both penalty visuals now shake THE GHOST first, then turn — data applies at trigger time, the move's t marks that instant, live play and playback show the identical shake-then-turn sequence. Render.shakeBoard removed; `_makePenaltyGhost`/`_runPenaltyAnim` are the shared machinery (render.js).
- **~300ms tile-settle hold before the ghost snapshot** (user caught the joining tile never visibly rotating): the trigger fires from the same refresh() that committed the joining move, so the snapshot froze the canvas before the tile's rotation animation (ROTATION_ANIM_MS 200 + 30ms settle) painted — tile sat un-rotated through the shake, popped at reveal. runBoardPenalty now holds ~300ms (input already locked; quit-during-hold guard restored); the tile lands and the red join reads for a beat before the shake. Replays inherit the rhythm automatically (recorded t = post-hold apply moment).
- **Shake tuned across three user rounds**: 450ms → 900ms ("double it") → but two ease-in-out iterations read as TWO PULSES and 1.2% amplitude wandered too far → final: ONE LINEAR pass over 900ms, direction hop every ~56ms through a fixed 8-direction pattern, amplitude 0.5% of board width (floor 2px) — a steady buzz, no swell, no seam. Knobs: the 0.005 amplitude + 15-hop count in _runPenaltyAnim.
- If the v1.29 zip predates any of this, it all ships here (supersede convention).
- Version bumped 1.29 → 1.30.

## 2026-07-29 — Release v1.29 (joined-paths penalty: board rotates 90° or mirror-flips)
- **New chaos-penalty mechanic**: joining two different-color paths (flashing-red overlap) shakes the board (~450ms warning), then — 50/50 — either ROTATES it 90° (CW/CCW random; dims swap, non-square boards rescale to fit) or MIRROR-FLIPS it across a random axis (dims unchanged; 3D card-turn — the ghost's backface past 90° IS the mirrored board, so the landing is seamless). Input locked throughout (`boardRotating` flag + canvas hold class). Trigger is EDGE-based — the overlap survives both transforms (connectivity invariant), so re-punishment requires un-joining and re-joining. Fires in Zen/Marathon AND PotD; never in tutorial (its script deliberately joins paths), replays, or builds.
- **The data genuinely transforms** (maze.js `rotateBoard`/`flipBoard` + gates.js twins; game.js applyBoardRotation/applyBoardFlip orchestrate): tiles carry rotation AND _solution through the same map (offset-from-solved → minSolveMoves/hints stay valid), cross-lanes/twin keys/terminals/locks all remap. **Mirror math is NOT rotation math**: reflections conjugate rotations (M∘Rᵏ=R⁻ᵏ∘M), so under a flip `quadScramble` values NEGATE (matrix mirrors positionally) and Gates' unison `delta` NEGATES (solutionDir mirrored) — rotation leaves both alone (solutionDir +1, values/delta unchanged). Tile rotations under mirror are found by port-set matching (`mirrorRotFor` — no uniform shift exists; safe because all 3 tile shapes are achiral). Gate edge-mapping verified by hand for all 4 transform×direction cases.
- **Recording/replay/undo**: penalties record as `rotateBoard`/`flipBoard` moves at APPLY time; replays apply data synchronously inside the visual's applyFn (later moves need the transformed grid) with the animation running in parallel; snapshot-undo rewinds through them — both undo paths now REFIT when dims change across a step (rotation only). ⚠ Old cached clients replaying NEW recordings skip the unknown move types → that replay desyncs visually (ages out with cache updates).
- **Quit safety**: Render.cancelSpin also tears down penalty visuals (shake anim + ghost + hold); potd.js quitToMenu now calls it (penalty fires in PotD; marathon goToMenu already did). Marathon clock keeps ticking through the ~1.1s penalty — deliberate (it IS the punishment). Reduced-motion: instant data transform, no theatrics.
- Test recipe: repeated joins → ~half rotations both directions, ~half flips both axes; flip a gated board (prongs mirror, same twists to open), flip a quad board (hint still snaps quads), undo through both variants, watch a replay containing them, quit mid-penalty.
- Version bumped 1.28 → 1.29.

## 2026-07-29 — Release v1.28 (3D board-spin transition between Zen/Marathon puzzles)
- **Next-puzzle transition**: tap past the solve popup → the solved board tumbles into the distance (off-axis rotate3d + translateZ recede + fade, 480ms), then the new puzzle tumbles in on a mirrored axis (560ms). User-tuned from an initial 340/400 ("slow it down a bit"). Durations live in render.js `SPIN_OUT_MS`/`SPIN_IN_MS` and MUST match the CSS animation-durations on `.mazeSpinGhost` / `#maze.maze-spin-in`.
- **Mechanism (render.js spinOutBoard/spinInBoard/cancelSpin)**: GHOST canvas snapshot of the solved board animates away while the real canvas hides (`.maze-spin-hold`) — the build pipeline blanks/redraws it invisibly, untouched. spinInBoard waits out the spin-out remainder (pre-gen resolves faster than the animation), then spins the real canvas in; returns total ms to visible.
- **Marathon clock compensated**: onPuzzleReady shifts puzzleStartMs forward by the spin window and delays startTimer to match (guarded) — the transition eats zero play time; old-client leaderboard fairness preserved.
- Scope/safety: advance()-path only (first puzzles/resumes/PotD/replays unaffected); goToMenu calls Render.cancelSpin (else a quit mid-spin strands the next board invisible via the hold class); pointer-events off during hold+spin; ghost inserted as canvas nextSibling with z-index 0 (under HUD by DOM order); prefers-reduced-motion skips everything.
- **cinematic_bass timing experiment — settled on ORIGINAL**: tried at tumble-start, then at full settle; user preferred immediate-at-onPuzzleReady (overlapping the outbound tumble). Callback plumbing fully removed; comments in both files record the history — don't re-try without reading it.
- Version bumped 1.27 → 1.28.

It only "fit" pre-v1.25 because the collapsing spacer silently absorbed the overflow (= the title-jump bug itself).

In 920-1150 the block's clamps sit at/near caps → near-desktop look; the differences at 1080 fullscreen are budget-capped PotD thumbs (8rem=128px vs 144) and the 2vh spacer (~22px vs ~76 — menu anchors a bit higher, identically on all tabs). Above 1150 base sizes genuinely fit with slack. Zen/Marathon card sizing unaffected (#menuMarathonTypes ID clamp caps the same in and out of the block).

## 2026-07-29 — Release v1.26 (PotD slot thumbs sized by height budget)
- **PotD slot thumbs in the ≤920px-tall block now size from a height BUDGET, not a flat vh multiple**: `clamp(2.8rem, calc(50vh - 15rem), 8rem)`. Why: the stack has TWO card rows, so thumb size should be (viewport − fixed chrome)/2 — a plain 8vh grew too slowly (v1.25 left dead space under the footer at ~615px, user screenshot) and the chrome is floor-bound so it isn't linear in vh.
- Calibration history (all user-screenshot-driven, same day): 8vh ≈ 49px too small at ~615px → K=14rem gave ~91px at ~630px, scrollbar returned → **K=15rem final**. TUNE K, NOT THE 50vh COEFFICIENT, and err toward LARGER K — a little slack under the footer beats a scrollbar. Full math in the styles.css comment on the rule.
- Portrait/landscape phones untouched (their own blocks + the load-bearing portrait re-asserts from v1.25 still win).
- Version bumped 1.25 → 1.26.
