Legacy `_setting_musicMuted` honored on first boot only. `Music.startCreditsSequence`/`stopCreditsSequence` flip a `forceCredits` flag so the end-credits scene plays a credits song regardless of the user's mode. Credits pool comes from admin API's `lists.credits` for `?game=circuitousness` — until the admin populates that list, music falls back to the gameplay pool (so the sequence isn't silent but uses regular game music).
- **Credits CSS overflow trap**: `.credits-overlay` had `overflow:hidden` lifted from TANTЯO; on narrow windows the 34% `.credits-content` width made the title overflow horizontally and the overlay clipped it. Switched to `overflow:visible` on overlay + scroll + content — body's own `overflow:hidden` still handles past-viewport clipping. **Lesson:** don't propagate `overflow:hidden` down the chain when only the outermost layer (body) needs it.
- **Title-O canvas size lock-in bug** (was intermittent for weeks): `Render.renderSnippet` was setting `targetCanvas.style.width = sizeCss + 'px'` inline, pinning the canvas at whatever was measured on the FIRST paint. If that paint caught the title mid-font-load (small), the canvas stayed small forever — the rAF redraw loop kept re-measuring the now-pinned size. Fix: stop writing inline width/height in renderSnippet; canvas has CSS `width:100%; height:100%` so it tracks its parent automatically. Drawing buffer is still set for sharpness.
- **Always-warm starter pre-gen** (Marathon): on page load `ensureStartersBuilt()` (game.js) queues all 8 starter puzzles (`s1..s4, q1..q4`) at their level-1 dims via `STARTER_PLAN` (derived from `MARATHON.TYPES` + `MIN_DIM_*`). Worker chain: `requestBuild` → worker.onmessage → `fillPreGenQueue` (lookahead during play) → `ensureStartersBuilt` (refill consumed starters). `invalidatePreGen` now preserves entries whose key is in `STARTER_KEY_SET` so a config change wipes lookahead but not starters. `fillPreGenQueue` no longer fires at menu (between games) — the dims would be stale from the previous game; starters own that idle window. `q4` (4-path quad) is the slow starter — 8-12s build time on dev.
- **Worker preempt**: `maze.js init()` now returns boolean (`true` = completed, `false` = aborted) and checks new `abortFlag` at every attempt boundary (alongside `hurryFlag`). `maze-worker.js` switched to FIFO `pending` queue; `generate {urgent: true}` clears the queue, prepends the urgent job, and calls `Maze.setAbort()`. Player picking a not-yet-cached starter triggers `requestBuild(..., {urgent: true})` — the in-flight build is aborted within ~1 attempt boundary and the player's puzzle starts immediately. Wasted work is re-queued by `ensureStartersBuilt` afterward.
- **Pre-gen console logging**: 🧩 building / 🧩 preempt → / ✅ built / 🎯 cache hit / ⚡ hurry. Each line includes purpose tag (`starter sN`, `lookahead L+N`, `player request`), dims, path count, and quad/singular. Live trace of the worker's activity makes the pre-gen lifecycle debuggable.
- **Settings popup widening**: 26rem → 32rem, `.settingsSelect` max-width 16rem → 22rem, so the music dropdown's longest option (`🎲🎤 Game Playlist + End Credits by Claude`) fits within the popup when opened. Native dropdown inherits the select's width.
- **Version not bumped** despite substantial changes — per universal rule 4, only `/release` does that. Run `/rel` from this folder to bump `APP_VERSION`/`PAGE_VERSION` and regenerate the deploy zip.

## 2026-05-22 — PotD replay + leaderboard tab + hint rewrite

- **PotD replay shape mismatch** (crash + bogus "1/96" puzzle counter): marathon submits `events` as an ARRAY of full per-puzzle recordings; PotD submits `events` as the SINGLE recording's flat moves array. `Game.replayAll` only understands the marathon shape — so `events[0]` was a move object, `deepCloneSnapshot(undefined.initialState)` crashed, and `events.length` got rendered as puzzle count. Fix lives in `startPotdReplay` (marathon.js): fetch recording + the puzzle's snapshot from `/api/potd/today/<slot>`, reconstruct a marathon-shape recording (`initialState` from the snapshot's `maze`, `gates` from snapshot's `gates`, `quadMode`/`pathCount` parsed from the slot string), wrap in `[rec]`. Today-only — back-end has no date-aware snapshot endpoint yet; if/when historical PotD replays land, add `/api/potd/puzzle/<date>/<slot>`.
- **Title-O seam saga** (tile boundaries visibly cutting through the red O): tried (1) `lineCap = 'square'` → made it worse, visible tangent-stub shoulders, (2) angular arc extension that stays on the curve → no visible change, (3) enabling quad mode → did fix the seam but also merged all 4 tile bevels into one big tile face, which the user wanted kept distinct. Final fix is `Render.paintSnippetRingMask`: title-renderer.js draws the per-tile snippet, then we overlay a SINGLE continuous-circle stripe stroke (using the existing `litStripes` colours so the gradient + pulse match) directly on top. The per-tile seams ARE still there underneath — they're just covered. Walls render below the channel in z-order so inner tile bevels survive.
- **Hint rewrite** (`maze.js#hint`): three passes in priority order. (1) Walk player's CURRENT lit chain from each entry via `walkFrom`; hint first lit tile with a wrong rotation. (2) Walk the SOLUTION path via new `solutionPaths()` (mirror of `solutionEdges()` but returns ordered cells; same un-scramble dance for quad mode); hint first wrong cell beyond where the lit chain ended. (3) Original tier-based random pick — only reached when both walks find nothing (port-equivalent-but-lit-wrong tiles, or player-locked tiles at solution rotation). Quad-mode "wrong orientation" handled by `quadAlreadyPortEquivalent` so symmetric rotations don't get hint-locked uselessly.
- **Leaderboard now has a PotD/Marathon tab** at the top of `#leaderboard`. Same slot dropdown drives both (taxonomy is identical: `s1`–`s4`, `q1`–`q4`). Cache key namespaced per mode AND per date for PotD so yesterday's cached board can't paint over an empty fresh-today one. `loadBoard`/`saveBoard`/`fetchBoardFromServer` have backwards-compat shims so the marathon submit/flush call sites still work with single-arg calls. Default tab on entry = `ModePicker.getMode()`, but a tab change made by the user is preserved when returning from a replay.
- **Latent bug fixed**: `deepCloneSnapshot` in game.js was silently dropping `entry4`/`exit4` — every 4-path replay (slots s4 and q4) lost path 4 in both marathon and PotD modes. Added both fields. Marathon recordings made before this fix will still be missing path 4 on replay.
- **Misc this session**: PotD dev-reset moved from `RESET_ON_LOAD = true` (auto-fire every page load) → manual button on the debug page (`Potd.devReset()` public API). `TRANSITION_QUIET_MS` 1000ms → 333ms (still scales out per tap so click-cascade can't skip). Default volumes: music 1.0, sfx 0.75 (only apply to new installs — existing localStorage values preserved). `audience_boo` removed from all SFX pools and trigger lists. `#hudTimer` was inheriting `.urgent`/`.penalty` from marathon's game-over state when PotD reused it; `Potd.showHud()` now strips them. PotD startPuzzle clears `Maze`+`Render` so the previous-mode puzzle isn't visible behind the "Generating…" banner. Music wired into PotD (`Music.start` in `startPuzzle`, `Music.stop` in `quitToMenu`).

## 2026-05-20 — Gates: quad mode, replay, edge-tile fixes

- **Quad-mode gates** anchor at QUAD-CORNERS only (`vertexStride = 2` in sub-tile coords passed to `Gates.assignGates`); prong still spans one sub-tile `cellSize`. `Maze.solutionEdges` in quad mode un-scrambles the live grid via reverse `rotateQuad` calls inside a `try/finally` — walks the solved layout — then `restoreState` + manual `quadScramble` restore (snapshot/restore round-trip the grid but NOT `quadScramble`, so it's saved separately).
- **Bevel "not opaque" was canvas AA, not alpha** — bevels rendered at `globalAlpha = 1` correctly, but AA at the bevel's OUTER edge blended bevel-color with the dark tile underneath, producing a translucent halo. Fix: pre-fill the gate's outer polygons at face color before `drawBeveledPolygon` so the AA blends bevel-with-face (smooth red-on-red).
- **Recording/replay now carries gate state** — `Gates.snapshot/restore` added; stored in `recording.gates` at `startRecording` time, restored at `playOneRecording`, plus a new `{type:'gate', ccw, t}` move handled by `applyReplayMove`. Original symptom: replay was inheriting the live game's CURRENT gate placement (from a possibly-larger puzzle), so prongs rendered past the smaller replayed grid's edge. Pre-gates recordings (no `rec.gates`) fall through to `Gates.clear()` and replay sans gates.
- **Edge-tile rotation render fix** in `drawAnimatedFrame`: dirty set rebuilt as `[...neighborKeys, ...rotatingKeys]` so rotating tiles iterate LAST in every pass — visible on top of neighbors, not buried under them. Clip = dirty cells PLUS a `~0.22*cellSize` margin around each rotating tile so the 45° rotation overshoot extends into the canvas padding instead of being cropped at the grid boundary. `offCanvas` dim pass also clears the margin so stale dim pixels don't ghost-blend through the composite.
- **Perimeter survives the new margin** — the rotation margin clips into the bottom portion of entry/exit notches (notch length `0.4*cs`, margin `0.22*cs`). Final pass of `drawAnimatedFrame` calls `drawCircuitOutline(w, rim)` inside the same clip; most strokes are no-op (clip filters them) but the notch's affected portion gets redrawn each frame. User preference: perimeter intactness > overshoot visibility in any overlap region (overshoot can be covered where it intersects perimeter).

## 2026-05-20 — Gates feature — first cut

- Each gate has its own random `solutionDir`; all share a global `delta`. Rotate in unison but point in **varied** directions — user: "just because they rotate in sync doesn't mean they'll be pointing in the same direction." Both per-gate `solutionDir` and `delta` randomized at placement so player doesn't start at the gates-solved state. Count formula: `4 + floor(solveCount / 3)`, bumped from the initial `3 + /4` after first playtest.
- Placement runs on the **main thread** after `Maze.loadSnapshot` (NOT in `maze-worker.js`) — keeps `Marathon.getSolvedCount` current even on pre-gen cache hits. `walkFrom` guards with `typeof Gates !== 'undefined'` so worker context doesn't error on the missing module.
- Quad mode **skips** gate placement entirely (v1 decision — quad-corner placement is a future pass). Hints intentionally do NOT consider gate state, per user: "Hints don't need to bother with the gates. The player can see which tile was solved, and be able to recognize whether a gate needs rotating."
- Render gotchas: `drawBeveledPolygon` got an optional `bevelOverride` so the small gate polygons aren't bevel-dominated. `PALETTE.faceAlpha = 1` is required to override the default `TILE_FACE_ALPHA` translucency. Gates aren't tile-keyed, so `canPartial` requires `gateAnims.length === 0` (gate rotations force full draws), AND `drawAnimatedFrame` calls `drawGates` so adjacent rotating tiles don't clear gate pixels via the dirty-cell clip.
- **Open**: gate moves not yet in recording/replay (`{type:'gate', ccw, t}` planned; `snapshotState` needs Gates state for replays to start at the right orientations). Singularity counter is also not yet incremented on gate rotation — needs to match the twin-rotation "one move" treatment.

## 2026-05-19 — Random puzzle progression + pre-gen sync

- **`Marathon.dimsForLevel` is now per-game random**: a `growthSequence` array (one entry per level transition, `'r'` or `'c'`) is rolled lazily by `ensureGrowthSequence(lev)` and reset on `startGame`. Each transition is a 50/50 row-vs-col growth. Replaces the old deterministic 4-step cycle. Lazy + append-only means pre-gen's lookahead calls pin the upcoming choices, so any later call for the same level returns identical dims.
- **Pre-gen sync via `Marathon.upcomingDims(count)`**: game.js's old `nextSize` walk used "grow smaller dim" logic that diverged from Marathon at puzzle 2 (8x8 → 9x8 here vs 8x9 in Marathon). Result: every advance was a cache miss + the worker had to finish the wrong-size in-flight build before starting the right one → "Building puzzle…" delays of a minute+ on bigger grids. Now `fillPreGenQueue` asks Marathon for the actual upcoming dims when Marathon is the active state machine; falls back to nextSize in debug mode.
- **Hurry-on-mismatch safety net was DECLINED** (worker doesn't cancel in-flight builds when a new request arrives — it queues). User wanted any future pre-gen-sync regression to surface as visible "Building puzzle…" rather than being silently masked by an automatic cancel. Don't add it back without that conversation.

## 2026-05-19 — Hint improvements: quad port-equivalence + 10s floor

- **`quadAlreadyPortEquivalent(qr, qc)` in maze.js**: simulates the would-be hint rotation symbolically (each new-position tile = previous-position tile rotated +turns) and checks every position's connections stay the same via the existing `rotationsHaveSamePorts`. If yes → hint would be a chain-no-op → skip the quad. Catches all-STRAIGHT-same-parity quads, rotationally-symmetric ELBOW patterns, cross/straight checkerboards, etc, where 180°/90°/270° rotation moves nothing through the chain.
- **`HINT_PENALTY_MIN_MS = 10000`** in config: marathon.js `onHintUsed` takes `max(timeRemaining × HINT_PENALTY_FRACTION, HINT_PENALTY_MIN_MS)`. Without the floor, the fractional cost trends to 0 as the timer winds down and players can hint-spam to solve a puzzle nearly for free. Player explicitly hit this exploit during playtest.
- **`HINT_PENALTY_FRACTION` bumped 0.25 → 0.33** per playtest tuning. The floor still kicks in when 33% of remaining time falls below 10s.

## 2026-05-19 — Background image: pre-build shuffle + on/off toggle

- **`Render.shuffleBackground()` moved** from `Marathon.onPuzzleReady` (fires AFTER the build completes) to the START of game.js's `startPuzzle` callback (right after Maze.clear + Render.draw). New image is now visible throughout the "Building puzzle…" wait rather than appearing after. `cinematic_bass` SFX still fires from `onPuzzleReady` (when the puzzle is actually visible — semantically distinct).
- **Background Image toggle in Settings popup** — third row alongside Music + SFX. Persisted as `<slug>_setting_backgroundEnabled` (default true). `Settings.setBackgroundEnabled(b)` propagates to `Render.setBackgroundEnabled(b)`. When OFF, body's `backgroundImage` is cleared AND `backgroundColor` is overridden to pure `#000` (otherwise the CSS default `#0a0a2e` dark navy would show, not the literal black the user asked for).
- **`currentBgUrl` tracked in render.js** — toggling off then on re-applies the same image rather than advancing the bag. The bag still advances on every `shuffleBackground()` call regardless of enabled state (so the sequence is consistent across enable/disable cycles); only the DOM application is gated.
- **`Render.init` reads `Settings.isBackgroundEnabled()` pre-paint** so a persisted-disabled state doesn't flash an image on page load. `Settings.init` also re-pushes the state to Render as belt-and-braces for any DOMContentLoaded ordering edge case.
- **i18n** added across all 15 languages. Label is "Background Image" (was briefly "Background" before the rename).

## 2026-05-18 — Leaderboard Watch button alignment

- `#leaderboardEntries li` grid had **4 columns but 5 children** when a Watch button was present, so the button wrapped into a 2nd grid row in the rank (2rem) column — visible as a squished button with the ▶ icon on one line and "Watch" stacked below.
- Fix: bumped grid to **5 columns** (`2rem 1fr auto auto auto`). The 5th column is reserved on EVERY row so buttons align vertically on the far right next to the time column, regardless of which rows have a button. Entries without a button leave the column empty (no cell content needed).
- Added `align-items: center` on the row + `white-space: nowrap` on `.lbWatch` as belt-and-braces against future cramped layouts. Dropped the obsolete `margin-left: 0.5rem` (grid `gap` covers spacing now).

## 2026-05-17 — Music: intro-then-shuffle paradigm (universal rule 7b)

- **`music.js` rewritten** to implement the intro-then-shuffle paradigm (now codified as universal rule 7b in `../../CLAUDE.md`). Two pools from the admin API:
  - `MUSIC_INTRO_LIST_NAME = 'album_intro'` — curated artistic sequence. Plays ONCE per player in position order, then never replays as a sequence.
  - `MUSIC_SHUFFLE_LIST_NAME = 'gameplay'` — random pool that plays forever after the intro. **Falls back to the INTRO list if the SHUFFLE list is empty** (Circuitousness currently has no songs marked under `gameplay` in the admin, so post-intro just shuffles `album_intro`).
- **Per-player intro progress** lives in two localStorage keys: `_introRemaining_v1` (JSON array of song IDs not yet played, shifted one-per-play) and `_introFingerprint_v1` (`|`-joined source IDs). Admin reorders mismatch the fingerprint → restart intro for everyone. Deliberate: "new album release, listen to it again."
- **Queue/history preserved across `Music.stop()`** so songs progress BETWEEN games rather than restarting from #1 on every new game. Only the audio element + `currentSong` reset on stop so the Now Playing UI hides; `queue`, `queuePos`, `history`, `lastPlayedId`, `introRemaining` all persist.
- **`MUSIC_BASE_URL` points at TANTЯO's `blockchainstorm-frontend` `Music/` release** (not a Circuitousness-specific repo). MP3s are shared across games; admin's per-game membership table is the source-of-truth for which songs play where. Saves maintaining a duplicate release for reused songs.
- **Cache key bumped `_songLibrary_v2` → `_songLibrary_v3`** when the cached shape changed from `{songs:[]}` to `{intro:[],shuffle:[]}`. Pre-release, no migration needed.
- **API endpoint**: `${AUTH_API}/api/songs?game=circuitousness` (umbrella API). CORS allowlist on that API now includes `http://localhost:8765` (see umbrella's `app.py` — separate codebase).

## 2026-05-17 — Settings popup + volume controls + Now Playing UI

- **`settings.js` + popup** (HTML/CSS in `index.html` / `styles.css`). TANTЯO-style dark-blue gradient popup with two settings rows: each has `[label] [volume slider 0–100] [mute toggle]`. Persisted to `<slug>_setting_(music|sfx)(Muted|Volume)`. Defaults: unmuted, 0.6.
- **Two trigger buttons**: `#settingsBtn` (menu top-right gear) AND `#hudSettingsBtn` (in-game HUD, just left of QUIT). Both share `handleSettingsClick`. The original "no settings during gameplay" hard guard was REMOVED per user — they accept that opening Settings during a puzzle spends their timer. The hover-rotate animation on the gear is intentional polish.
- **`Music.setVolume(v)` applies live** (mid-song slider drag changes loudness immediately, not on next track). `Sfx.setVolume(v)` only affects future plays — one-shot SFX in flight finish at their original volume.
- **Now Playing UI** (`#nowPlaying`, bottom-right): "♪ NOW PLAYING ♪" label + song name + ⏮⏯⏭ controls. Hidden via `.visible` class toggle, driven by `Music.setOnSongChange((song, paused) => ...)` callback. Wired inline at bottom of `music.js` (tight coupling — not worth a separate module).
- **Skip-prev navigation** uses a `history` array of song IDs; `historyPos` rewinds on prev. `advanceToNext` replays history forward before pulling fresh from the queue, so prev → forward replays the same sequence rather than diverging.

## 2026-05-17 — PWA fixes: favicon SVG + resilient SW precache

- Three intertwined issues fixed in one go: `favicon.ico` was missing (404 + broke SW `cache.addAll` precache — one missing asset rejected the whole batch, leaving the cache empty), four manifest PNG icons were referenced but didn't exist, and the SW failure cascaded into "PWA caching core assets" not actually caching anything.
- **`favicon.svg` created** (gold "C" on dark blue `#0a0a2e` rounded square, 64×64). Inline SVG — no binary authoring required.
- `manifest.json` collapsed from 4 missing PNG entries to 1 SVG entry with `purpose: "any maskable"` — modern browsers accept SVG as install icon.
- `sw.js` precache loop switched from `cache.addAll(CORE_ASSETS)` to per-asset `cache.add()` with try/catch. One missing file now logs a warning but doesn't tank the whole batch. Defensive future-proofing on top of the immediate fix.

## 2026-05-16 — SFX system + click + broken-path flash/fade

- **`audio.js` added**. Per-type shuffle bag (Fisher-Yates, no variant repeats inside a type until exhausted); OR-triggers pick the type uniformly first, then a variant from that type's bag. Single `play()`, `playLoop(key, spec)` / `stopLoop(key)` for the overlap glitch. **Glitch overlap = single variant looped via `Audio.loop`**, NOT a chain through the pool — earlier rotating-chain implementation was wrong per the user. 200ms duck/fade-out on every `play()` so a new sound supersedes anything still playing; the natural `ended` event is processed BEFORE the chain-handoff for loop variants so loop hand-offs don't fade themselves. `click()` synthesized via Web Audio (lazy AudioContext, ~30ms filtered-noise burst with frequency jitter); bypasses ducking pipeline; fires on tile rotations + hint. SFX skipped during replay.
- **Eager preload at module init** — without explicit `audio.load()` even `preload='auto'` is ignored by mobile browsers. ~85 parallel fetches at page load with `PRELOAD_FIRST = ['cinematic_bass', 'applause_long']` priority because those fire first in any session. Bandwidth spike at page load is the trade. **iOS/iPad Safari WILL FAIL** to load — GitHub 302 redirects don't follow in Audio elements (same problem TANTЯO solved via backend proxy). Mirror via backend if iOS support matters.
- **Twin-twist-break SFX rule** (game.js handlePointer). Per-path analysis. A = clicked tile / quad in quad mode; B = partner tile / partner quad (`_twin.partner`). Fire iff: (1) A NOT in lost (player didn't directly break their tile — that's "their choice"), AND (2) `bOldLaneGone` — any pre-walk port-pair through B's cells is missing in post. This is **strictly broader than "B's cell in lost"**: catches the elbow-still-lit case where B's cell stays lit via a different corner but its old chain-corner is gone, orphaning everything past B. AND (3) remainder (lost ∖ bCells) spans ≥ 2 "units" — cells in singular, quads in quad — so single-tile tip-loss doesn't fire.
- **Broken-path visual** = `Render.fadeLanes(entries)` Pass D after lit pass: 200ms FLASH (additive composite via `globalCompositeOperation='lighter'`, overlay alpha 0.7→0) then 1500ms alpha ramp 1→0. Lane entries captured pre-rotation; safe to redraw because A+B are excluded from the fade set (their rotations changed), all other lost cells kept the same tile rotation so the lit-lane geometry is still valid. Cleared on newPuzzle. partial-draw rotation animation is disabled while any fade is alive (palettes vary per frame).
- **Joined-paths flash** (two paths share a lane) — `JOINED_PATH_IDX = -2` sentinel in `buildLitKeys`; `litPalette(-2)` returns time-modulated dark red (450ms period would have been too fast; 900ms reads correctly). Old behavior of overwriting one path's color with another's was confusing.

## 2026-05-16 — HUD redesign: HINT replaces score, timer centered

- **`#hudScore` removed**; `#hudHintBtn` (inside `#hud`) takes its place. Standalone `#hintBtn` (top-right) is now `display: none` in game mode (still used by debug mode). Both buttons share `handleHintClick` in game.js — binding to both is safe because only one is visible per mode.
- **Timer `absolute`-centered**. Pure flex `space-between` was off-center because left items (Singular + Puzzle) are wider than right items (HINT + QUIT). `hudLevel` and `hudHintBtn` are also `absolute`, anchored 10rem off-center for symmetry — fixed offset rather than viewport-relative, intentionally.
- **Gotcha for future animations on `#hudTimer`**: any keyframe that sets `transform` must bundle `translate(-50%, -50%)` or the timer snaps to the top-left anchor and jumps down-right. `timerPenaltyShake` keyframes updated to `translate(calc(-50% ± Xrem), -50%)` to preserve centering during the shake.
- **`[hidden]` specificity fix on `.gameOverNameRow`**: `display: flex` on the class was overriding the browser default `[hidden] { display: none }`, so `gameOverNameRow.hidden = true` for zero-score / non-ranking game-overs had no visible effect. Added `.gameOverNameRow[hidden] { display: none }` to win on specificity.

## 2026-05-16 — Leaderboard auto-jump + offline replay + click-quiet gate

- **After save, transition straight to the leaderboard** with the player's row highlighted (`.lbHighlight` — gold wash + 1.6s pulse animation, runs twice). Match by `id` for server-saved entries (back-end now returns `id` in the submit response); fall back to `(name, solved, totalMs)` triple for local-only entries. `pendingHighlight` cleared in `goToMenu` and `startGame`. Row scroll-into-view'd via `scrollIntoView({block:'center', behavior:'smooth'})` so mid-list highlights aren't lost below the fold.
- **Name input auto-focuses** when the ranking game-over row is un-hidden — cursor lands in the field so the player can type without an intermediate click. Wrapped in try/catch in case the element was detached while the leaderboard fetch was in flight.
- **Replay buttons now work for local-fallback entries**. Local save (back-end unreachable) now stores `events: recordings` + `hasRecording: true` on the entry. Painter shows ▶ Watch on any entry with `(id && hasRecording)` OR `events.length > 0`. New `startReplayWithEvents(events, name)` is the common playback path; `startReplay(scoreId)` fetches from API then hands off. localStorage footprint grows with stored events — IndexedDB if the ~5-10MB cap becomes an issue.
- **Inter-puzzle click-quiet gate (1s)**. Any tap inside the solve-transition popup pushes the gate out another 1s, so a click-cascade from mashing through a solve can't accidentally skip the transition. Canvas taps during transition are swallowed entirely (NOT a way to advance) — only popup clicks count.

## 2026-05-16 — Per-puzzle time formula scales by path count

- **New formula**: `fresh_ms = tile_count × per_tile × N × 1000` and `cap_ms = tile_count × cap_per_tile × N × 1000`. Per-tile constants now `1.5` (singular) / `3` (quad); cap constants `2` / `4`. Cap kept strictly above fresh at every N (2 vs 1.5, 4 vs 3) so banking has headroom regardless of path count — alternative considered (flat cap that clips fresh at high-N) rejected. `timeForPuzzle` / `timeCapForPuzzle` take `pathCount` arg now; both call sites in marathon.js pass `decoded.pathCount`.
- **Old constants** (`TIME_PER_TILE_SINGULAR = 2`, `TIME_CAP_PER_TILE_SINGULAR = 3` etc) are gone — pre-multiplied values replaced by per-path base values.

## 2026-05-03 — Title-O fine-tuning + stale-canvas fix between games

- **Title-O dialed values** (in `title-renderer.js`): `widthFrac: 0.28`, `alpha: 0.33`, `color: '#ff0000'` — found by user via debug-mode sliders. Brackets ended at `0.22em` arms / `0.095em` borders / `padding: 0 0.06em`. Initial C bumped to `1.18em` via `.titleInitial` span.
- **Subtle bug fixed**: `wallThickness()` in render.js was using `GROOVE_FRAC` directly, ignoring the snippet width override. Symptom: the path got wider but the wall geometry didn't move, so the wider channel just bled underneath unchanged walls. Apply the same `snippetMode && snippetWidthFrac` check that the rim drawing uses.
- **`Maze.clear()` added** (zeros grid + entries + highlighted + locked + won, keeps ROWS/COLS/pathCount/quadMode). Called from TWO spots: (1) `TitleRenderer.draw` after first paint when there was no real puzzle to restore, and (2) game.js's `startPuzzle` callback at the top of every new puzzle. Without #2, the previous game's puzzle pixels persisted on the canvas during the next worker build (`Render.draw` with stale grid would just repaint the old puzzle).
- Anton (Google Fonts) reverted — user preferred the original Helvetica Neue / Arial Black / Impact stack.

## 2026-05-03 — Marathon menu title: brackets + system font + Render-snippet O

- All-caps wordmark "CIRCUIT[O]USNESS" wrapped in `.titleFrame` with 4 absolutely-positioned L-shaped `.bracket` spans (viewfinder framing). Brackets are 0.28em with 0.045em borders so they read as small corner accents, not a heavy frame.
- Font: original `Helvetica Neue` / `Arial Black` / `Impact` system stack at weight 900, `font-stretch: condensed`. No external font dep.
- Maze-O is a `<canvas id="titleOCanvas">` painted by `TitleRenderer.draw()` which uses **the actual in-game `Render` module**, not a copy. `Render.renderSnippet(targetCanvas, sizeCss)` (added to render.js) saves Render's canvas/ctx/cellSize/origin/offscreen state, swaps in the title canvas at a fresh layout, calls `drawCore` (with a `snippetMode` flag that skips `drawCircuitOutline`), then restores. `TitleRenderer.draw` snapshots live Maze state, `Maze.loadSnapshot`s a synthetic 2×2 elbow snapshot, calls `renderSnippet`, then restores the saved Maze state.
- Synthetic snapshot: 4 elbows at rotations 1/2/3/0 (TL/TR/BR/BL) so each arc center lands at the 2×2 group's geometric center → the four channels join into a complete circle. Dummy entry/exit are required by `loadSnapshot`'s `updateHighlighted` walk; the entry tile has no N port so the walk returns immediately, leaving `Maze.highlighted` empty (channels render un-lit, as desired).
- First-paint case: when the menu shows before any puzzle has built, `Maze.grid` is null. Title-renderer detects this and skips the snapshot/restore — the synthetic 2×2 stays in Maze until the next `startPuzzle` overwrites it via `newPuzzle`. Harmless (canvas is hidden behind the menu overlay until then).
- Canvas backing-store sized to `displayed × DPR` for crispness. Re-paints on `resize`.

## 2026-05-03 — Click-to-advance between puzzles

- Replaced the `MARATHON.TRANSITION_MS` auto-timeout with a tap-to-advance — players were missing the win cue and feeling rushed. Popup now stays up until the player taps anywhere (popup or canvas).
- New popup line: pulsing "TAP TO CONTINUE" hint (`#solveContinue`, `solveContinuePulse` keyframe at 1.4s).
- `Marathon.advance()` is the public API; called by:
  - The popup's own click listener (`solveTransitionEl.addEventListener('click', advance)`)
  - `handlePointer` in game.js when `Marathon.isInTransition()` — so canvas taps advance instead of rotating tiles (which would just break the gold path the player is looking at).
- `Marathon.isInTransition()` exposed for game.js gating. Hint button also bails when in transition (player has already won, hint would just lock a tile on a puzzle they're about to leave behind).
- Removed `MARATHON.TRANSITION_MS` from config.js (no longer used).

## 2026-05-03 — Hint penalty in Marathon

- Each successful hint now lops `MARATHON.HINT_PENALTY_FRACTION` (0.25 = 25%) off the player's remaining time. Tunable in config.js.
- Visual: timer cell shakes (`timerPenaltyShake` keyframe) + flashes red (`timerPenaltyFlash`), and a `−Xs` float appears just **below** the timer, drifts upward, and fades over ~1.1s. (Was originally above the timer, but the timer sits at the very top of the viewport — text above it slides off-screen behind the menubar where the player can't read it.) So the cost is unmissable even if the player wasn't looking at the timer when they tapped Hint.
- `Marathon.onHintUsed()` is called from game.js's hint button handler, gated on `Marathon.isPlaying()` so debug-mode hint and between-puzzle hint clicks don't fire it. No-op if `inTransition` (between puzzles).
- Skipped if `Maze.hint()` returned null (no hint available — usually means the player has already solved everything that needs hinting).
- **Subtle DOM gotcha**: `renderTimer` originally set `hudTimer.textContent`, which wiped any child elements every 100ms tick — including the float. Wrapped the displayed time in `<span class="hudTimerVal">` so renderTimer only updates the inner span; the float (a sibling) survives across ticks.

## 2026-05-03 — Marathon polish: 5×5 floor, 3-path retry bump, inter-puzzle transition

- `MARATHON.MIN_DIM` 4 → 5. The 4×4 with 3+ paths case had a notable failure rate even with retries — geometry doesn't always fit. 5×5 is essentially always feasible for 1-3 paths.
- 3-path mode `maxAttempts` 150 → 400 (in both quad and non-quad init branches in maze.js). Belt-and-braces — at 5×5 succeeds in a handful of attempts, but big enough to cover edge cases.
- **Inter-puzzle transition**: solving used to jump straight to the next puzzle, which was disorienting if the player wasn't watching closely. Now `Marathon.onSolve` sets an `inTransition` flag, stops the timer, shows a centered popup with `Puzzle N solved!` + `+Xs banked · next puzzle: M:SS`, and defers `startNextPuzzle` by `MARATHON.TRANSITION_MS` (1800ms). The "banked" amount = how much of the leftover actually carried (capped contribution); shows `Time cap reached · next puzzle: M:SS` when 0. Quit/menu paths call `clearTransition()` so the deferred advance doesn't fire after a state change.
- The HUD score updates immediately at solve time (within `onSolve`) rather than waiting for `startNextPuzzle` — keeps the HUD in sync with the popup so when the popup hides, the score doesn't suddenly jump.

## 2026-05-03 — Marathon game mode + debug-mode URL gate

- **`?debug=true`** keeps the existing free-form playground (sliders, manual progression, win banner). Default (no param) is the new Marathon game flow. Gate is set on `<html>` via inline script in index.html before the stylesheet kicks in (no debug-flash). CSS toggles `.mode-debug` / `.mode-game` blocks.
- **8 game types**: regular/quad × 1..4 paths. `MARATHON.TYPES = ['r1'..'r4', 'q1'..'q4']`.
- **Puzzle progression**: starts at `MARATHON.MIN_DIM` (4 logical), grows by `GROWTH_PER_LEVEL` (1) each puzzle, capped at `MAX_DIM_REGULAR` (15) or `MAX_DIM_QUAD` (8). "Tile" = visible/interactive unit, so quad 4 logical = 16 tiles even though it's 8×8 sub-tiles.
- **Timer**: per puzzle = `TIME_PER_TILE_SINGULAR` (2s) or `TIME_PER_TILE_QUAD` (4s) × tile count. Carry-over leftover time to next puzzle, then capped on the **total** (carry + fresh combined) at next puzzle's `TIME_CAP_PER_TILE_SINGULAR` (3s) or `TIME_CAP_PER_TILE_QUAD` (6s) × tile count — so effective max banking is `(cap − fresh) × tiles` = 1s/tile singular, 2s/tile quad. All values in `MARATHON` const in config.js — easy to retune.
- **Critical**: `startNextPuzzle` calls `stopTimer()` so the clock pauses during the next puzzle's worker build. Restarts in `Marathon.onPuzzleReady()` (called from game.js after `await newPuzzle()`). Without this, the player loses 1-3s per puzzle to build time.
- **8 local leaderboards**: `localStorage[PROJECT_SLUG + '_lb_<type>']`, top 10 each. Sort: descending `solved`, ascending `totalMs` as tiebreaker. Game-over screen shows projected rank if score would land in top 10. Save button disabled when `solved === 0` (no zero-score entries).
- **Hooks game.js → Marathon**: `Marathon.onSolve()` called from `refresh()` on `Maze.won` rising edge (instead of showing the win banner); `Marathon.onPuzzleReady()` called at end of `startPuzzle` callback so timer anchors to "player can see puzzle".
- **Hooks Marathon → game.js**: `startPuzzle({ rows, cols, pathCount, quadMode })` updates Maze config + invalidates pre-gen cache + Render.refit + newPuzzle in one go. `quit()` clears banner / cancels replay for clean idle state.
- **Hint button**: still works in Marathon (lets player burn time to advance). Repositioned to bottom-right in game mode so it doesn't overlap the top HUD.
- Backend leaderboards + puzzle-of-the-day are deferred — local-only for now per spec.

## 2026-05-01 — Partial-redraw during rotation animation

- Old `draw()` iterated all ROWS×COLS tiles in three passes (dim channel, lit channel, walls) every frame, regardless of how few tiles were actually animating. On 15×15 a full draw measured ~31ms — below 60 fps and visibly choppy.
- Refactored: extracted body to `drawCore()` (full draw, used for initial loads, hint pulses, dramatic flash, resize, and the first frame after a rotation kick-off). Added `drawAnimatedFrame()` that only re-renders animating tiles + their 8 neighbors, clipped to the bounding box (with `0.3*cellSize` margin for the 45°-rotation overshoot). Hoisted `buildLitKeys`, `renderTileDim/Lit/Wall`, `laneIsOpposite`, `laneIsVertical` so both paths share them.
- `animateRotationAt` sets a `needsFullDraw` flag so the FIRST frame after a rotation kick-off is a `drawCore` (the canvas still showed PRE-rotate state, so the partial-redraw region needs a fresh backdrop). Subsequent frames during the same animation use `drawAnimatedFrame`.
- `draw()` falls back to `drawCore` whenever a hint pulse, dramatic flash, or pulse-driving state is active (those vary palettes globally each frame). Rotation-only animations get the fast path.
- Clip path is the **union of dirty cell rects**, not their bounding box. **Why:** twin-pair rotations have two animating tiles that can be far apart on the grid. A bbox-clip would clear the entire gap between them, and since `drawAnimatedFrame` only redraws the dirty cells (not the gap), the body bg would show through the canvas as a transparent void. Per-cell clipping leaves non-dirty cells intact.
- Measured on 15×15: drawCore = 31.6ms, partial draw ≈ 1.9ms. Still well under the 16ms 60fps budget.

## 2026-05-01 — Per-side overlap-interval clustering for path rings

- `drawCircuitOutline` previously treated each perimeter side as a single shared resource: any pair whose route touched that side bumped everyone else's rank, even if the routes occupied disjoint segments. With 4 paths this routinely pushed an "alone on its segment" path several rings outward for no visual reason.
- Fix: `computePathRoute` now returns `sideSpans` — perimeter-coord intervals per side that the route actually covers. `drawCircuitOutline` builds one cluster per maximal-overlap group of intervals on each side (sort by lo, merge while next.lo ≤ curHi). `offsetFor(pairIdx, side)` uses the *cluster* size + rank instead of the side-wide usage list. Disjoint intervals on the same side both get baseLen now.
- Min path length floor `MIN_PATH_CELLS = 4` enforced in path 1's loop, `tryAddPath`, and the backtracking `place` (lane count for `pathIdx` must be ≥ 4 to accept a candidate). Tiny entry-near-exit stubs are rejected.

## 2026-05-01 — 4th path (orange) wired in + backtracking placement

- **4-path mode added**: slider max bumped to 4. Plumbing: `entry4`/`exit4` state in maze.js, `setPathCount` accepts 4, snapshot/restoreState/loadSnapshot carry the new fields, `updateHighlighted` walks/tags it as `pathIdx=3`, public API exposes `entry4`/`exit4`. game.js `setPathCount` validation extended.
- **Orange palette**: `LIT_ORANGE = '#FFA040'` (HI `#ffd6a8`, LO `#7a3a05`) in render.js. `litPalette(3)` returns it. `drawCircuitOutline` `pairs` array pushes `{ entry4, exit4, pathIdx: 3 }` when present. `padFracFor(4) = 1.15` (extra room for a 4th perimeter ring). `pathsWon` now `[false×4]`.
- **Backtracking placement for 4-path mode** (the linear placement boxed path 4 out almost every time): `buildPuzzle` branches on `pathCount === 4` into a recursive `place(currentLanes, pathIdx, used)` that tries `MAX_BT_PAIRS=3` endpoint configs × `MAX_BT_ROUTES=5` DFS routes per level, capped by a shared `budget.steps=100` total `generateLanes` calls. On success returns the all-4-paths lane map; on budget exhaustion falls back to the deepest partial via a `snap()` snapshot that records best.depth + the entry/exit pairs at that depth. **Why:** greedy DFS for paths 2/3 picks routes that disconnect path-4-feasible regions; backtracking rolls back a path and tries a different (pair, route) when a deeper level fails. Outer init `maxAttempts` for 4-path mode dropped to 25 (was 80) since each attempt is now thorough.
- **Verified empirically**: 6/6 4-path builds succeeded on 8×8 in ~20s total; 12×12 also reliably places 4 paths.
- **Tried + reverted earlier**: scaling `tryAddPath` retries by `pathIdx` (12 + pathIdx*6) ballooned per-init cost beyond 30s. Reverted to flat MAX_PATH_GEN_TRIES=12 before adding backtracking.

- **Quad twins** (whole-quad pairing in quad mode): `assignQuadTwins` pairs entire 2×2 quads via `TWIN_COLORS`. Each sub-tile in a paired quad gets `_twin = { partner: "qr*2,qc*2", color }` referencing the partner quad's TL. `scrambleQuads` scrambles twin pairs with the SAME turn count (otherwise lockstep can never bring both to scramble=0 simultaneously). `Maze.rotate`/`applyHintAt`/`hint` quad branches all cascade to the partner quad. `flashTwinPair` in quad mode adds all 8 sub-tiles (4 per quad) to `dramaticPulseTiles` so the WHOLE quad flashes, not just the clicked sub-tile.
- **Hint port-equivalence fix**: `tileShouldSkip` uses `rotationsHaveSamePorts(type, current, solution)` instead of strict `rotation === _solution`. **Why:** STRAIGHT tiles are 2-symmetric — rotations 0/2 and 1/3 expose identical port sets, so a tile at rotation 2 with solution 0 is *visually* correct and the player would rightly object to it being hinted. Cross is 4-symmetric (always correct), elbow has no symmetry (strict equality).
- **Hint twin-pair conservative skip**: skip the pair (both members) if *either* tile in the pair has `tileShouldSkip = true`. **Why:** lockstep rotation means hinting one disturbs the partner. If the partner was at solution, the hint moves it AND locks it — leaving it at the wrong rotation, locked, making the puzzle unwinnable. Skip the whole pair instead. Same logic in quad mode at the quad level.
- **`effectivePalette` layer ordering**: `_playerLocked` palette override must run BEFORE the dramatic-pulse blend, otherwise the white player-lock palette overwrites the pulse blend every frame and the flash becomes invisible. Order: twin/hint base → hint pulse → **player-lock white** → dramatic pulse → return.
- **Hint rotation animates**: `applyHintAt` returns `{ turns }`, `hint()` propagates it, `Render.animateRotationAt(r, c, ccw, turns)` accepts an N-turn count and computes `deltaRad = (±π/2) * turns`. Replay's hint branch also passes turns. Duration stays constant at 200ms regardless of turns — a 270° spin in 200ms feels fine; could tune later if it reads as rushed.

## 2026-04-30 — Long-press to lock + first-time toast

- **Long-press (500ms) toggles a player-lock** on a tile (or whole quad in quad mode). `Maze.togglePlayerLock(r, c)` sets `_playerLocked` on the tile(s); `rotate()` rejects if any cell in the would-be-rotated set has it. **Why 500ms:** user dropped from initial 1000ms — felt sluggish. Mouse-move > 12px during the press cancels (so dragging isn't an accidental lock).
- **Twin pairs lock together** (rotating one rotates both — leaving one half movable would be inconsistent). **In quad mode** all 4 sub-tiles lock together (any of the 4 having `_playerLocked` blocks the quad's rotation).
- **Hint-locked tiles can't be player-locked on top** — `togglePlayerLock` no-ops when `locked.has(k)` is already true. The hint's red palette already signals lock; layering player-lock white on red would muddy the signal.
- **Lock visual is color shift only, not opacity.** Tried fully-opaque face first, user rejected — color shift to `LOCK_FACE_COLOR` (default `#ffffff`, debug-tunable) at normal `TILE_FACE_ALPHA` is enough. The face color override sits in `effectivePalette` after all other palette mutations so it's the final word.
- **First-time toast** "Tile locked — press and hold again to unlock" shows once per browser, gated by `localStorage[PROJECT_SLUG + '_lock_hint_shown']`. 3.5s auto-dismiss. Lives in `#lockToast` at `top: 70%` so it doesn't collide with the centered win/building banners. i18n key: `lock.firstHint`.

## 2026-04-30 — 2-path canonical check + animation default

- **2-path mode now runs the canonical check** (per-path BFS-shortest with the OTHER path's exclusive cells blocked must be ≥ intended length, plus rotation-cost shortcut detection). 3-path stays at "first valid build wins" — adding canonical on top would compound the already-tight retry budget. **Why:** the prior "shortcuts are unlikely with multi-path density" rationale is probabilistic, not a proof, and breaks down on sparse 2-path puzzles.
- **"Shortcut" definition for 2-path**: alternate paths that don't traverse cells exclusively on the OTHER path. Shared cross tiles (lanes from both paths) are NOT blocked — each path is entitled to its own lane through the cross. The user explicitly chose this simplification over a joint-path canonical (which would also have to verify the alt leaves the other path solvable).
- **Cost**: 2-path canonical generation is ~10s on 8×8, ~18s on 10×10 in the dev preview. Pre-gen worker absorbs this off-thread; lookahead cache normally hides it from the player. **Tunable knobs:** `maxAttempts = 24` (outer retry) and `smart < 6` (smartReroll iterations per attempt) in the `pathCount === 2` branch of `init()`.
- **8×8 2-path is on the edge** — sometimes succeeds, sometimes the smartReroll loop runs out and falls back to a non-canonical 2-path snapshot. Acceptable for now; if it's perceptibly bad, raise the knobs above or relax `minPathLength` for 2-path.
- **Animate Rotations now defaults ON** (`ANIMATE_ROTATIONS = true` in render.js, `<input checked>` in index.html). Was off when first added.

## 2026-04-30 — Quad Tiles, rotation animation, control polish

- **Quad Tiles** (debug toggle): generates at 2× dims, quad rotates as a unit (4-cycle sub-tile positions + each sub-tile's own rotation +1). Skips canonical-uniqueness check (coupled sub-tile rotations → check over-rejects) and twins. `scrambleQuads` tracks `quadScramble[qr][qc]` (0..3 CW offset) so `hint()` can pick non-solved quads. `fixDeadEnds` iterates filler tiles: cross with exactly 1 inner-port dead-end → straight on the still-connected lane; else outward-facing elbow. Pre-gen cache key suffixed `Q`/`q` so cross-mode entries don't bleed.
- **Animate Rotations** (debug toggle): 200ms rAF. Maze state mutates on click; animation visually counters from -90° back to 0°. **`setTimeout` safety-net** at duration+30ms forces a final identity draw — on large grids the rAF chain drops to 27-48ms intervals (vs 16ms budget) and can leave a residual-tilt frame on screen if we rely only on rAF.
- **Open issue:** large-grid rendering is sluggish (~11ms/frame on 14×14 vs 16ms rAF budget). Per-tile passes (3 of them, each iterating ROWS×COLS) are the bottleneck. Not yet addressed — possible follow-up: cache per-tile path objects, reduce per-frame allocations, or batch fillRects.
- **Race fix in `Render.draw()`**: early-return if `grid.length !== Maze.ROWS`. `Maze.setDimensions` updates ROWS/COLS immediately but `loadSnapshot` lags; rapid-fire debug-panel toggles let a refit/draw fire in that gap and walk past the still-old grid → TypeError. Symptoms: "Building puzzle…" stuck banner with crash in console.
- **Control swaps + elbow bevel order**: mouse left=CCW, right=CW, touch=CW (touch default matches secondary-mouse). Touch handler `preventDefault`s to suppress synthetic click, so click handler is mouse-only. Elbow walls now draw arc bevels FIRST and straight bevels LAST (`arcEdges` param to `drawBeveledPolygon`) — fixes asymmetric SEAM_OVERLAP where the arc was visibly poking past one of the two arc-end straight edges.

## 2026-04-28 — Leaderboard / time-attack design (planned, not implemented)

- **Shape**: endless ladder, score = level reached, tiebreaker = cumulative solve time (lower is better). Starts at 4×4, grids grow each level. Fixed per-level timer (~100 sec). Game over when timer hits 0 mid-puzzle.
- **Banked time carryover**: unused time on a level rolls into the next level's clock. Creates strategic depth (race early, cushion for late). Apply a **soft cap** at ~2× per-level base so stockpiling doesn't trivialize mid-game. Without a cap, skilled players coast.
- **Three leaderboards by path count (1/2/3)** = three difficulty tiers. Path count is the genuinely-orthogonal difficulty axis (different cognitive style). Twin density still scales with grid size for visual variety, but is NOT a difficulty axis (we established twins don't shrink the search space — partner is filler, irrelevant rotation). Don't market "more twins = harder."
- **Hints in ladder mode cost banked time** (~15 sec each). Forces strategic spend; at the bank cap, hints are "free" (would lose overflow anyway) but deep in a hard level they're a real sacrifice.
- **Anti-cheat**: time-based JS leaderboards are trivially cheatable (pause timer in devtools, edit score). Design the submission payload NOW to include the seed + rotation sequence so server-side replay validation can be added later without breaking old scores. Don't have to build validation day-one but don't lock yourself out of it.
- **Rejected**: "frozen tiles at random rotation" as a difficulty axis — breaks canonical-uniqueness invariant (puzzle becomes unsolvable, or requires allowing multiple solutions which is a different game).

## 2026-04-28 — HINT pulse + twin-pair dramatic flash

- **Tile face opacity default raised to 0.74** (was 0.5). `TILE_FACE_ALPHA` in render.js + matching slider default in index.html.
- **HINT pulse**: hint-locked tiles (`tile._hinted`) breathe red — `±18% brightness, 1500ms period` (`HINT_PULSE_AMP`/`HINT_PULSE_PERIOD_MS`). `tile._hinted = true` is set in `Maze.hint()` on the picked tile only — its twin partner (if any) just gets locked, not `_hinted`.
- **Twin-of-hint visual**: when a hint locks a tile that has a twin, the partner shows its own twin-color FACE with red BEVELS (border) — `effectivePalette()` `isTwinOfHint` branch in render.js. The user explicitly didn't want the partner to flood red.
- **Dramatic flash**: clicking a hint-locked tile (or its twin partner) → `Render.flashTwinPair(r, c)` → 600ms loud red pulse on BOTH tiles in the pair (mirror, so the lock relationship reads at a glance). Triggered from game.js `handlePointer` rejection branch. Animation loop is rAF-driven, only running while `shouldKeepAnimating()` returns true.
- **Dramatic flash uses brightness boost on top of red blend**, not just blend. First version only blended `LOCKED_PALETTE` over the existing palette, which was invisible on the hint primary (already full red — red-on-red = no change). User reported "I don't see its twin flashing in unison". Fix: apply +60% brightness scale at peak in addition to the red blend, so already-red tiles still pop. Verified: hint-tile bevel brightness +94 peak, partner-tile inner +35, both tracking the same 3-wave pattern over 600ms.
- **Each twin pair = one path tile + one filler tile** by design ([maze.js:996-997](maze.js:996)). So when `hint()` picks a path-side twin, its partner (the filler-side twin) gets the partner-of-hint visual (twin-color face + red bevels). All branches are reachable — twins on the solution path are normal. Verified the flash itself works (delta +93/+84 redness on pair, 0 elsewhere).

## 2026-04-28 — Per-side perimeter offsets for multi-path

- **Each path's polyline now uses a different offset per perimeter SIDE** (`render.js:offsetFor`). When a path is alone on a side it stays at `baseLen`; on shared sides it joins a tight cluster with other paths. User complaint that motivated this: pink path was jutting far out on the left even though no other path was there — a single per-polyline offset can't accommodate that.
- **`computePathRoute(entry, exit)`** returns each path's traversed sides set + corner sequence, computed with a constant `baseLen` so routing decisions are independent of the per-side offsets used for rendering. Per-side corner positions in `drawCircuitOutlinePair` use the offset of each adjacent side, so corners auto-bend when offsets differ on neighboring sides — no diagonal-step rendering needed, just clean L-corners at different distances per side.
- **`step = stroke + 2`, NOT `halfStroke + 2`** — first attempt made strokes overlap by `halfStroke`. Ring CENTERS must be at least one full stroke width apart for the strokes themselves not to overlap. Adjacent ranks separated by `step` give a 2px gap.
- **Shift-everyone-outward, not clamp-inner**: when the innermost ring's symmetric position would sit inside the grid edge, shift ALL rings outward by the same amount (`shift = max(0, minR - innerPos)`). Initially I clamped only the inner ring, which broke symmetric spacing — adjacent gaps became unequal (visible touching between innermost and middle). Shifting everyone preserves uniform `step` between ranks.
- **`PAD_FRAC` still scales with pathCount** (1/2/3 → 0.7/0.8/1.0). Even with the tight cluster, 3 paths sharing a side need padding ≈ baseLen + step + halfStroke = ~0.8 cellSize for the outermost ring's outer edge to fit inside the canvas. `Render.refit()` is called on path-count change so cellSize recomputes.

## 2026-04-28 — Three-path mode + dynamic ring padding

- **Generalized pathCount** (1/2/3) replaces the old `TWO_PATHS_MODE` boolean. `Maze.setPathCount(n)` is the new API; `setTwoPathMode(b)` kept as a back-compat alias mapping to `setPathCount(b ? 2 : 1)`. Wire goes maze ↔ worker ↔ game ↔ index.html slider; cache key suffix and `preGenSize.pathCount` carry the integer.
- **buildPuzzle drops failed extras silently** instead of returning false. Prior version returned false if path 2/3 couldn't fit, which caused a silent worker hang (grid stayed null → assignTwins crashed). Now path 1 always succeeds → we always have a valid grid; init's outer loop tracks the BEST snapshot (most paths placed) across retries and ships that. User can get fewer paths than requested if the grid is too tight, but never a hang.
- **Path 1 cap scaled by pathCount**: `path1Cap = Math.floor(pathCap / pathCount)` in multi-path mode. Without this, path 1 still uses 50% of cells and there's no room for path 2/3.
- **`maxAttempts = 60` for pathCount=3** in init's multi-path branch. 8×8 with 3 paths needs many random retries (success rate ~60-80% per attempt). Worst-case fresh build ~9 seconds. Acceptable because pre-gen runs in background.
- **PAD_FRAC scales with pathCount**: 0.7 / 0.8 / 1.0 for 1 / 2 / 3 paths. Multi-path needs more canvas padding to stack 2-3 rings without clipping or overlapping the grid edge. Implemented as `padFracFor(pathCount)` in render.js. **`Game.setPathCount` calls `Render.refit()`** because cellSize depends on PAD_FRAC — without refit, the canvas wouldn't resize when toggling.
- **Ring distribution for multi-path** is now "evenly between minR and maxR" instead of "centered around baseLen with fixed step". This guarantees rings don't clip/overlap regardless of stroke width or grid size. minR = halfStroke + 2 (just clear of grid), maxR = padding - halfStroke - 2 (just inside canvas). Single-path still uses `baseLen` directly to preserve the original look.
- **Color scheme finalized**: path 0 green, path 1 blue, path 2 pink — each turns gold individually when its walk reaches its exit. `Maze.pathsWon = [bool, bool, bool]`; `Maze.won` is the AND of all (banner only on full completion). LIT_PINK constants in render.js mirror the green/blue palette structure.

## 2026-04-28 — Two-path mode + per-path coloring + tile face alpha

- **Two-path mode** (`Maze.setTwoPathMode`, debug-panel checkbox): generates puzzles with two independent entry/exit pairs that may share cells via cross tiles. The user's argument was that two paths constrain the grid enough to make shortcuts improbable, so `init()` skips the canonical check entirely in two-path mode (`if (TWO_PATHS_MODE) { ...first valid build wins...; return }`). Generation is much faster for big grids than strict single-path. Worker message includes `twoPaths` flag and echoes it back; main checks both response dims AND mode against `preGenSize` so stale-mode snapshots get discarded.
- **Lane tagging for path coloring**: `generateLanes(seedLanes, pathIdx)` tags each lane it adds with its path index. buildPuzzle calls path 1 with idx 0, path 2 with idx 1. When filling the grid, single-lane tiles get `_path = 0|1`; cross tiles get `_crossLanes = [[a,b,p],...]`. Render uses these for hint-locked pre-emptive lighting so each lane gets its own color even before the connecting chain reaches it.
- **Per-path win**: `Maze.pathsWon = [won1, won2]` exposes individual completion state. `Maze.won` is still the AND of both (so the "Path connected" banner only shows on full completion, matches existing UX). Renderer's `litPalette(pathIdx)` returns gold for the just-completed path independent of the other — path 1 turns gold while path 2 is still blue, then path 2 catches up.
- **Color scheme**: path 0 = green→gold (`LIT_GREEN_*` already existed), path 1 = blue→gold (new `LIT_BLUE_*` constants in render.js, no debug sliders yet — add if user wants to tune blue).
- **Pre-gen cache key includes mode suffix** (`"5x4,1"` vs `"5x4,2"`) so toggling never serves the wrong-mode puzzle. `Game.setTwoPathMode(b)` calls `invalidatePreGen()` and forces a fresh build of the current dims.
- **Tile face opacity slider** (`TILE_FACE_ALPHA`, default 0.5): inner tile-face fill drawn with `ctx.globalAlpha`; bevel trapezoids stay opaque so the bevel silhouette stays crisp. User wanted the starfield to show through tile centers.
- **Known issue, unfixed**: in two-path mode, if buildPuzzle fails for ALL `MAX_INIT_ATTEMPTS` (e.g. very small grid where path 1 occupies every cell, leaving no room for path 2), `grid` stays null and `assignTwins()` crashes. Worker freezes silently — main shows "building Nx N (...s)" forever. Workaround for now: avoid two-path on grids smaller than 5×5 (the user's progression starts at 4×4, so this matters). Real fix: fall back to single-path when all two-path attempts fail.

## 2026-04-28 — Pre-gen lookahead cache + hurry-cancel

- **Replaced single-slot pre-gen with a lookahead Map** (`PREGEN_LOOKAHEAD = 3` in `game.js`). After each puzzle loads, `fillPreGenQueue()` walks forward in the progression and queues the first missing size; each worker response chains into the next one. `newPuzzle` consumes from `preGenCache` by `"r,c"` key. Cache stays populated even when a fast hint-solver advances — buffer drains one entry per puzzle but refills as builds complete.
- **Hurry signal** for the catch-up case. `Maze.setHurry()` flips a flag init() checks at every attempt boundary, bailing with `bestState` (or current attempt's grid). Worker handler in `maze-worker.js` calls it on `{type:'hurry'}`. `newPuzzle` posts hurry only when worker is in-flight on the wanted dims; fresh builds (initial load, slider jump) just send a normal generate request.
- **Removed always-on leniency** (`skipSmartRerolls`/`skipCapShrink` past 100/200 tiles). Strict generation runs always now. Lookahead gives enough lead time even at 14×14+; hurry is the only path that can ship a sub-canonical puzzle, and only when player actually caught up. The user explicitly preferred this over global leniency.
- **Banner threshold = 500ms** with split text: "Loading…" for in-flight wait, "Building puzzle…" for fresh builds. Tiny grids never flicker; only legit long waits surface a message.
- **Debug-panel pre-gen indicator**: `Cache: 5x4 5x5 6x5 | building 7x6 (1.9s)` polls every 200ms. Shows every cached size + any in-flight build with elapsed seconds, so it's obvious when pre-gen is keeping up vs catching up.
- **maze-worker.js cache-busts its own `importScripts('maze.js')`** by reading `?t=` off its own URL. Without this, even with no-cache headers + cache-busted worker URL, a freshly-spawned worker can still latch onto a stale cached `maze.js`.
- **File:// fallback** in `ensureWorker()`: try/catches `new Worker()` (Workers can't load from file://); on failure, sets `workerAvailable=false` and `newPuzzle` builds via main-thread `Maze.init()` instead of waiting on a non-existent worker. Page works without dev server, just without pre-gen.

## 2026-04-28 — Progression mode + Web Worker pre-gen + canonical-path fix

- **Game is now a progression**: starts at 4×4, advances on win to next size by alternating which dim grows (4×4→5×4→5×5→6×5→…). Default ROWS/COLS in `maze.js` are 4. Win-banner click / N-key triggers `Game.newPuzzle()` with no args; advance only happens when `lastWon` is true. Manual slider sets explicit dims (no advance). `syncDebugSliders()` mirrors auto-progressed size back into the debug panel.
- **Pre-gen via Web Worker** (`maze-worker.js`): worker has its own private Maze instance and builds the next-progression size in parallel while the player solves the current one. Main thread loads via `Maze.snapshotState()` / `Maze.loadSnapshot()` (now publicly exposed). Worker is the sole maze builder when available — main never calls `Maze.init()` directly. **File:// fallback**: `ensureWorker()` try/catches construction; on failure it builds on main thread via `Maze.init()`. The banner is only shown if a fresh build (no pre-gen, no in-flight match) takes >500ms; pre-gen-ready transitions are silent.
- **Canonical-path constraint** (`shortestPossiblePathLength() >= intended`): old silent-fallback bug ("solved in 15 cells while HINT showed 40-cell intended") fixed by combining (a) hard cap on intended path at 50% of grid cells — beyond this the player can route through path tiles via free rotation, unsatisfiable; (b) heavy elbow bias on filler tiles (`pickFillerType` → 65% elbow / 20% straight / 15% cross); (c) `smartReroll` that walks the BFS shortest path and changes filler types to break the connection (opposite-pair shortcut → ELBOW, adjacent-pair → STRAIGHT); (d) outer pathCap-shrink loop that drops the cap if even smart rerolls can't satisfy; (e) `bestState` snapshot tracking longest canonical attempt across all tries. Stress-tested: 0 fails / 50 generations.
- **Async generation with yields**: `Maze.init` is async; `await yieldToBrowser()` between attempts so the browser can paint and process events on big grids (15×15 takes ~30s but no "page unresponsive" dialog). `Game.newPuzzle` is async, debug-panel slider routes through `rebuildGrid → Game.newPuzzle` and tracks a `pendingSize` so rapid slider drag collapses to the latest desired size.
- **Dev workflow gotchas**:
  - `index.html` has a `document.write` cache-buster (lines 75–85) that appends `?t=Date.now()` to script srcs so iterative edits are picked up — **MUST be reverted to plain `<script src>` tags before /release**.
  - `.claude/dev-server.py` is a custom Python server that adds `Cache-Control: no-cache` headers; `launch.json` uses `py .claude/dev-server.py 8765` (not `python -m http.server`). Without no-cache headers the browser sticks to old maze.js indefinitely once cached.
  - File:// has three known-failures: manifest CORS-blocked, SW registration fails ("URL protocol 'null' not supported"), Worker construction throws SecurityError. None are bugs — fallback handles Worker; the others don't affect gameplay.
- **Open**: `gridWSlider`/`gridHSlider` `max=15` will be exceeded by a long progression run (slider clamps visually, actual game grows past).

## 2026-04-26 — Polygon-bevel prototype for path walls (bevel-test.html)

- **Why isolated, not in `render.js` yet:** adding beveled inner edges to path channels has stalled the session multiple times; user asked for a fresh polygon prototype instead of another retrofit onto the stroke + `destination-out` pipeline. Existing strokes are single-color and physically can't reproduce the outer tile's directional light/shadow.
- **Approach:** treat each tile as a 3×3 grid of segments and draw walls as beveled polygons via inset-polygon offset + one trapezoid per edge — same shape language as the existing outer-tile bevel, generalized to L-shapes including concave corners. Helpers in the file: `insetPolygon`, `drawBeveledPolygon`, `beveledRect`.
- **Tile decomposition:** Cross = 4 outer-corner squares. Straight = two 3-wide bars. L = 5-segment outer-L polygon (one shape, with one concave corner) + one 1×1 inner-corner square on the opposite side of the bend.
- **Next step (only on user OK):** port the polygon-bevel helpers into `render.js` to replace the stroke-based un-lit-channel rendering. Earlier estimate was ~80–120 lines. Do NOT try to bolt the bevel onto the stroke pipeline — that path keeps stalling the session.
- **Saved a feedback memory** for the stall pattern (`~/.claude/projects/.../memory/feedback_beveled_paths_stall.md`) so future sessions don't loop on the stroke retrofit again.

## 2026-04-25 — Starfield background + un-lit grooves at α=0.42

- **Image is a CSS body background, NOT painted into the canvas.** URL lives in `BACKGROUND_IMAGE_URL` (config.js) and is injected at init via `document.body.style.backgroundImage` so config.js stays the single source of truth. styles.css already has `background-size: cover` etc. Canvas itself is transparent (`clearRect` at draw start, no fillRect) so the body bg shows through padding and through translucent grooves.
- **Tile bevels stay fully opaque — only un-lit grooves are translucent.** First attempt globally dimmed bevels too; user wanted just the unlit paths.
- **Cross-tile gotcha (the reason for the offscreen canvas):** stroking the two lanes of an un-lit cross *directly at α=0.42* stacks alpha at the intersection (1−0.58² ≈ 0.66), so the cross center looks visibly denser than the rest of the path. Fix: render un-lit rim+dark to an offscreen canvas at α=1 (same-color overlaps land identically, no stacking), then a single `drawImage(offCanvas, 0, 0)` onto main at α=0.42. Pass C (lit) then paints rim+lit on main at α=1. `DIM_ALPHA = 0.42` constant in render.js.
- **Lit pass skips drawing dark.** `strokeLitLayer`'s `lo` width = dark width, so dark under lit is invisible — only rim + lit needed.
- **Service worker precaches the image** via `fetch(url, { mode: 'no-cors' })` + `cache.put` because cross-origin opaque responses fail `cache.addAll`'s `response.ok` check. EXTERNAL_ASSETS list in sw.js. Precache runs on install — only kicks in after `/rel` bumps APP_VERSION and the new SW activates. Until then the image fetches directly from GitHub.
- **Image source:** GitHub Releases asset on `crdeardorff74-commits/circuitousness-front-end`, tag `Images`, file `level1.jpg`. To swap or add levels, upload to that release and update `BACKGROUND_IMAGE_URL` (or generalize to a list keyed by level).

## 2026-04-25 — DPR fix: render in device pixels

- **The persistent "thin lines at tile boundaries inside path channels" bug ate ~10 iterations.** Root cause: Canvas anti-aliasing on non-integer DPR displays (Windows scaling 1.25/1.5). With the usual `ctx.scale(dpr)` setup, integer CSS coords land on FRACTIONAL device pixels — adjacent tiles' fillRect/stroke edges had AA-mismatched pixels at every shared boundary, showing as faint cross-channel lines. **Fix:** compute everything in device pixels (`canvas.width = grid + 2*pad` directly, `ctx.setTransform(1,0,0,1,0,0)` — no DPR scaling), force `cellSize` even, `cellAt` converts incoming CSS pointer coords to device pixels. Earlier attempts (per-cell gridline skip, channel extension by bevel-thickness, removing L/R bevel contrast, fillRect-instead-of-stroke for straights) ALL FAILED because they fought the symptom on the CSS side; only device-pixel rendering eliminates it.
- **Game now framed as a "circuit"** — entry/exit notches connect via an external connector around the grid perimeter, drawn as ONE polyline through `drawCircuitOutline` so every corner is a smooth `lineJoin='round'` bend (separate strokes had visible butt-cap seams). Lit palette is `LIT_GREEN` while in progress, flips to `LIT_GOLD` the moment `Maze.won` becomes true (single `litPalette()` drives every lit element). User mused about renaming the game to something circuit-related but didn't commit.
- **Twin locking** added — `TWIN_PAIR_COUNT` tile pairs (one path-tile + one filler each) rotate together via `_twin = { partner, color }` tags. Cross tiles deliberately excluded (4-rotation-symmetric → coupling would be invisible). Per-pair pastel palette derived programmatically from a base hex via `paletteFromBase` (additive lighten for top, multiplicative darken for bot/right). Hint-locked twins lock their partner too (with the same rotation delta) so they can't desync.
- **Cheat → Hint** rename (i18n key, button id, JS function). Hint-locked path tiles now also light their solution lane gold *before* the connecting chain reaches them — red bevel = locked, gold groove = confirmed-correct.
- **Difficulty thresholds scale with SIZE** in `maze.js`: `MIN_PATH_LENGTH = round(SIZE * 2.25)`, `MIN_PUZZLE_LENGTH = round(SIZE * 1.75)`, `MIN_SHORTCUT_TWISTS = round(SIZE * 1.25)`, `MAX_DFS_STEPS = SIZE² * 125`, `TWIN_PAIR_COUNT = max(1, SIZE−5)`. Retry counts (`MAX_PATH_GEN_TRIES`, `MAX_REROLL_ATTEMPTS`, etc.) stay constant — they tune search algorithms, not difficulty.
- **`MIN_PUZZLE_LENGTH` is on BFS shortest-*possible* path** (treats rotation as free, since the player controls it), not on the intended DFS path. Without this, a puzzle with a long intended path but a short alternative shortcut still felt trivial — the player completes the shortest path, not the DFS one. Endpoint picker now takes farthest-of-`ENDPOINT_SAMPLES=5` random configs so the BFS floor is reachable on attempt 1.
- **Right-click rotates CCW**; touch and left-click rotate CW. `contextmenu` handler suppresses the browser menu only on the canvas.
- **Path channel `pathChannel` extends endpoints by 0 px now** — earlier attempts to extend by 1 or by `b` (bevel thickness) caused visible "tabs" sticking out of cells at terminating channels. The DPR fix made those workarounds unnecessary.

## 2026-04-25 — First playable: 12×12 maze with self-crossing path support
- Game concept (per the user): random grid of straight/elbow/cross tiles, all randomly rotated. Player rotates tiles to connect a continuous path from entry port to exit port on the perimeter. Lit gold = currently connected to entry. Win when the lit path reaches the exit.
- **Cross tiles must support self-crossing paths.** A cross has two non-touching lanes (N–S + E–W); the same solution path can use BOTH lanes (enters via N, exits S, wanders, comes back, enters E, exits W). My initial build forbade this (single-visit DFS, crosses only as filler decoys); user corrected — generator now uses port-walking DFS that lets a cell be revisited iff: (a) first lane was opposite-pair, (b) second lane uses the perpendicular opposite pair (the cross's other lane). Elbow first lanes can never get a second lane.
- Generator is permissive about crosses but doesn't *bias* toward them — many puzzles will have no path-crossings. If the player wants more crosses, add a bias (e.g. preferring exit ports that re-enter visited cells).
- `_solution` field stashed on path tiles for debugging — peek with `Maze.grid[r][c]._solution` in the console. Cross tiles record `_solution: 0` since rotation doesn't affect their connectivity.
- Controls: click/tap rotates a tile 90° CW. `N` key or click on the win banner generates a new puzzle.
- **PWA gotcha:** `sw.js` won't register from `file://`. Preview panel and `python -m http.server` both work fine.
- Open / next: levels & scoring (user said "later"), tile rotation animation (currently snaps), optional cross-bias for harder puzzles, multi-path levels (which would change generation entirely).

## 2026-04-25 — Added PROJECT_SLUG (universal rule 1 update)
- Universal rule 1 now defines two constants per project: `PROJECT_NAME` (display) and `PROJECT_SLUG` (lowercase ASCII identifier). Done because the rename earlier today exposed how many slug-shaped strings sit outside `PROJECT_NAME`'s reach.
- `config.js` now declares both. `sw.js` pulls the slug in via `importScripts('/config.js')` (worker context can't read page globals). `i18n.js` derives `LANG_STORAGE_KEY` from the slug. `index.html` builds its sessionStorage refresh key from the slug.
- **Worker-safety constraint:** `config.js` must stay free of DOM/window references at module top level so `importScripts()` doesn't blow up. Currently safe (only constants + `Logger`). If anything DOM-shaped gets added to `config.js`, gate it behind `typeof window !== 'undefined'`.

## 2026-04-25 — Renamed twixt → twixted
- The original name "Twixt" collides with the **Twixt** strategy board game (1962, Alex Randolph; trademark likely held by Hasbro via the Avalon Hill acquisition). Even though video games (USPTO Class 9/41) and board games (Class 28) sit in different trademark classes, the *likelihood-of-confusion* test could go either way, and SEO would be a permanent fight against the existing game. Rebranding now is cheap insurance.
- Renamed everywhere: folder (`twixt-front-end` → `twixted-front-end`), `PROJECT_NAME` constant, manifest name/short_name/description, page `<title>` and `<h1>`, SW `CACHE_NAME` slug (`twixt-v` → `twixted-v`), `localStorage` key (`twixt_lang`), `sessionStorage` key (`twixt_sw_refreshed`), and the placeholder API URL in `config.js`.
- Project will be a **video game** of some other kind — explicitly NOT a board game (which makes the trademark concern less acute, but renaming was still the right call).

## 2026-04-25 — Project scaffolded (as `twixt`)
- Created via `/create twixt`. Versions at `0.01`. No favicon yet.
- Main JS named `game.js` because the project is intended to be a game.
- Three-input modules (`gamepad.js`, `touch-controls.js`, `controls-config.js`) are skeletons only — APIs declared, bodies stubbed. `DEFAULT_KEYBOARD` / `DEFAULT_GAMEPAD` need filling in once gameplay actions are designed.
- Next: TBD — first feature.

## Process insight worth keeping
- Universal rule 1 promises that renaming "should require touching only `PROJECT_NAME`," but in practice the lowercase project slug threads through several places that aren't covered by that constant: folder names, `CACHE_NAME` in `sw.js`, `localStorage`/`sessionStorage` namespacing keys, the manifest, and any deploy URLs. If we rename a project later, expect to touch all of those by hand. (Could be solved cleanly by adding a `PROJECT_SLUG` constant alongside `PROJECT_NAME` — worth doing as a universal change someday, not now.)
